\renewcommand{\accession}{VIII}
\title{\textbf{\Huge ZSA Guide}\\[6pt]\large Zcash Shielded Assets: asset identity, issuance, transfer, and burn}
\author{m@rek.onl}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This volume of \emph{The Zcash Arboretum} describes the OrchardZSA protocol
of ZIP 226 and ZIP 227, both with status Draft, as a delta on the Orchard
protocol that the \emph{Ironwood Guide} constructs. The protocol extends the
Orchard protocol by the issuance, transfer and burn of Custom Assets. The
volume constructs, in dependency order, the identity of an Asset; the note,
its commitment and its value commitment, extended by the Asset Base; the
Action statement and the Action Group; burn and the binding validating key;
issuance and the global issuance state; and the status of the transaction
carrier. It proves no counterfeiting across Assets, supply integrity,
issuance authority and privacy of the Asset type under named assumptions.
NU7, specified by ZIP 259 (Draft), introduces no transaction format and
includes neither ZIP, so no network upgrade includes the protocol; its
transaction format survives only in withdrawn text, and every construction
is therefore classified as specified, designed but unspecified, or an open
problem. The volume assumes the \emph{Math Guide}; the \emph{Crypto Guide}
for signatures (\S``Digital signatures''), Pedersen commitments
(\S``Commitment schemes'', \S``The Pedersen commitment'') and random oracles
(\S``Hash functions and the random oracle model''); the \emph{Halo 2 Guide}
for the proof system; and the \emph{Ironwood Guide} for the Orchard
protocol. It is non-normative: the Zcash Protocol Specification, cited as
the protocol specification with the title of a section, and the Zcash
Improvement Proposals are authoritative.
\end{abstract}
\clearpage
\tableofcontents
\clearpage

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}

This section fixes the subject of the volume, its status and its vocabulary
before any construction: the sources and their status, and the notation
(\S\ref{sec:intro-scope}); the three epistemic classes, the status of a
result proved here and the carrier of a construction
(\S\ref{sec:intro-classes}); the Assets, the OrchardZSA protocol and the
premise on the pool that carries its notes (\S\ref{sec:intro-protocol});
and the requirements that a protocol for shielded assets must meet, each
paired with the result of this volume that discharges it
(\S\ref{sec:intro-req}).

\subsection{Scope and status}\label{sec:intro-scope}

This volume of \emph{The Zcash Arboretum} describes the OrchardZSA protocol,
defined in \S\ref{sec:intro-protocol}, as a delta on the Orchard protocol
that the \emph{Ironwood Guide} constructs. Every construction of the volume
modifies an object of that volume, or adds an object beside it, and the
objects of the Orchard protocol are cited by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
titles, never re-derived: \S``Notes and note commitments'', with Definition
``Note'' and Definition ``Note commitment''; \S``The note seed: Ironwood
derivations (lead byte $\mathtt{0x03}$)''; \S``Actions, bundles, and dummy
notes'', with Definition ``Dummy notes''; \S``Value commitments and the
binding signature'', with \S``Value commitments'' (Definition ``Net value
commitment''), \S``The balancing value'' and \S``The binding signature'';
\S``The Action statement and its proof'', with \S``The Action statement''
(Definition ``Orchard Action statement''); \S``Nullifiers''; \S``Sinsemilla:
hash, commitment, and short forms''; \S``GroupHash, domain separation, and
nothing-up-my-sleeve generators''; \S``The Orchard protocol and its two
pools''; \S``The transaction format''; \S``Value conservation'', with
Theorem ``Balance''; \S``Fields, groups, and encodings''; and
\S``Requirements on a shielded payment''. The volume further assumes the
\emph{Math Guide}; the \emph{Crypto Guide} for digital signatures
(\S``Digital signatures''), Pedersen commitments (\S``Commitment schemes'',
\S``The Pedersen commitment'') and random oracles (\S``Hash functions and
the random oracle model''); and the \emph{Halo 2 Guide} for the proof
system. No other volume is cited. The volume is non-normative: the Zcash
Protocol Specification, cited as the protocol specification with the title
of a section, and the Zcash Improvement Proposals are authoritative.

\emph{Sources.} Two ZIPs specify the protocol. ZIP 227, ``Issuance of Zcash
Shielded Assets'', specifies the issuance of Custom Assets; ZIP 226,
``Transfer and Burn of Zcash Shielded Assets'', specifies their transfer and
burn. Both have status Draft and category Consensus (ZIP 226 and ZIP 227,
preambles). ZIP 226 is written relative to the protocol with ZIP 2005
applied (ZIP 226, Abstract), and calls ZIP 2005 ``Orchard Quantum
Recoverability''; the current title of ZIP 2005 is ``Ironwood Quantum
Recoverability'', and its status is Proposed (ZIP 2005, preamble). Every
construction of the OrchardZSA protocol is therefore fixed, where it is
fixed at all, by Draft text, and none belongs to the consensus rules of any
network upgrade. The class of each construction
(\S\ref{sec:intro-classes}) is stated once, where the construction is made.

\begin{definition}[ZIP statuses]\label{def:zip-status}
The status field of a ZIP takes one of the values that ZIP 0, ``ZIP Status
Field'', fixes; a ZIP with several revisions carries one status per
revision. This volume uses five of them.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Draft}: the status of every ZIP on submission. ZIP 226, ZIP
  227, ZIP 229, ZIP 258 and ZIP 259 have it.
\item \emph{Proposed}: typically the stage after Draft, set after
  consideration, feedback and rough consensus of the community. ZIP 2005 has
  it.
\item \emph{Active}: typically used for Process or Informational ZIPs, set
  once rough consensus on a Proposed ZIP is reached. Revision~$0$ of ZIP 317
  has it.
\item \emph{Final}: the status of a Consensus or Standards ZIP that is both
  implemented and activated on the Zcash network.
\item \emph{Withdrawn}: the owners of the ZIP have removed it from
  consideration by the community. ZIP 230 and ZIP 246 have it.
\end{itemize}
A ZIP text is \emph{live} when its status, or the status of the revision
concerned, is Draft, Proposed, Active or Final. Every published ZIP that
this volume cites is either live or Withdrawn. The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``ZIP'' names only the statuses Draft and Reserved; Reserved is not
used here.
\end{definition}

\emph{Status with respect to network upgrades.} NU6.3 is the active network
upgrade, deployed by ZIP 258, and NU7 is specified by ZIP 259 (Draft). The
sources of the consensus changes of NU7 are the protocol specification,
ZIPs 200, 218 and 2003, and ZIP 237, which ZIP 259 names but which is
unpublished; none of these is ZIP 226 or ZIP 227; NU7
introduces no new transaction format, and version-$5$ and version-$6$
transactions remain valid (ZIP 259, Abstract and Specification, ``NU7
consensus changes''). ZIP 258 does not mention Zcash Shielded Assets (ZIP
258, Specification). ZIP 226, ``Deployment'', still names NU7 as the network
upgrade that deploys the protocol, which ZIP 259 contradicts, and ZIP 227,
``Deployment'', reads ``TBD''. The assignment of the OrchardZSA protocol to
a network upgrade is therefore an open problem in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:classes}, stated in \S\ref{sec:intro-classes}.

\emph{Notation.} The notation is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, whose
table in \S``Fields, groups, and encodings'' maps it to the names of the
protocol specification. Table~\ref{tab:notation} extends that map to the
names that ZIP 226 and ZIP 227 use and that the table of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide} does not list. The names of the ZIPs appear in that
table only. The table lists no symbol that this volume defines: every such
symbol, among them the Asset Base, the Asset Identifier and Asset Digest,
the OrchardZSA note and value commitments, the split flag, the pause flag,
the issuance keys, the global issuance state and the burn set, is defined at
its first use. The same convention applies to the generator
$L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ of Construction~\ref{def:split-nf}, which ZIP 226
writes calligraphically.

Prose uses British spelling, as in finalisation and authorisation.
Identifiers and titles taken from the ZIPs keep their own spelling, as in
the flag $\mathsf{finalize}$ and the title ``Issuance Authorization
Signature Scheme'' of ZIP 227.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Epistemic classes}\label{sec:intro-classes}

\begin{definition}[Three epistemic classes]\label{def:classes}
Each construction of this volume is in exactly one of three classes.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item It is \emph{specified} when normative text of the protocol
  specification or of a live ZIP (Definition~\ref{def:zip-status}), Draft
  included, fixes it.
\item It is \emph{designed but unspecified} when no live normative text and
  no published proof fixes it, but a complete design of it exists, in
  withdrawn ZIP text, in text removed from a ZIP, in a document outside the
  ZIPs, or as an argument of a ZIP's rationale without proof, and that
  design conflicts with no live text.
\item It is an \emph{open problem} when the protocol needs it and either no
  text, live, withdrawn or removed, supplies a design of it; or every
  existing design conflicts with live text, in that a value or position that
  the design assigns is assigned otherwise by live text; or live texts
  contradict one another about it.
\end{enumerate}
The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Epistemic classes'' uses the first
two names for claims about the deployed protocol; here they are redefined
for constructions, and the third is added.
\end{definition}

Each construction is classified where it is made. Among the designed but
unspecified constructions are the OrchardZSA circuit
(\S\ref{sec:action-proof}), the derivation of the commitment trapdoor of an
OrchardZSA note (\S\ref{sec:notes-seed}) and the fee terms
(\S\ref{sec:carrier-fees}). Among the open problems are the pool that
carries OrchardZSA notes (Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}); the live carrier,
whose withdrawn version~$6$ is now the format of ZIP 229, which need not
support Zcash Shielded Assets (ZIP 229, Non-requirements); the bit of the
pause flag $\mathsf{enableZSA}$, whose position in withdrawn ZIP 230, bit~$2$
of the flags byte, is that of $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$ in ZIP 229
(\S\ref{sec:carrier-format}); and the value of
the lead byte, whose removed value $\mathtt{0x03}$ is the Ironwood lead byte
(\S\ref{sec:notes-seed}).

\begin{definition}[Status of a result proved in this volume]
\label{def:result-status}
A theorem, proposition, lemma or corollary of this volume is proved here
for the constructions as the ZIPs state them, and carries no class: the
classes of Definition~\ref{def:classes} apply to constructions and to the
rationale arguments of the ZIPs. The hypotheses of a result are named in
its statement. A hypothesis that concerns a designed but unspecified object,
or an open problem (Definition~\ref{def:classes}(iii)), makes the result
\emph{conditional} on that object:
\begin{itemize}
\item on the OrchardZSA circuit, through Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} and
  Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-zk} of ``The OrchardZSA Action proof''
  (\S\ref{sec:action-proof});
\item on the signed message, through Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover} of
  ``Issuance authorisation'' (\S\ref{sec:issue-auth});
\item on an open problem, through a hypothesis that names it:
  Lemma~\ref{lem:first-issuance}, Proposition~\ref{prop:reference-copy}(a)
  and Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy}.
\end{itemize}
A result about notes created by issuance, such as the provenance of Asset
Bases or supply integrity, holds for every deterministic derivation of the
commitment trapdoor $\rcm$ from the published fields of such a note. It
depends on the designed but unspecified derivation of $\rcm$ only in that
some such derivation must be fixed for a validator to compute the
commitment. Where a ZIP argues the same property in its rationale only, that
argument remains designed but unspecified; a proof in this volume does not
change its class.
\end{definition}

Every construction states its class where it is made, and every result the
premises on which it is conditional. ``Classification of the components''
(\S\ref{sec:status}) tabulates both.

\begin{definition}[Carrier of a construction]\label{def:carrier}
The \emph{carrier} of a construction is the set of transaction fields that
encode it, together with the digests that commit to it: the transaction
identifier, the authorising-data digest and the signature digest, the three
digests of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and
signatures''. A construction can be specified while its carrier is not.
\end{definition}

\subsection{The OrchardZSA protocol}\label{sec:intro-protocol}

\begin{definition}[Asset, Custom Asset, Native Asset]\label{def:asset}
An \emph{Asset} is a type of note that can be transferred on the Zcash block
chain. Each Asset is identified by an Asset Identifier (ZIP 227, Terminology).
ZEC is the default, and currently the only defined, Asset on Mainnet, and TAZ
the default, and currently the only defined, Asset on Testnet; each is the
\emph{Native Asset} of its network. A \emph{Custom Asset} is an Asset other than
ZEC and TAZ (ZIP 227, Terminology; ZIP 226, ``Burn Mechanism''). The Asset
Identifier of a Custom Asset is constructed in ``Asset descriptions and Asset
Identifiers'' (\S\ref{sec:asset-id}); no ZIP constructs one for the Native
Asset, whose Asset Base is fixed directly (Definition~\ref{def:native-base}). In
this volume \emph{issuance} means the issuance of a Custom Asset, in the sense
of ZIP 227. It is distinct from the creation of ZEC by the block subsidy in a
coinbase transaction (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Coinbase
transaction''), which the OrchardZSA protocol does not modify.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA protocol]\label{def:orchardzsa}
The \emph{OrchardZSA protocol} is the extension of the Orchard protocol that
ZIP 226 and ZIP 227 define jointly: ZIP 227 specifies the issuance of Custom
Assets, and ZIP 226 their transfer and burn. ZIP 227 is to be implemented
only together with ZIP 226, because the notes that issuance creates can be
transferred only by the OrchardZSA transfer protocol (ZIP 227, Abstract,
where ``must'' is lower-case and is not a key word of BCP 14). Relative to
the Orchard protocol it makes the following changes, each constructed in the
section named with it.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item Each note gains one field, a point of the Pallas group that names its
  Asset (``Notes and note commitments'', \S\ref{sec:notes}).
\item The note commitment becomes a case split that leaves the commitments
  of ZEC notes unchanged (same section).
\item The net value commitment takes that point as its value base, in place
  of the fixed base $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (same section).
\item The binding validating key further subtracts the burnt amounts
  (``Burn and the binding signature'', \S\ref{sec:burn}).
\item An Action of a Custom Asset that consumes no note pads its consumed
  side with a copy of a note already committed, of the same Asset and
  counted at value~$0$, instead of a dummy note (``OrchardZSA Actions and
  Action Groups'', \S\ref{sec:action}).
\item The Action statement changes accordingly (same section).
\item ZIP 227 adds issuance and a global issuance state (``Issuance of
  Custom Assets'', \S\ref{sec:issue}; ``The global issuance state'',
  \S\ref{sec:state}).
\end{enumerate}
The sources of these changes are ZIP 227, Abstract, and ZIP 226, Abstract
and Specification, with ``Split Notes'', ``Value Balance Verification'' and
``Backward Compatibility''.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[The Orchard-pool premise]\label{rem:pool-premise}
ZIP 226 places OrchardZSA notes in the Orchard pool, which the Orchard and
the OrchardZSA protocols then share, and adds their note commitments to the
same note commitment tree (ZIP 226, ``Privacy Implications'', ``Backward
Compatibility'' and ``Rationale for Note Commitment''). It also assumes ZIP
2005 applied (ZIP 226, Abstract). ZIP 2005 applies its recoverable note
plaintext format, of lead byte $\mathtt{0x03}$, only to notes of the
Ironwood pool (ZIP 258, ``ZIP 2005 activation''), and note plaintexts of the
Orchard pool take the lead byte $\mathtt{0x02}$ only (protocol
specification, \S``Note Plaintexts and Memo Fields''). Since NU6.3 the
Orchard pool is sealed (ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules from NU6.3 activation'';
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Orchard protocol and its two pools''):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item a coinbase transaction has no Orchard-pool Actions;
\item every Orchard-pool Action creates its note at the address of the note
  it consumes, cross-address transfers being disabled;
\item the balancing value of the Orchard pool is non-negative.
\end{enumerate}
Read literally, these rules have consequences that no ZIP states. Rule~(b)
excludes, for a Custom Asset, every created note at an address other than
that of the note which the same Action consumes or copies; it thus
excludes a transfer of a Custom Asset to such an address, and every padding
copy at an address other than that of its Action's created note. Rule~(c)
is stated for ZEC only, and leaves unaddressed the entry of issued value
into a pool that admits no inflow. ZIP 226 and ZIP 227 have not been
revised for the pool rules of NU6.3 (ZIP 226 has only given up lead byte
$\mathtt{0x03}$), ZIP 258 does not mention them, and no ZIP places
OrchardZSA notes in the Ironwood pool: ZIP 2005 treats the extension as
undeployed (``if that were deployed'', Terminology) and requires it to take
a new lead byte (``Usage with other proposals requiring note plaintext
format changes'').

Class: which pool carries OrchardZSA notes is an open problem
(Definition~\ref{def:classes}). Live texts contradict one another about it:
ZIP 258 states that no new value may enter the Orchard pool, while ZIP 227
adds the commitments of issued notes to the note commitment tree (ZIP 227,
``Addition to the Note Commitment Tree'') that ZIP 226 places in that pool,
and no live text places the notes elsewhere. The later sections state each
construction as ZIP 226 and ZIP 227 state it, and recall this remark where a
consequence falls: at Split Inputs, at reference notes and at the order of
the note commitment tree.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Requirements on shielded assets}\label{sec:intro-req}

\begin{definition}[Requirements Z1 to Z5]\label{def:req-zsa}
A protocol for shielded assets meets the following requirements. Each
refines one of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Requirements R1 to
R5'', or is new (ZIP 227, Requirements; ZIP 226, ``Privacy Implications'',
``Rationale for Value Commitment'' and ``Backward Compatibility'').
\begin{enumerate}[label=Z\arabic*., leftmargin=2.4em]
\item \emph{Per-Asset conservation.} Requirement R4 holds Asset by Asset:
  in every valid bundle, for each Asset, the value consumed equals the value
  created plus the value that leaves the pool, burnt value counted as
  leaving.
\item \emph{No counterfeiting across Assets.} No transaction creates value
  of one Asset from value of another Asset, or from nothing; value of a
  Custom Asset enters only by issuance.
\item \emph{Supply integrity.} The amount of a Custom Asset in circulation
  equals the amount issued minus the amount burnt, can be tracked publicly,
  and stops growing once its issuer finalises the Asset.
\item \emph{Issuance authority.} Only the holder of an issuer's key issues
  or finalises that issuer's Assets.
\item \emph{Hiding of the Asset type.} A shielded transfer reveals which
  Assets it moves no more than burn and issuance publish. This refines the
  hiding parts of R1 and R4.
\end{enumerate}
Two design requirements are recorded with Z2. First, the identification of
an Asset should be unique among all shielded pools, and different issuer
keys should not produce the same Asset Identifier (ZIP 227, Requirements,
where ``should'' is lower-case). Second, the identity of an Asset maps to a
point of the Pallas group that serves as a private value base inside the
Action proof (ZIP 226, Overview, ``Rationale for Value Commitment'' and
``Value Commitment Correctness'').
\end{definition}

Table~\ref{tab:req-results} names, for each requirement, the result of this
volume that discharges it. The checks that serve each requirement are mapped
in Table~\ref{tab:verify-division} of ``Verification of an OrchardZSA
transaction'' (\S\ref{sec:state-verify}), where all of them are defined.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
Requirement & Result \\
\midrule
Z1, Z2 & Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}, ``No counterfeiting across
  Assets'' \\
Z3 & Theorem~\ref{thm:supply}, ``Supply integrity''; \\
  & Proposition~\ref{prop:final-supply}, ``Finalised supply never grows'' \\
Z4 & Theorem~\ref{thm:issue-authority}, ``Issuance authority'' \\
Z5 & Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy}, ``Privacy of the Asset type'' \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Requirements and the results that discharge them. Each result
states its assumptions and the premises on which it is conditional
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}).}
\label{tab:req-results}
\end{table}
\FloatBarrier

\section{Asset identity}\label{sec:asset}

This section constructs the identity of an Asset in dependency order: the
issuance key pair, the issuance authorisation signature scheme and the
issuer identifier (\S\ref{sec:asset-keys}); the asset description and the
Asset Identifier (\S\ref{sec:asset-id}); the Asset Digest, the Asset Base
and its byte encoding (\S\ref{sec:asset-base}); and the Asset Base of the
Native Asset (\S\ref{sec:asset-native}). Every construction of the section
is taken from ZIP 227 and ZIP 226, both Draft. The results of the section
separate Assets computationally: distinct pairs of issuer and description
yield distinct Asset Identifiers and distinct Asset Bases, and no Asset Base
of a Custom Asset coincides with a fixed generator of the Orchard protocol,
except with negligible probability.

Throughout, $\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[k]}$ denotes the set of byte strings of
length $k$ and $\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[\mathbb{N}]}$ the set of byte strings
of arbitrary length; $\|$ denotes concatenation. For a $16$-byte
personalisation $P$ and a byte string $x$,
$\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}(P, x)$ and $\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(P, x)$
denote unkeyed BLAKE2b with personalisation $P$, input $x$ and an output of
$32$ and $64$ bytes respectively (protocol specification, \S``BLAKE2 Hash
Functions''). The former is the function $\mathsf{h}$ of the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and signatures''.

\subsection{Issuance keys and the issuer identifier}\label{sec:asset-keys}

\begin{definition}[Issuance key pair]\label{def:issuance-keys}
The \emph{issuance authorising key} is a byte string
$\mathsf{isk} \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}$. The \emph{issuance
validating key} is $\mathsf{ik} := \mathsf{DerivePublic}(\mathsf{isk})$,
with $\mathsf{DerivePublic}$ the algorithm of
Definition~\ref{def:issueauthsig}; it lies in
$\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]} \cup \{\bot\}$, and a key pair is usable only
when $\mathsf{ik} \neq \bot$. The key $\mathsf{isk}$ authorises the issuance
of Asset Identifiers by one issuer and is used only by that issuer; the key
$\mathsf{ik}$ validates those issuances. Neither key is used for spend
authorisation, which remains with the Orchard spend authorising key (ZIP
227, ``Issuance Keys'' and ``Rationale''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, identity.py:
% [sec:asset-keys] ik = BIP-340 PubKey(isk): 32 bytes; issuer = ik_encoding
%   = [0x00] || ik = 1 + 32 = 33 bytes
% [sec:asset-keys] issueAuthSig = [0x00] || sigma = 1 + 64 = 65 bytes
% Cited: 32-byte ik and isk; 64-byte signature: ZIP 227 'Orchard ZSA Issuance
%   Authorization Signature Scheme'
\begin{definition}[Issuance authorisation signature scheme]
\label{def:issueauthsig}
The \emph{issuance authorisation signature scheme} $\mathsf{IssueAuthSig}$
is a digital signature scheme in the sense of the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s
Definition ``Digital signature scheme'' (protocol specification,
\S``Signature''), instantiated as BIP-340 Schnorr signatures over secp256k1
with the standard secp256k1 parameters (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The
transparent layer's schemes: ECDSA and BIP-340 over secp256k1'',
Construction ``BIP-340 Schnorr over secp256k1, schematically''; cited, not
re-derived). Let $n$ be the order of the secp256k1 group. The types are
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{Message} &= \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[\mathbb{N}]}, &
  \mathsf{Signature} &= \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[64]} \cup \{\bot\}, \\
  \mathsf{Public} &= \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]} \cup \{\bot\}, &
  \mathsf{Private} &= \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]},
\end{align*}
a public key being the $32$-byte big-endian $x$-coordinate of BIP 340. The
algorithms are the following.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The algorithm $\mathsf{DerivePublic}(\mathsf{isk})$ returns the BIP-340
  public key of $\mathsf{isk}$, or $\bot$ exactly when BIP-340 key
  derivation fails, that is, when $\mathsf{isk}$ read as a big-endian integer
  is $0$ or at least $n$.
\item The algorithm $\mathsf{Sign}(\mathsf{isk}, M)$ runs BIP-340 signing on
  $M$ under $\mathsf{isk}$ with auxiliary data $a := [\mathtt{0x00}]^{32}$
  and returns its output $\sigma \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[64]}$, or $\bot$
  if it fails. With $a$ fixed, signing is a deterministic function of
  $(\mathsf{isk}, M)$.
\item The algorithm $\mathsf{Validate}(\mathsf{ik}, M, \sigma)$ returns $0$
  if $\sigma = \bot$; otherwise it returns $1$ if BIP-340 verification of
  $\sigma$ on $M$ under $\mathsf{ik}$ succeeds, and $0$ if not.
\end{enumerate}
ZIP 227 writes the verification key in $\mathsf{Validate}$ as
$\mathsf{key}$, although the parameter is named $\mathsf{ik}$; the reading
$\mathsf{ik}$ is the only one that type-checks. Batch verification MAY be
used; precomputation MAY be used if and only if it produces equivalent
results (ZIP 227, ``Issuance Authorization Signature Scheme'', ``Orchard ZSA
Issuance Authorization Signature Scheme'', ``Derivation of issuance
validating key'' and ``Issuance Authorization Signing and Validation'').
Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\begin{assumption}[Unforgeability of the issuance signature]
\label{ass:issueauthsig-euf}
The scheme $\mathsf{IssueAuthSig}$, with key generation that samples
$\mathsf{isk}$ uniformly from $\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}$ conditioned on
$\mathsf{DerivePublic}(\mathsf{isk}) \neq \bot$, and with
$H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/challenge}}$ and $H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/nonce}}$ modelled
as independent random oracles, is existentially unforgeable under chosen-message
attack (\emph{Crypto Guide}, Definition
``Existential unforgeability under chosen-message attack'').
\end{assumption}

The assumption is justified, not proved. The \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Theorem
``EUF-CMA security of Schnorr signatures in the ROM'' (\S``Security in the
random oracle model and the forking lemma'') proves the property for Schnorr
signatures under the discrete-logarithm assumption, and that volume's remark
following Construction ``BIP-340 Schnorr over secp256k1, schematically''
carries the proof to BIP-340 with the tagged challenge hash
$H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/challenge}}$ modelled as a random oracle. With $a$ fixed,
the nonce is $H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/nonce}}(t \,\|\, \mathsf{ik} \,\|\, M)$ for
the secret $t := d \oplus H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/aux}}(a)$, where $d$ is the
BIP-340 secret scalar of $\mathsf{isk}$, fixed per key. With
$H_{\mathtt{BIP0340/nonce}}$ also modelled as a random oracle, independent
of the challenge oracle, the nonce is a fresh uniform value per message to
any party ignorant of $t$, and the proof carries over as for deterministic
nonces (\emph{Crypto Guide}, Construction ``EdDSA, schematically'').

\begin{definition}[Issuer identifier and signature encodings]
\label{def:issuer}
For $\mathsf{ik} \neq \bot$, the \emph{validating-key encoding} is
$\mathsf{ik\_encoding} := [\mathtt{0x00}] \,\|\, \mathsf{ik}$, of $33$ bytes,
and the \emph{issuer identifier} is
$\mathsf{issuer} := \mathsf{ik\_encoding}$. For $\sigma \neq \bot$, the
\emph{signature encoding} is
$\mathsf{issueAuthSig} := [\mathtt{0x00}] \,\|\, \sigma$, of $65$ bytes. In
both encodings the initial byte names the signature scheme and MUST be
$\mathtt{0x00}$, which denotes BIP 340 (ZIP 227, ``Derivation of issuance
validating key'', ``Issuance Authorization Signing and Validation''). The
map $\mathsf{ik} \mapsto \mathsf{issuer}$ is injective. Note owners and
validators use the identifier to associate an Asset with its issuer; ZIP
227, ``Issuer Identifier'', records that the equality
$\mathsf{issuer} = \mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ may cease to hold once key
rotation is specified. Class: specified; its carrier is not.
\end{definition}

The encoding $\mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ occurs only in the $\mathsf{issuer}$
field of the Issuance Bundle of ``Issuance Actions and Issuance Bundles''
(\S\ref{sec:issue-bundle}). No transaction format carrying that field is
specified.

\subsection{Asset descriptions and Asset Identifiers}\label{sec:asset-id}

\begin{definition}[Asset description]\label{def:asset-desc}
An \emph{asset description} $\mathsf{asset\_desc}$ is a byte string chosen
by the issuer, carrying any information about the issuance. ZIP 227
requires it to be non-empty and states that it SHOULD be a well-formed UTF-8
code-unit sequence according to Unicode 15.0.0 or later; no maximum length is
imposed (ZIP 227, ``ZIP 227 Asset Identifiers''). Only its hash
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ (Definition~\ref{def:assetid}) is visible to
consensus, so neither non-emptiness nor the UTF-8 condition can be enforced
by consensus: both are issuer-side conventions. Within the scope of one
issuer, distinct Assets are obtained from distinct descriptions. Class:
specified.
\end{definition}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, identity.py:
% [sec:asset-id] personalisations ZSA-AssetDescCRH and ZSA-Asset-Digest:
%   16 bytes each (the BLAKE2b personalisation length)
% [sec:asset-id] assetDescHash = BLAKE2b-256(ZSA-AssetDescCRH, asset_desc):
%   32 bytes
% [sec:asset-id] EncodeAssetId = [0x00] || issuer || assetDescHash
%   = 1 + 33 + 32 = 66 bytes (fixed lengths, so the encoding is injective)
\begin{definition}[Asset Identifier and its encoding]\label{def:assetid}
For an issuer identifier $\mathsf{issuer} \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[33]}$
and an asset description $\mathsf{asset\_desc}$, let
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{assetDescHash} &:= \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}(
    \texttt{ZSA-AssetDescCRH}, \mathsf{asset\_desc})
    \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}, \\
  \mathsf{AssetId} &:= (\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash}), \\
  \mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId}) &:= [\mathtt{0x00}] \,\|\,
    \mathsf{issuer} \,\|\, \mathsf{assetDescHash}
    \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[66]}.
\end{align*}
The pair $\mathsf{AssetId}$ is the \emph{Asset Identifier} and
$\mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId})$ its canonical encoding (ZIP 227,
``ZIP 227 Asset Identifiers''). The leading byte of the encoding is a
version byte, reserving room for other issuance protocols; it is distinct in
role from the scheme byte that begins $\mathsf{issuer}$, so every current
encoding begins with two $\mathtt{0x00}$ bytes of different meaning. The
encoding is injective, since its three components have the fixed lengths
$1$, $33$ and $32$ bytes. Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Uniqueness of Asset Identifiers]\label{prop:assetid-unique}
Consider two distinct pairs of issuer identifier and asset description,
$(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{asset\_desc})$ and
$(\mathsf{issuer}', \mathsf{asset\_desc}')$, with Asset Identifiers
$\mathsf{AssetId}$ and $\mathsf{AssetId}'$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $\mathsf{issuer} \neq \mathsf{issuer}'$, then
  $\mathsf{AssetId} \neq \mathsf{AssetId}'$.
\item If $\mathsf{issuer} = \mathsf{issuer}'$, then
  $\mathsf{asset\_desc} \neq \mathsf{asset\_desc}'$, and
  $\mathsf{AssetId} = \mathsf{AssetId}'$ holds exactly when the two
  descriptions collide under
  $\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}(\texttt{ZSA-AssetDescCRH}, \cdot)$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence, under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance
of BLAKE2b'', no efficient algorithm outputs two distinct pairs of issuer
and description with equal Asset Identifiers, or with equal encodings
$\mathsf{EncodeAssetId}$, except with negligible probability. Status: proved
here for the specified construction; conditional on no unspecified object.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The issuer identifier is a component of $\mathsf{AssetId}$, so distinct
issuers give distinct pairs, which is claim~1. For one issuer the
descriptions differ, and equal identifiers mean equal values
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ on distinct descriptions, a BLAKE2b-256 collision
under one personalisation; this is claim~2. An algorithm that outputs such
a collision outputs two distinct inputs $(P, x) \neq (P, x')$ of
$\mathsf{h}$ with equal values, which the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption
``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b'' (\S``Transaction digests and
signatures'') excludes except with negligible probability. Equal encodings
imply equal identifiers by the injectivity recorded in
Definition~\ref{def:assetid}.
\end{proof}

The requirement of ZIP 226, ``Asset Identifiers'', that every new Asset have
a new and unique Asset Identifier is met by the choice of a new description.
ZIP 227, ``Rationale'', argues the separation of issuers informally; that
argument is a rationale of the ZIP, whose class is designed but unspecified,
and Proposition~\ref{prop:assetid-unique} supplies its proof. Since the
proposition separates Assets only through the issuer and any issuer may
reuse any description, ZIP 227, ``ZIP 227 Asset Identifiers'', requires
that wallets MUST NOT display $\mathsf{asset\_desc}$ alone as the name of an
Asset (see also ZIP 227, ``Displaying Asset Identifier information to
users'').

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Asset Digests and Asset Bases}\label{sec:asset-base}

OrchardZSA adds three inputs of $\GroupHash$ to those of the Orchard
protocol, which the \emph{Ironwood Guide} lists in its table of
\S``GroupHash, domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve generators''.
Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa} lists them as strings, each with the
subsection that uses it (ZIP 227, ``OrchardZSA Asset Bases''; ZIP 226,
``Split Notes'' and ``Note Structure and Commitment''). The objects they
define are constructed at their uses.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
Domain separator $D$ & Message $M$ & Use & Subsection \\
\midrule
\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA} & $64$-byte Asset Digest
  & Asset Base of a Custom Asset & \S\ref{sec:asset-base} \\
\texttt{z.cash:Orchard} & \texttt{L}
  & additional term of a split nullifier & \S\ref{sec:action-nf} \\
\texttt{z.cash:SinsemillaQ} & \texttt{z.cash:ZSA-NoteCommit-M}
  & hash base, Custom Asset note commitment & \S\ref{sec:notes-cm} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Group-hash inputs of OrchardZSA, in addition to those of the
Orchard protocol. The subsections are ``Asset Digests and Asset Bases'',
``Nullifiers of OrchardZSA notes'' and ``Note commitments''. The commitment
to a Custom Asset note reuses the blinding input
(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-NoteCommit-r}, $\varepsilon$) and the Sinsemilla
table entries of the Orchard protocol, so they add no row. The table lists
input strings only.}
\label{tab:grouphash-zsa}
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Distinctness of the OrchardZSA group-hash inputs]
\label{lem:grouphash-distinct}
The input pairs of Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa} are pairwise distinct and
distinct from every input pair of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s table of
\S``GroupHash, domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve generators''.
Hence, under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``GroupHash as a random
oracle'', the values of $\GroupHash$ at all these pairs are independent
uniform points of $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$, and, under that assumption and
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'',
that volume's Lemma ``Relations among fixed generators'' applies to any
polynomial collection of them together with the Orchard generators.
Status: proved here; conditional on no unspecified object.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The separator \texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA} occurs in no row of the Orchard
table. Under \texttt{z.cash:Orchard} the Orchard table has only the messages
\texttt{G} and \texttt{K}, both distinct from \texttt{L}. Under
\texttt{z.cash:SinsemillaQ} it has only the messages
\texttt{z.cash:\allowbreak Orchard-NoteCommit-M},
\texttt{z.cash:\allowbreak Orchard-CommitIvk-M} and
\texttt{z.cash:\allowbreak Orchard-MerkleCRH}, all distinct from
\texttt{z.cash:\allowbreak ZSA-NoteCommit-M}. The three new pairs have pairwise
distinct separators or messages. The map from $(D, M)$ to the input hashed
by $\GroupHash$ is injective, as the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``Domain
separation'' records, so distinct pairs are distinct oracle inputs; the
random-oracle assumption then gives independent uniform values, and the
cited lemma applies verbatim, its hypothesis being only that the inputs are
distinct.
\end{proof}

The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b''
concerns the $32$-byte output only. The Asset Digest is a $64$-byte output,
hence a separate assumption.

\begin{assumption}[Collision resistance of BLAKE2b-512]
\label{ass:blake2b512-cr}
No efficient algorithm outputs, except with negligible probability, two
inputs $x \neq x'$ with
$\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(P, x) = \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(P, x')$
under one $16$-byte personalisation $P$, in the sense of the \emph{Crypto
Guide}'s Definition ``Collision resistance'' (\S``Security notions:
preimage, second-preimage, and collision resistance'').
\end{assumption}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, identity.py:
% [sec:asset-base] AssetDigest = BLAKE2b-512(ZSA-Asset-Digest,
%   EncodeAssetId): 64 bytes = 512 bits
% Cited: personalisation ZSA-Asset-Digest, 512-bit digest: ZIP 227 'Asset
%   Digests'; 16-byte personalisation length: spec 'BLAKE2 Hash Functions'
\begin{construction}[Asset Digest and Asset Base]\label{def:assetbase}
For the Asset Identifier $\mathsf{AssetId}$ of a Custom Asset, the
\emph{Asset Digest} is
\[
  \mathsf{AssetDigest}_{\mathsf{AssetId}} := \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(
    \texttt{ZSA-Asset-Digest}, \mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId}))
  \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[64]}.
\]
For $D \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[64]}$ let
\[
  \mathsf{ZSAValueBase}(D) := \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, D)
  \in \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}),
\]
with $\GroupHash$ the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``GroupHash on
Pallas'' (protocol specification, \S``Group Hash into Pallas and Vesta'');
no cofactor is cleared. The separator gives a string $\mathsf{DST}$ within
the $255$-byte bound of that construction. The \emph{Asset Base} of the
Asset is
\[
  \mathsf{AssetBase}_{\mathsf{AssetId}} :=
    \mathsf{ZSAValueBase}(\mathsf{AssetDigest}_{\mathsf{AssetId}})
\]
(ZIP 227, ``Asset Digests'', ``Asset Bases'' and ``OrchardZSA Asset
Bases''). The subscript is dropped when the identifier is clear. The whole
chain, from issuer and description to Asset Base, is
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{asset\_desc}
  &\xrightarrow{\ \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}(\texttt{ZSA-AssetDescCRH},
    \cdot)\ } \mathsf{assetDescHash}, \\
  (\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash}) = \mathsf{AssetId}
  &\xrightarrow{\ \mathsf{EncodeAssetId}\ }
  \mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId}), \\
  \mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId})
  &\xrightarrow{\ \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(\texttt{ZSA-Asset-Digest},
    \cdot)\ } \mathsf{AssetDigest}, \\
  \mathsf{AssetDigest}
  &\xrightarrow{\ \mathsf{ZSAValueBase}\ } \mathsf{AssetBase}.
\end{align*}
Class: specified.
\end{construction}

The Asset Identifier is global across the shielded protocols that support
Zcash Shielded Assets; the Asset Base is per protocol, the map
$\mathsf{ZSAValueBase}$ being instantiated per protocol, here for OrchardZSA
(ZIP 227, ``Asset Identifier'', ``Asset Bases'').

Let $\Ep^{*} := \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \setminus \{\mathcal{O}\}$. ZIP 226,
``Note Structure and Commitment'', types the Asset Base of a note in
$\Ep^{*}$, as ``a valid group element that is not the identity''; ZIP 227
types the Asset Base of an Issue Note (``Issue Note'') and the domain of the
issuance state (``Global Issuance State'') likewise. The codomain of
$\GroupHash$, by contrast, is the whole group.

\begin{lemma}[Non-identity of derived Asset Bases]\label{lem:assetbase-nonzero}
Under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``GroupHash as a random
oracle'', for every $D \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[64]}$,
\[
  \Pr[\mathsf{ZSAValueBase}(D) = \mathcal{O}] = 1/p_{\Vesta},
\]
and an algorithm that makes $q$ queries to the oracle finds an Asset
Identifier whose Asset Base is $\mathcal{O}$ with probability at most
$q/p_{\Vesta}$, the inputs of its output being counted among the queries.
Status: proved here; conditional on no unspecified object.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the model each value is uniform on the Pallas group, of order
$p_{\Vesta}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Fields, groups, and encodings''),
so it is $\mathcal{O}$ with probability $1/p_{\Vesta}$. A union bound over
the $q$ queries gives the second claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Identity output of the Asset Base derivation]
\label{rem:assetbase-identity}
The map $\mathsf{ZSAValueBase}$ has no case for the identity, and ZIP 227
states no rule for an issuance whose derived Asset Base is $\mathcal{O}$.
The typing in $\Ep^{*}$ of the Asset Base of an Issue Note and of the domain
of the issuance state makes such an issuance unconstructible, hence invalid.
Class: specified; the rejection follows from the normative typing in
$\Ep^{*}$, and ZIP 227 states no separate rule. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:assetbase-nonzero} the event has negligible probability.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Separation of Asset Bases]\label{prop:assetbase-separation}
Let the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``GroupHash as a random oracle''
hold, and consider an algorithm that makes $q$ queries to $\GroupHash$, the
inputs of its output being counted among the queries.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The map $\mathsf{AssetId} \mapsto \mathsf{AssetBase}_{\mathsf{AssetId}}$
  is not injective; ``unique'' in ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
  Commitment'', means only that it is deterministic.
\item If the algorithm outputs $\mathsf{AssetId} \neq \mathsf{AssetId}'$
  with equal Asset Bases, then either it has found a collision of
  \[
    \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(\texttt{ZSA-Asset-Digest}, \cdot)
  \]
  on distinct encodings, or it is in an event of probability at most
  $q^2/(2p_{\Vesta})$.
\item It outputs an Asset Identifier whose Asset Base equals one of $t$
  fixed generators, among them $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
  $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and $K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, each the value of
  $\GroupHash$ at an input of the Orchard table or of
  Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa} other than the first row, with probability
  at most $qt/p_{\Vesta}$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence, under Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr}, the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumptions ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b'' and ``GroupHash as
a random oracle'', and by Proposition~\ref{prop:assetid-unique}, distinct
pairs $(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{asset\_desc})$ yield distinct Asset Bases,
and no Asset Base of a Custom Asset is an Orchard generator, except with
negligible probability. Status: proved here for the specified chain;
conditional on no unspecified object.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Claim~1. The map is defined on all pairs in
$\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[33]} \times \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}$, of which
there are more than $2^{256}$, and takes values in a group of prime order
$p_{\Vesta} < 2^{255}$; it is therefore not injective. It is a composition
of deterministic maps.

Claim~2. By the injectivity of $\mathsf{EncodeAssetId}$
(Definition~\ref{def:assetid}), distinct identifiers have distinct
encodings; equal digests of distinct encodings are a BLAKE2b-512 collision
under \texttt{ZSA-Asset-Digest}. Otherwise the digests are distinct, hence
distinct oracle inputs under \texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, whose values are
independent uniform points in the model. Among $q$ queried inputs two share
a value with probability at most
$\binom{q}{2}/p_{\Vesta} \le q^2/(2p_{\Vesta})$.

Claim~3. By Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct} every input under
\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA} differs from the inputs of the $t$ generators, so
each queried Asset Base is uniform and independent of them, and equals a
given one with probability $1/p_{\Vesta}$. A union bound over the $q$
queries and the $t$ generators gives $qt/p_{\Vesta}$.

The final sentence composes claims~2 and~3 with
Proposition~\ref{prop:assetid-unique}: distinct pairs of issuer and
description yield distinct identifiers except with negligible probability,
distinct identifiers yield distinct Asset Bases except with negligible
probability by claim~2 and Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr}, and the
bounds of claims~2 and~3 are negligible for polynomial $q$ and $t$.
\end{proof}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, identity.py:
% [sec:asset-base] asset_base = LEBS2OSP_256(repr_P(AssetBase)): 32 bytes
% Cited: 32-byte asset_base = LEBS2OSP_l_P(repr_P(AssetBase)): ZIP 226
%   'Note Structure and Commitment'; l_P = 256: spec 'Pallas and Vesta'
\begin{definition}[Byte encoding of an Asset Base]\label{def:assetbase-bytes}
The \emph{byte encoding} of an Asset Base is the $32$-byte string
\[
  \mathsf{asset\_base} := \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}(\mathsf{AssetBase}^{\star})
  \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]},
\]
with $\mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}$ and the star encoding those of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Fields, groups, and encodings'', Definitions
``Integer and byte encodings'' and ``Star encoding'' (ZIP 226, ``Note
Structure and Commitment''). It is typed as a byte string throughout,
although ZIP 226 also types it as a bit string in its clause on the note
plaintext. The encoding is canonical on the Pallas group, by the
canonicity of the star encoding established after the same Definition; ZIP
226 notes that canonicity may fail for future shielded protocols. Class:
specified; its carrier is not.
\end{definition}

The placements of $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ in a note plaintext and in the
entries of a burn set are encodings of ZIP 230, which is Withdrawn; they are
not specified.

%   | EncodeAssetId 66 | AssetDigest 64 | asset_base 32 (bytes)
% [sec:asset-keys] issueAuthSig = [0x00] || sigma = 1 + 64 = 65 bytes
\begin{table}[ht]

\begin{tabular}{@{}lrll@{}}
\toprule
Object & Bytes & Defined in & Source \\
\midrule
$\mathsf{ik}$ & $32$ & Definition~\ref{def:issueauthsig}
  & ZIP 227, ``Derivation of issuance validating key'' \\
$\mathsf{issuer} = \mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ & $33$
  & Definition~\ref{def:issuer} & ZIP 227, ``Issuer Identifier'' \\
$\mathsf{issueAuthSig}$ & $65$ & Definition~\ref{def:issuer}

$\mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{AssetId})$ & $66$
  & Definition~\ref{def:assetid} & ZIP 227, ``ZIP 227 Asset Identifiers'' \\
