\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Sizes along the identity chain, in bytes. ZIP 227 publishes no test
vectors, and no worked instance is given.}
\label{tab:identity-sizes}
\end{table}

Every formula of the chain is specified, in Draft ZIP text: the issuance
keys and $\mathsf{IssueAuthSig}$, the identifier $\mathsf{issuer}$, the hash
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$, the identifier $\mathsf{AssetId}$ and its encoding,
the Asset Digest, the Asset Base, its encoding $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ and the
Native Asset Base of \S\ref{sec:asset-native}. The identity case is
specified, by typing (Remark~\ref{rem:assetbase-identity}). The
encodings that would carry the chain on chain belong to the carrier.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{The Native Asset Base}\label{sec:asset-native}

\begin{definition}[Native Asset Base]\label{def:native-base}
The Native Asset, ZEC on Mainnet and TAZ on Testnet (ZIP 227,
``Terminology''), has no Asset Identifier under ZIP 227 and does not pass
through the chain of Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}. Its Asset Base is
fixed as
\[
  \mathsf{AssetBase} := V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    = \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv}, \texttt{v}),
\]
the value base of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Net value
commitment'' (\S``Value commitments''), where $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and its
companion $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv},
\texttt{r})$ are constructed; they are cited, not re-derived. ZIP 226,
``Asset Identifiers'' and ``Value Commitment'', writes the definition for
ZEC; its burn rules (``Burn Mechanism'', ``Additional Consensus Rules for
the assetBurn set'') identify the Native Asset, ZEC or TAZ, with the base
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. Class: specified.
\end{definition}

The value commitment and the note commitment of a Native Asset note are
computed by the Orchard formulas. Two things differ: the derivation of
$\rcm$ takes the OrchardZSA lead byte (Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}),
and the note plaintext carries $\mathsf{asset\_base}$
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-plaintext}; ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
Commitment''). The value commitment of ZIP 226, ``Value Commitment'', is
$[v^{\mathsf{net}}]\,\mathsf{AssetBase} + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
which at the base $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is the Orchard net value commitment
$[v^{\mathsf{net}}]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ of
the cited Definition. The note commitment of ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
Commitment'', is by its case split the Orchard note commitment when the
Asset Base is $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$; the case split is stated in ``Note
commitments'' (\S\ref{sec:notes-cm}). The base $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is a
group-hash output under a separator distinct from
\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, so by Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation}
no Asset Base of a Custom Asset equals it, except with negligible
probability.

\section{Notes and note commitments}\label{sec:notes}

This section extends the Orchard note by one field, the Asset Base
(\S\ref{sec:notes-note}); extends the note commitment by a case split whose
native branch is the Orchard commitment (\S\ref{sec:notes-cm}); states the
derivations of the note's randomness from its seed and what no live text
fixes of them (\S\ref{sec:notes-seed}); states the field set of the note
plaintext (\S\ref{sec:notes-plaintext}); and replaces the fixed value base of
the net value commitment by the Asset Base (\S\ref{sec:notes-cv}). It proves
hiding and binding of both commitments within and across Assets. Every
construction of the section is taken from ZIP 226, which is Draft; the
Orchard objects it extends are those of the \emph{Ironwood Guide} and are
cited, not restated.

\subsection{The OrchardZSA note}\label{sec:notes-note}

\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA note]\label{def:zsa-note}
An \emph{OrchardZSA note} is a tuple
\[
  n = (d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \psi, \rcm)
\]
of type
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{Note}^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}} := {}&
    \{0,1\}^{88} \times \bigl(\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \setminus
    \{\mathcal{O}\}\bigr) \times \{0, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\} \\
  &\times \Ep^{*} \times \F_{p_{\Pallas}} \times \F_{p_{\Pallas}}
    \times \F_{p_{\Vesta}},
\end{align*}
with $\Ep^{*} = \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \setminus \{\mathcal{O}\}$ as in
\S\ref{sec:asset-base}. It is the note of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``Note'' (\S``Notes'') with exactly one added field, the
\emph{Asset Base} $\mathsf{AssetBase} \in \Ep^{*}$ of the note's Asset:
for a Custom Asset the point of Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}, for the
Native Asset the point $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:native-base}. The components
$(d, \pkd, v, \rho, \psi, \rcm)$ and their types, with $\rcm$ typed in
$\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$, are those of the cited definition. ZIP 226 types the
added field in $\PP^{*}$, a group element that is neither the identity nor
$\bot$ (Table~\ref{tab:notation}). The value $v$ counts units of the note's
Asset; the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Unit of value'' (the
zatoshi) applies only when $\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (ZIP
226, ``Note Structure and Commitment''; protocol specification,
\S``Shielded Pools and Notes''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Note commitments}\label{sec:notes-cm}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, encodings.py:
% [sec:notes-cm] Orchard message g_d* || pk_d* || LE_64(v) || LE_255(rho)
%   || LE_255(psi): 1086 bits (109 chunks)
% [sec:notes-cm] OrchardZSA custom-branch message, the Orchard message
%   || asset_base (256 bits): 1342 bits = 135 chunks of 10 bits (padding 8);
%   135 <= c = 253 (at most k c = 2530 bits)
% [sec:notes-cm] the extra 256 bits of asset_base add 26 chunks
% Cited: k = 10, c = 253: spec 'Sinsemilla Hash Function'; 1086-bit Orchard
%   message, 109 chunks: Ironwood Guide Construction 'Orchard instances';
%   253-chunk bound: Ironwood Guide Construction 'Sinsemilla hash on Pallas'
\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA note commitment]\label{def:zsa-notecommit}
Let $n = (d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \psi, \rcm)$ be an
OrchardZSA note, $\gd := \mathsf{DiversifyHash}(d)$, and
\[
  M(n) := \gd^{\star} \,\|\, \pkd^{\star} \,\|\, \mathrm{LE}_{64}(v)
    \,\|\, \mathrm{LE}_{255}(\rho) \,\|\, \mathrm{LE}_{255}(\psi)
\]
the $1086$-bit Orchard message of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition
``Note commitment''. Let
\[
  M^{\mathsf{ZSA}}(n) := M(n) \,\|\, \mathsf{AssetBase}^{\star},
\]
where the $256$-bit star encoding $\mathsf{AssetBase}^{\star}$ has the bytes
$\mathsf{asset\_base}$ of Definition~\ref{def:assetbase-bytes}. The string
$M^{\mathsf{ZSA}}(n)$ has the fixed length $1342$: $135$ chunks of $10$
bits with padding $8$, against $109$ chunks for $M(n)$, within the chunk
bound $c = 253$ of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Sinsemilla
hash on Pallas''. With the Orchard blinding base and the hash point
\begin{align*}
  H_D &:= \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-NoteCommit-r},\,
    \varepsilon), \\
  \hat{M}^{\mathsf{ZSA}} &:= \mathsf{SinsemillaHashToPoint}(
    \texttt{z.cash:ZSA-NoteCommit-M},\, M^{\mathsf{ZSA}}(n)),
\end{align*}
the \emph{OrchardZSA note commitment}
$\cmsym := \NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcm}(n)$ of $n$ is defined
in two branches. In the \emph{native branch},
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, it is
\[
  \cmsym := \NoteCommit_{\rcm}(M(n)),
\]
with $\NoteCommit$ the Orchard commitment of the cited definition. In the
\emph{custom branch}, $\mathsf{AssetBase} \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, it is
\[
  \cmsym :=
  \begin{cases}
    \hat{M}^{\mathsf{ZSA}} + [\rcm]\,H_D
      & \text{if } \hat{M}^{\mathsf{ZSA}} \neq \bot, \\
    \bot & \text{otherwise}.
  \end{cases}
\]
In
both branches $\cmsym \in \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \cup \{\bot\}$, and the
\emph{extracted note commitment} is
$\cmx := \Extract_{\PP}^{\bot}(\cmsym) \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}} \cup \{\bot\}$,
as for Orchard. The custom branch is not the routed
$\mathsf{SinsemillaCommit}$ of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Sinsemilla commitment and short commitment'' for any domain: its hash
domain is new, while its blinding base is Orchard's. The hash base
$Q(\texttt{z.cash:ZSA-NoteCommit-M})$ is a row of
Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa}. The new domain hashes the single length
$1342$, as the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``Fixed length'' requires of
every domain (ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and Commitment''; protocol
specification, \S``Sinsemilla commitments''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

In the native branch the Asset Base is not an input of the commitment.
Hence the OrchardZSA commitment of a note with
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ equals, bit for bit, the Orchard
commitment of its Orchard components; an Orchard note and a native
OrchardZSA note with the same Orchard components have the same $\cmx$; and
every leaf of the Orchard-pool note commitment tree created before
OrchardZSA remains a valid opening target. Both branches output in
$\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \cup \{\bot\}$, so custom-branch commitments enter
the same tree. Each statement is immediate from
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} (ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
Commitment'' and ``Rationale for Note Commitment'').

\begin{proposition}[Hiding and binding of OrchardZSA note commitments]
\label{prop:zsa-cm}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Hiding with one output distribution. Let $\rcm$ be uniform on
  $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ and independent of the content. For every content
  $(d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \psi)$ whose hash point in its
  branch is not $\bot$, the commitment $\cmsym$ is uniform on
  $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$. Hence the distributions of $\cmsym$ and of
  $\cmx$ depend neither on the content nor on the branch, and commitments
  to ZEC and to Custom Assets are identically distributed.
\item[(b)] Binding. Under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions
  ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' and ``GroupHash as a random oracle'',
  no efficient algorithm outputs, except with negligible probability,
  OrchardZSA notes $n \neq n'$ with $\cmx(n) = \cmx(n') \neq \bot$; in
  particular none with
  $\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \neq \mathsf{AssetBase}'$,
  and none whose contents are equal except for the Asset Base.
\item[(c)] Under the same assumptions, no efficient algorithm outputs,
  except with negligible probability, a custom-branch content with
  $\hat{M}^{\mathsf{ZSA}} = \bot$.
\end{enumerate}
Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified
construction; conditional on no unspecified object. Part~(a) is stated for
uniform $\rcm$ and does not cover a derived trapdoor, whose derivation is
classified in Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write
\[
  Q_{\mathsf{O}} := Q(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-NoteCommit-M}), \qquad
  Q_{\mathsf{Z}} := Q(\texttt{z.cash:ZSA-NoteCommit-M}).
\]
The generators
$Q_{\mathsf{O}}$, $Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$, $S(0), \dots, S(1023)$ and $H_D$ are
values of $\GroupHash$ at pairwise distinct inputs, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct} and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s table of
\S``GroupHash, domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve generators''.

(a)~In both branches $\cmsym = \hat{M} + [\rcm]\,H_D$, with the same
blinding base $H_D \neq \mathcal{O}$ and a point $\hat{M}$ fixed by the
content. The proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Hiding and binding
of SinsemillaCommit''~(i) uses only these two facts, the first established
for $H_D$ before that volume's Proposition ``Hiding and binding of note
commitments''; so $\cmsym$ is uniform, whatever the content and the branch.
The value $\cmx$ is a fixed function of $\cmsym$.

(b)~Step~1. The map from a content to its branch and message is injective
except on a diversifier collision: the branch is fixed by whether
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$; in the native branch the Asset
Base is determined, and the message $M(n)$ determines the Orchard components;
in the custom branch the message $M^{\mathsf{ZSA}}(n)$ determines them and,
by the injectivity of the star encoding, the Asset Base. A diversifier
collision is excluded as in Step~1 of the proof of the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Proposition ``Hiding and binding of note commitments''~(b).
Outside that event, $n \neq n'$ gives distinct branches, or one branch with
distinct messages, or one branch and one message with $\rcm \neq \rcm'$.

Step~2. Let $\cmx(n) = \cmx(n') \neq \bot$. Both commitments are points,
and by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Coordinate extraction identifies
opposite points'', $\cmsym' = \pm\cmsym$; both hash points are not $\bot$.
Three cases remain.

Both native. The Orchard components differ, since the Asset Bases agree, and
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Hiding and binding of note
commitments''~(b) applies.

Both custom. The three steps of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition
``Collision resistance of the Sinsemilla instances'' hold for the message
length $l = 1342$ and $n = 135$ chunks. Since
$2^{135} \le 2^{c} \le (p_{\Vesta} - 1)/2$, every difference
$\chi_j(m) - \chi_j(m')$ of the unfolding in its proof has absolute value
below $p_{\Vesta}$, so $\chi$ is injective, and the coefficient $2^{136}$ on
$Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$ in the opposite case is non-zero modulo $p_{\Vesta}$.
Equal points on distinct messages, or opposite points, therefore give a
non-trivial relation among $Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$, the $S(j)$ and $H_D$. Equal
messages with $\rcm \neq \rcm'$ and equal points give
$[\rcm - \rcm']\,H_D = \mathcal{O}$, impossible for $H_D \neq \mathcal{O}$
in a group of prime order $p_{\Vesta}$; opposite points on equal messages
are the opposite case above.

Across branches. Let $n$ be native and $n'$ custom, with pieces $m$ and
$m'$ of $M(n)$ and $M^{\mathsf{ZSA}}(n')$. Unfolding both hash points gives
\[
  [2^{109}]\,Q_{\mathsf{O}} \mp [2^{135}]\,Q_{\mathsf{Z}}
  + \textstyle\sum_{j} [\chi_j(m) \mp \chi_j(m')]\,S(j)
  + [\rcm \mp \rcm']\,H_D = \mathcal{O}.
\]
The coefficient $2^{109}$ on $Q_{\mathsf{O}}$ is non-zero modulo
$p_{\Vesta}$.

In each case the relation is non-trivial among values of $\GroupHash$ at
distinct inputs, which the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Relations among
fixed generators'' excludes except with negligible probability. The first
particular case of~(b) is the case across branches; the second is either
that case or the case of two custom notes with distinct messages.

(c)~Step~(2) of the proof of the cited proposition applies to the domain
of $Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$: an operand $\mathcal{O}$ arises only if
$Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$ or some $S(j)$ is $\mathcal{O}$, itself a non-trivial
relation, and an exceptional case at step $i$ yields a relation with
coefficient $\alpha\,2^{i-1}$ on $Q_{\mathsf{Z}}$, where
$\alpha \in \{-1, 1, 2\}$ and $0 < |\alpha\,2^{i-1}| \le 2^{135}
< p_{\Vesta}$. The same lemma excludes both.
\end{proof}

ZIP 226 argues the property in rationale only, attributing it to the
Sinsemilla hash function (``by definition of the Sinsemilla hash function'',
``Rationale for Note Commitment''; see also ``Privacy Implications''). The
unblinded hash is deterministic and hides nothing; the hiding comes from the
term $[\rcm]\,H_D$. The plaintext equivalence of Orchard and native OrchardZSA
notes, which is withdrawn ZIP 230 text, is not used.

\begin{remark}[Contextual distinguishability of commitments]
\label{rem:zsa-cm-context}
Commitments are hidden only as group elements. ZIP 226, ``Privacy
Implications'', states that Orchard note commitments are distinguishable
from OrchardZSA note commitments by context, since the two protocols are
carried by different transaction versions (in its text, version~$5$ for
Orchard and version~$6$ for OrchardZSA), whether or not both are active. No
current format assigns OrchardZSA a version: version~$6$ is the format of
ZIP 229, ``Transaction Format'', which carries Orchard-pool and
Ironwood-pool Actions and no ZSA. ZIP 248, ``Potential Future Bundle
Types'', lists OrchardZSA provisionally as variant~$2$ of bundle type~$3$,
the Orchard bundle type, whose public variant identifier would likewise
separate the two. Within one OrchardZSA bundle, commitments to ZEC and to
Custom Assets are identically distributed under a uniform trapdoor
(Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(a)); the derived trapdoor, undefined in live
text, is not covered (Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}).
The carrier is treated in ``The transaction carrier''
(\S\ref{sec:carrier}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The note seed and the lead byte}\label{sec:notes-seed}

ZIP 226, ``Abstract'', is defined relative to the protocol with ZIP 2005
applied, and reads $\mathsf{H}^{\mathsf{rcm}}$, $\mathsf{H}^{\psi}$ and the
recoverable plaintexts as ZIP 2005 defines them; it calls ZIP 2005
``Orchard Quantum Recoverability''. ZIP 2005 is now ``Ironwood Quantum
Recoverability'' (Proposed). It is active with NU6.3 and merged into the
protocol specification (ZIP 258, ``ZIP 2005 activation''; ZIP 2005,
``Specification Updates''), and it applies its recoverable plaintext format,
lead byte $\mathtt{0x03}$, only to Ironwood-pool notes (ZIP 229,
``Transaction Format''), while ZIP 226 places OrchardZSA notes in the
Orchard pool (Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}). The two premises no longer
coincide. Two constructions depend on them: the lead byte, here, and the
seed of split nullifiers, at ``Nullifiers of OrchardZSA notes''
(\S\ref{sec:action-nf}). The derivations of ZIP 2005 are constructed in the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The note seed: Ironwood derivations (lead byte
$\mathtt{0x03}$)'', and are cited, not restated.

\begin{definition}[Seed derivations of an OrchardZSA note]
\label{def:zsa-seed}
Let an OrchardZSA note be created with recipient $(d, \pkd)$, value $v$,
element $\rho \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, note seed $\mathsf{rseed}$ uniform on
the $32$-byte strings, and lead byte $\mathsf{leadByte}$. Let
$\underline{\rho} := \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho)$ and
$\gd := \mathsf{DiversifyHash}(d)$. Then
\begin{align*}
  \psi &:= \mathrm{ToBase}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(
    [\mathtt{0x09}] \,\|\, \underline{\rho})\bigr), \\
  \rcm &:= \mathsf{Derive\_rcm}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(\mathsf{leadByte},
    \gd^{\star}, \pkd^{\star}, v, \underline{\rho}, \psi),
\end{align*}
where $\psi$ does not depend on the lead byte, and
$\mathsf{Derive\_rcm}$ is defined only for two lead bytes:
\[
  \mathsf{Derive\_rcm}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(\mathsf{leadByte}, \dots) :=
  \begin{cases}
    \mathrm{ToScalar}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(
      [\mathtt{0x05}] \,\|\, \underline{\rho})\bigr)
      & \text{if } \mathsf{leadByte} = \mathtt{0x02}, \\
    \text{the } \mathsf{pre\_rcm} \text{ derivation}
      & \text{if } \mathsf{leadByte} = \mathtt{0x03},
  \end{cases}
\]
the latter being that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Derivations under lead byte $\mathtt{0x03}$''. Neither input list
contains $\mathsf{AssetBase}$. ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and Commitment'',
requires $\mathsf{leadByte}$ to be the placeholder
$\{\{\mathsf{ZSALEADBYTE}\}\}$ whenever \S``Sending Notes (Orchard)'' or
\S``Dummy Notes (Orchard)'' of the protocol specification is invoked,
directly or indirectly, for an OrchardZSA note. That rule speaks of ``the
computation of $\rho$ and $\psi$''; under the amended specification the lead
byte enters only $\mathsf{Derive\_rcm}$. The specification admits lead byte
$\mathtt{0x02}$ in the Orchard pool and $\mathtt{0x03}$ in the Ironwood pool
only (protocol specification, \S``Note Plaintexts and Memo Fields''), and
ZIP 2005, ``Usage with other proposals requiring note plaintext format
changes'', requires such proposals to use a new lead byte value or values.
Classes: the derivation of $\psi$ is specified; the derivation of $\rcm$ and
the value of the lead byte are classified in Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Unassigned lead byte]\label{rem:leadbyte-open}
Each part carries one class of Definition~\ref{def:classes}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The derivation of $\psi$ is specified.
\item The derivation of $\rcm$ is designed but unspecified. A complete
  design existed: ZIP 226 fixed $\mathtt{0x03}$ with $\mathsf{H}^{\mathsf{rcm}}$
  before $\mathtt{0x03}$ passed to ZIP 2005, and the design needs only a
  lead byte.
\item The value of the lead byte is an open problem. The removed design's
  $\mathtt{0x03}$ is the Ironwood lead byte in live text, a design that
  conflicts with live text; the placeholders
  $\{\{\mathsf{ZSALEADBYTE}\}\}$ (ZIP 226), $\{\{\mathsf{LEADBYTE}\}\}$
  (withdrawn ZIP 230) and $\{\{\mathsf{MBLEADBYTE}\}\}$ (ZIP 231, ``Note
  plaintext lead byte assignment'') are unassigned.
\end{enumerate}
Until the byte is assigned, the $\rcm$ of an OrchardZSA note is undefined in
live text, and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Hiding under the
derived trapdoor'' does not transfer. The OrchardZSA Action statement, like
the Orchard one, does not check the derivation (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
Remark ``What the statement does not check''~(ii)), so for the outputs of a
transfer the gap binds only sender and recipient. Its consequence for
validators is stated at ``Issue Notes'' (\S\ref{sec:issue-note}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The note plaintext}\label{sec:notes-plaintext}

\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA note plaintext]\label{def:zsa-plaintext}
The \emph{OrchardZSA note plaintext} of a created OrchardZSA note carries
the fields $(\mathsf{leadByte}, d, v, \mathsf{rseed}, \mathsf{memo})$ of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Note plaintext'' and, in addition, the
$32$-byte string $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:assetbase-bytes} (ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
Commitment''). The recipient needs $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ to recompute
$\cmx$ by Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} and to spend the note. Given
an encoding of fixed length and an assigned lead byte, the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Constructions ``Encryption to the recipient'' and ``Trial
decryption'' apply unchanged. Its acceptance checks (\S``Trial decryption
and note acceptance'') apply with the lead-byte check and the derivation of
$\rcm$ replaced by those of that lead byte
(Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}), $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ required to decode
to a point of $\Ep^{*}$, and the recomputed commitment that of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}. Classes: the field set is
specified; the encoding is an open problem.
\end{definition}

ZIP 226 delegates the encoding to ZIP 230, which is Withdrawn. Its layout
\[
  \mathsf{leadByte} \,\|\, d \,\|\, v \,\|\, \mathsf{rseed} \,\|\,
  \mathsf{asset\_base} \,\|\, \mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{memo}}
\]
presupposes the memo key $\mathsf{K}^{\mathsf{memo}}$ of ZIP 231, which is
now defined only for version-$7$ transactions (ZIP 231, ``Specification''),
and a lead byte that live text assigns otherwise
(Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}). No current ZIP defines an OrchardZSA
plaintext encoding. The statement of withdrawn ZIP 230 that Orchard and
native OrchardZSA notes need not be distinguished in plaintexts carries no
weight; the commitment half of that statement follows from
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}.

\subsection{The per-Asset value commitment}\label{sec:notes-cv}

\begin{definition}[Per-Asset value commitment]\label{def:zsa-cv}
Let an Action (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Action'') consume an
OrchardZSA note of value $v^{\mathsf{old}}$ and create an OrchardZSA note of
value $v^{\mathsf{new}}$, both with Asset Base
$\mathsf{AssetBase} \in \Ep^{*}$. Its \emph{net value} is the integer
\[
  v^{\mathsf{net}} := v^{\mathsf{old}} - v^{\mathsf{new}}
    \in \{-2^{64} + 1, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\}.
\]
For $\rcv$ drawn by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Value-commitment trapdoor'', its \emph{per-Asset value commitment} is
\[
  \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}
  := \mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcv}(\mathsf{AssetBase},
    v^{\mathsf{net}})
  := [v^{\mathsf{net}} \bmod p_{\Vesta}]\,\mathsf{AssetBase}
    + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
with $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and the reading of $v^{\mathsf{net}}$ modulo
$p_{\Vesta}$ as in the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Net value
commitment''. For $\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ it equals
$\mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}_{\rcv}(v^{\mathsf{net}})$. The only
change against Orchard is that the fixed-base multiplication by
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ becomes a variable-base multiplication by the
Action's Asset Base; the randomness term is unchanged. The condition that
controls the base is stated in ``The OrchardZSA Action statement''
(\S\ref{sec:action-statement}) (ZIP 226, ``Value Commitment''; protocol
specification, \S``Homomorphic Pedersen commitments (Sapling and
Orchard)''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Hiding and binding of per-Asset value commitments]
\label{lem:zsa-cv}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Perfect hiding. For every $B \in \Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$ and every
  integer $v$, with $\rcv$ uniform on $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ and independent of
  $(B, v)$, the point $[v \bmod p_{\Vesta}]\,B + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$
  is uniform on $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$.
\item[(ii)] Binding across bases. Call a base \emph{admissible} if it is
  given as the value of $\GroupHash$ at the input
  $(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv}, \texttt{v})$ or at
  $(\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, D)$ for a byte string $D$. Under the
  \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' and
  ``GroupHash as a random oracle'', no efficient algorithm outputs, except
  with negligible probability, two families $((B_i, v_i))_i$ and
  $((B'_j, v'_j))_j$ of polynomial size, of admissible bases with integers,
  and trapdoors $\rcv, \rcv'$, with
  \[
    \textstyle\sum_i [v_i]\,B_i + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    = \sum_j [v'_j]\,B'_j + [\rcv']\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
  \]
  while the base-value vectors differ: for some group-hash input $x$, the
  sum of the $v_i$ at bases with input $x$ and the sum of the $v'_j$ at
  bases with input $x$ differ modulo $p_{\Vesta}$. For one pair each,
  $(B, v)$ and $(B', v')$ with inputs $x$ and $x'$, equal commitments
  force $x = x'$, hence $B = B'$, and $v \equiv v'$, or else
  $v \equiv v' \equiv 0 \pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$.
\item[(iii)] Binding fails for arbitrary bases. For $B \neq \mathcal{O}$,
  $v \not\equiv 0$ and $v' \not\equiv 0, v \pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$, the point
  $B' := [v\,v'^{-1}]\,B$ satisfies $B' \notin \{\mathcal{O}, B\}$ and
  $[v]\,B = [v']\,B'$, so the two openings $(B, v)$ and $(B', v')$ share
  every trapdoor and every commitment. Hence the Action statement must
  control the base.
\end{enumerate}
Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here; conditional on no
unspecified object.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i)~The proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Hiding and binding of
net value commitments''~(i) uses only $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \neq
\mathcal{O}$ and the prime order $p_{\Vesta}$ of the group, not the value
base: $[\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is uniform, and so is its translate by
the fixed point $[v \bmod p_{\Vesta}]\,B$.

(ii)~Let $P_x$ denote the value of $\GroupHash$ at input $x$, and $c_x$
the difference, modulo $p_{\Vesta}$, of the two sums of values at input
$x$. Grouping both sides by input, a double opening gives
\[
  \textstyle\sum_x [c_x]\,P_x + [\rcv - \rcv']\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
  = \mathcal{O}
\]
with some $c_x \neq 0$, the relation of the proof of the \emph{Crypto
Guide}'s Theorem ``Properties of the vector commitment'' (\S``Pedersen
vector commitments''). The inputs $x$ are pairwise distinct by
construction, and distinct from the input
$(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv}, \texttt{r})$ of $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$: the
input of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ differs from it in the message, and every
input under \texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA} differs from both by
Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct}. The relation is therefore non-trivial
among values of $\GroupHash$ at distinct inputs, and the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Lemma ``Relations among fixed generators'' excludes it except with
negligible probability. For one pair each, $x \neq x'$ gives $c_x = v$ and
$c_{x'} = -v'$, non-trivial unless $v \equiv v' \equiv 0$; and $x = x'$
gives $c_x = v - v'$, non-trivial unless $v \equiv v'$.

(iii)~Direct computation: $[v']\,B' = [v' v\,v'^{-1}]\,B = [v]\,B$; the
scalar $v\,v'^{-1}$ is neither $0$ nor $1$ modulo $p_{\Vesta}$, and $B$
generates the group of prime order $p_{\Vesta}$, so $B' \neq \mathcal{O}$
and $B' \neq B$.
\end{proof}

ZIP 226, ``Rationale for Value Commitment'', states the change of value base
without a binding argument; Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-cv} supplies it for bases of
the admissible form, and part~(iii) shows that the form cannot be dropped.

\section{OrchardZSA Actions and Action Groups}\label{sec:action}

This section shows that the membership exemption of Orchard dummy notes is
unsound once the value base of an Action is a witness, and defines the
Split Inputs that replace dummy consumed notes for Custom Assets
(\S\ref{sec:action-split}); constructs the split nullifier and proves its
independence from the nullifier of the copied note (\S\ref{sec:action-nf});
states the OrchardZSA Action statement as a delta on the Orchard one, with
named conditions (\S\ref{sec:action-statement}); states the assumptions on
its proof and proves that a Split Input requires the spend authority of the
copied note (\S\ref{sec:action-proof}); and defines the Action Group
(\S\ref{sec:action-group}). The objects of the section are taken from ZIP
226 and ZIP 228, both Draft, with the rules of ZIP 227 that bound the number
of Action Groups. The Orchard objects they modify are those of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide} and are cited, not restated.

\subsection{Dummy notes and Split Inputs}\label{sec:action-split}

By the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Dummy notes'' (\S``Actions,
bundles, and dummy notes''), an Action whose consumed side is unused
consumes a dummy note of value $0$: under a fresh key or, when
$\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0$ and the created note is real, at the
expanded receiver of the created note and under the key from which that
receiver derives. Condition A3 of that
volume's Definition ``Orchard Action statement'', namely
$v^{\mathsf{old}} \cdot (\mathit{root} - \mathit{rt}) = 0$ or some
evaluation of $\mathsf{SinsemillaHash}$ in the fold returns $\bot$, exempts
every consumed note of value $0$ from membership (protocol specification,
\S``Dummy Notes (Orchard)'' and \S``Action Statement (Orchard)''). In the
Orchard protocol the exemption is sound for value: condition A4 multiplies
the value by the fixed generator $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (\emph{Ironwood
Guide}, Definition ``Net value commitment''), so a consumed note of value
$0$ contributes $[0]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = \mathcal{O}$, whatever path it
fails to prove. The per-Asset value commitment of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-cv} multiplies by a witnessed Asset Base instead.
The next proposition shows that the exemption, kept unchanged, then admits
a balance violation.

\begin{proposition}[Unsoundness of the dummy exemption under a witnessed
base]\label{prop:dummy-attack}
Let $\Rel^{-}$ be the relation obtained from the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``Orchard Action statement'' by adding an auxiliary input
$A \in \Ep^{*}$ and making three replacements: in A1 and A2, the
commitment $\NoteCommit$ is replaced by $\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}$
of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} with Asset Base $A$ for both notes;
in A4, the net value commitment becomes
\[
  \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}} = [v^{\mathsf{old}} - v^{\mathsf{new}}]\,A
    + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
as in Definition~\ref{def:zsa-cv}; condition A3 and conditions A5 to A10
are unchanged, so a consumed note of value $0$ is exempt from membership
whatever $A$ is. Then for every $v \in \{1, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\}$ there is a
bundle of two Actions whose witnesses satisfy $\Rel^{-}$, with balancing
value $0$ and no burn, whose binding signature verifies, and which creates a
ZEC note, of Asset Base $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, of value $v$ while every
consumed note has value $0$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[] Action~1 witnesses $A := [-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
  $v^{\mathsf{old}} = 0$ and $v^{\mathsf{new}} = v$;
\item[] Action~2 witnesses $A := V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, a dummy consumed
  note and $v^{\mathsf{new}} = v$.
\end{enumerate}
More generally, a witnessed base $A = \sum_i [a_i]\,B_i$ with known
coefficients $a_i$ over Asset Bases $B_i$ contributes
$\sum_i [a_i v]\,B_i$ for a committed value $v$, so a value committed on $A$
cancels values committed on the $B_i$. This is the attack of ZIP 226,
``Rationale for Split Notes'': without the enforcement ``the prover could
input a multiple (or linear combination) of an existing Asset Base, and
thereby attack the network by overflowing the ZEC value balance and hence
counterfeiting ZEC funds''. Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}):
proved here; ZIP 226 states the attack in words only.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The point $[-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is not $\mathcal{O}$, since
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ has prime order $p_{\Vesta}$, so Action~1 is well
typed. Both consumed notes have value $0$, so A3 holds for both Actions
without a path, and A8 holds for either value of $\mathsf{enableSpends}$;
A9 holds with $\mathsf{enableOutputs} = 1$. The remaining conditions hold
when the commitments, the nullifiers and $\rk$ are computed from the witness
by their definitions, under a key of the prover; each consumed note is
placed at the expanded receiver of the note its Action creates, so that A10
holds for either value of $\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$, as in the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``Meaning of A10''. The net value commitments
are
\begin{align*}
  \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_1
    &= [0 - v]\,\bigl([-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}\bigr)
       + [\rcv_1]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
     = [v]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + [\rcv_1]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}, \\
  \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_2
    &= [0 - v]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + [\rcv_2]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}.
\end{align*}
With balancing value $0$ and no burn, the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``Binding validating key'' gives
\[
  \mathsf{bvk} = \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_1 + \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_2
    = [\rcv_1 + \rcv_2]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
whose discrete logarithm $\mathsf{bsk} = \rcv_1 + \rcv_2$ to the base
$R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ the prover knows, so that volume's Construction
``Binding signature'' produces a valid signature. Action~2 creates a ZEC
note of value $v$, and no consumed note carries value. For the general
case, $[w]\,\bigl(\sum_i [a_i]\,B_i\bigr) = \sum_i [a_i w]\,B_i$ for every
integer $w$, by bilinearity of scalar multiplication, and the same
computation applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Split Input and Split Action]\label{def:split-input}
Let $B \in \Ep^{*}$ with $B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. A \emph{Split Input}
of Asset Base $B$ is an OrchardZSA note
$n = (d, \pkd, v, B, \rho, \psi, \rcm)$ (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-note}),
whose extracted commitment
\[
  \Extract_{\PP}\bigl(\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcm}(n)\bigr)
\]
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}) is a leaf of the note commitment tree
under the anchor of the consuming Action, consumed by an Action that sets
the witness bit $\mathsf{split\_flag} := 1$ of the statement defined in
``The OrchardZSA Action statement'' (\S\ref{sec:action-statement}). A Split
Input differs from a spend of $n$ in two respects only: its value is
replaced by $0$ in the net value commitment, while the commitment still
opens with the true value $v$; and the Action publishes the split nullifier
of ``Nullifiers of OrchardZSA notes'' (\S\ref{sec:action-nf}) in place of
the nullifier of $n$. A \emph{Split Action} is an Action whose consumed
note is a Split Input. ZIP 226 calls $n$ ``a previously issued input note
(that is, a note that has previously been included in the Merkle tree)'';
any leaf of Asset Base $B$ qualifies, whether its note was created by an
Action or by issuance (ZIP 226, ``Terminology'' and ``Split Notes''). Class:
specified.
\end{definition}

\emph{Padding rule.} In a bundle (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition
``Bundle'') of OrchardZSA Actions, for each Custom Asset whose Asset Base
$B$ has fewer consumed notes than created notes, the sender gives each
input-less Action of $B$ a Split Input of $B$ (ZIP 226, ``Split Notes'').
Actions of ZEC keep the dummy consumed notes of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``Dummy notes''; Actions of Custom Assets take no dummy consumed
note (ZIP 226, ``Backward Compatibility''). Both regimes occur within one
bundle of OrchardZSA Actions. That bundle is not the Orchard-pool bundle of
a version-$6$ transaction: version~$6$ is the format of ZIP 229, which
carries no ZSA fields (ZIP 229, ``Non-requirements''). ZIP 226 enforces in
the statement that every consumed note of a Custom Asset, split or not and
of any value, is a leaf: ``for Custom Assets we enforce that \emph{every}
input note to an OrchardZSA Action must be proven to exist in the set of
note commitments'' (``Rationale for Split Notes''). The condition is
constructed in ``The OrchardZSA Action statement''
(\S\ref{sec:action-statement}), and Corollary~\ref{cor:action-bases}, in
``Security'', states what it yields for the Asset Base of the created note.

\begin{remark}[Choice of the copied note]\label{rem:copied-note}
ZIP 226, ``Split Notes'', lets the sender copy one of three notes of Asset
Base $B$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(1)] another note of $B$ consumed by a real spend in the same bundle,
  but not by this Split Input;
\item[(2)] a different unspent note of $B$, which is not thereby spent;
\item[(3)] an already spent note of $B$, the zeroed value preventing a
  second spend.
\end{enumerate}
It states that ``we do not care about whether the previously issued note
copied to create a Split Input is owned by the sender, or whether it was
nullified before''. Proposition~\ref{prop:split-ownership}, in ``The
OrchardZSA Action proof'' (\S\ref{sec:action-proof}), states which of these
choices are available to a sender who does not hold the spend authority of
the copied note. Under Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}, if OrchardZSA notes
are in the Orchard pool, which ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules from NU6.3
activation'', restricts to Actions with $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress} = 0$,
then a Split Action whose created note is at an expanded receiver other
than that of the copied note violates the cross-address rule. ZIP 226 does
not address this case.
\end{remark}

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Nullifiers of OrchardZSA notes}\label{sec:action-nf}

An OrchardZSA note consumed by a real spend, with $\mathsf{split\_flag} =
0$, has the Orchard nullifier of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition
``Nullifier'':
\[
  \nfsym = \mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho, \psi, \cmsym)
  = \Extract_{\PP}\bigl([(\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho)
    + \psi) \bmod p_{\Pallas}]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + \cmsym\bigr),
\]
with $\cmsym = \NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcm}(n)$ of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} (ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and
Commitment'': ``generated in the same manner as in the Orchard protocol'';
protocol specification, \S``Computing $\rho$ values and Nullifiers''). It
depends on the Asset Base only through $\cmsym$; for a note with
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ it is the Orchard nullifier bit
for bit. The extension of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``One nullifier
per note'' to OrchardZSA notes is Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}, in
``Security''.

A Split Action publishes one nullifier, which enters the nullifier set of
its pool, and a nullifier must not repeat, either within a transaction or
across the block chain (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets'';
protocol specification, \S``Nullifier Sets''). The copied note may be
unspent, choice~(2) of Remark~\ref{rem:copied-note}, or spent in the same
bundle, choice~(1). Publishing its nullifier would, in the first case, make
it unspendable and, in the second, repeat a nullifier within the
transaction, which invalidates the transaction. The nullifier of a Split
Input must therefore differ from that of the copied note. ZIP 226 states the
purpose of the change as privacy: it is made ``to prevent the
identification of notes'' (``Circuit Statement'').

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:issue-rho] ... each ToBase output adds statistical distance at most
%   p_Pallas/2^512 < 2^-257 (about 2^-258.00)
% Cited: ToBase distance m/2^L: Math Guide 'Uniform sampling and the bias of
%   modular reduction'; PRF^expand = BLAKE2b-512 under Zcash_ExpandSeed:
%   spec 'Pseudo Random Functions'
\begin{construction}[Split nullifier]\label{def:split-nf}
Let
\[
  L^{\mathsf{Orchard}} := \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard}, \texttt{L}),
\]
the input of the second row of Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa}.
For a Split Input with nullifier deriving key $\nksym$, element
$\rho^{\mathsf{old}}$ and commitment $\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}$, the sender
draws $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$ uniformly from $\mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}$ and
sets
\[
  \psi^{\mathsf{nf}} := \mathrm{ToBase}\bigl(
    \mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed\_nf}}([\mathtt{0x0A}] \,\|\,
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho^{\mathsf{old}}))\bigr)
  \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}.
\]
The \emph{split nullifier} is
\[
  \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}} := \Extract_{\PP}\bigl(
    [(\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho^{\mathsf{old}})
      + \psi^{\mathsf{nf}}) \bmod p_{\Pallas}]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    + \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}} + L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}\bigr).
\]
Against the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Nullifier'' there are two
changes: $\psi^{\mathsf{old}}$ is replaced by the fresh $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$,
and the fixed point $L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is added before extraction. The
derivation adds one row to that volume's Construction ``Domain bytes of PRF
expansion'':
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llllll@{}}
\toprule
$b$ & Key $\kappa$ & Remainder $t'$ & $f$ & Output & Section \\
\midrule
$\mathtt{0x0A}$ & $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$
  & $\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho^{\mathsf{old}})$ & $\mathrm{ToBase}$
  & $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$ & \S\ref{sec:action-nf} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The key $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$ is secret, uniform and used once. The byte
$\mathtt{0x0A}$ is reserved for this use by ZIP 2005's amendment of
\S``Sending Notes (Orchard)'' (ZIP 2005, ``Changes to the Protocol
Specification''; ZIP 226, ``Split Notes''); the specification's own list of
expansion uses
(\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#abstractprfs}{\S``Pseudo
Random Functions''}) does not record it, and ZIP 2005's amendment of that
list adds $\mathtt{0x0A}$ and $\mathtt{0x0B}$. It differs from the bytes
$\mathtt{0x04}$ of $\mathsf{esk}$, $\mathtt{0x05}$ and $\mathtt{0x0B}$ of
$\rcm$ and $\mathtt{0x09}$ of $\psi$ of the Orchard sending derivations
(protocol specification, \S``Sending Notes (Orchard)'';
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-seed}). Class: specified, as a sender rule;
Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-unchecked} states what the statement enforces.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Independence of split nullifiers]\label{prop:split-nf}
Let $n$ be an OrchardZSA note with commitment $\cmsym \neq \bot$, elements
$\rho, \psi$, and nullifier $\nfsym$ under $\nksym$. For
$\psi' \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$ let $\nfsym^{\mathsf{split}}(\psi')$ be the
split nullifier of a copy of $n$ with $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} = \psi'$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``GroupHash as a
  random oracle'' and ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'', no efficient
  algorithm outputs $\nksym$, a note $n$ with the opening of its commitment,
  and $\psi' \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$ with $\nfsym^{\mathsf{split}}(\psi') =
  \nfsym$, except with negligible probability. This holds for every choice
  of $\psi'$, honest or not.
\item[(ii)] Let $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$ be uniform and used only for this
  derivation, and let $K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \neq \mathcal{O}$. To an
  adversary that knows $n$, $\nksym$ and the full viewing key, the split
  nullifier is indistinguishable from $\Extract_{\PP}(U)$ for $U$ uniform on
  $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$, with advantage at most
  \[
    \epsilon_{\mathsf{expand}} + \epsilon_{\mathsf{ToBase}} + \delta,
  \]
  where $\epsilon_{\mathsf{expand}}$ bounds the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
  Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'' for one query,
  $\epsilon_{\mathsf{ToBase}} \le p_{\Pallas}/2^{512} < 2^{-257}$ is the
  statistical distance of $\mathrm{ToBase}$ on a uniform $64$-byte input
  from the uniform distribution on $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, and
  $\delta := (p_{\Vesta} - p_{\Pallas})/p_{\Vesta}$ as in that volume's
  Proposition ``Nullifier unlinkability''.
\end{enumerate}
Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified
construction. Part~(ii) concerns honest senders, since the statement does
not enforce the derivation (Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-unchecked}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~Write
\[
  s := (\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho) + \psi)
    \bmod p_{\Pallas}, \qquad
  s' := (\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho) + \psi')
    \bmod p_{\Pallas},
\]
and $K := K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, $L := L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. By the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Coordinate extraction identifies opposite
points'', $\nfsym^{\mathsf{split}}(\psi') = \nfsym$ means
$[s']\,K + \cmsym + L = \pm([s]\,K + \cmsym)$. With the sign $+$,
\[
  L + [s' - s]\,K = \mathcal{O};
\]
with the sign $-$,
\[
  L + [s + s']\,K + [2]\,\cmsym = \mathcal{O}.
\]
Given its opening, $\cmsym \neq \bot$ is a known integer combination of the
Sinsemilla generators of its domain and of its blinding base, by the
unfolding of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Sinsemilla hash on
Pallas'' used in the proof of its Proposition ``Collision resistance of the
Sinsemilla instances''; in either branch of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} these generators are values of
$\GroupHash$ at inputs other than $(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard}, \texttt{L})$
and $(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard}, \texttt{K})$. In both cases $L$ has
coefficient $1$, and its input differs from every other input involved
(Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct}), so the algorithm has output a
non-trivial relation among values of $\GroupHash$ at distinct inputs, which
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Relations among fixed generators''
excludes except with negligible probability. This is the role of the
offset $L$, which ZIP 226 does not state: without it, $\psi' = \psi$
reproduces $\nfsym$.

(ii)~The hybrids follow the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Proposition ``Nullifier unlinkability''. First,
$\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed\_nf}}$, evaluated at the single input
$[\mathtt{0x0A}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho)$, is replaced by a
uniform $64$-byte string, at cost $\epsilon_{\mathsf{expand}}$: the key is
used nowhere else, and the rest of the game is an efficient function of the
answer and of $(n, \nksym)$. Second, $\mathrm{ToBase}$ of that uniform
string is replaced by a uniform $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$,
at cost $\epsilon_{\mathsf{ToBase}}$ (\emph{Math Guide}, \S``Uniform
sampling and the bias of modular reduction''; \emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Pseudorandom functions and field reductions''). Translation by
$\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho)$ is a bijection of
$\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$, so $s'$ is then uniform on
$\{0, \dots, p_{\Pallas} - 1\}$ and independent of $\nksym$, $\rho$ and
all else. Third, $s'$, read in $\F_{p_{\Vesta}}$, is replaced by a uniform
scalar $u \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$, at cost $\delta$, the statistical distance
computed in the cited proposition. Since $K \neq \mathcal{O}$ generates the
group of prime order $p_{\Vesta}$, the point $[u]\,K + \cmsym + L$ is then
uniform on $\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})$ whatever $\cmsym$ and $L$ are, and the
split nullifier is $\Extract_{\PP}$ of a uniform point.
\end{proof}

In the model of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``GroupHash as a
random oracle'', the event $K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = \mathcal{O}$ excluded in
part~(ii) has probability $1/p_{\Vesta}$.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{The OrchardZSA Action statement}\label{sec:action-statement}

The statement is defined as a delta on the relation
$\Rel_{\mathsf{Action}}$ of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition
``Orchard Action statement'', itself a relation in the sense of the
\emph{Crypto Guide}'s Definition ``Language, relation, witness''
(\S``Languages, relations, and witnesses''). Notation, types and conditions
not named below are those of that definition; conditions A6 to A9 are cited
by name and not restated.

\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA Action statement]\label{def:zsa-action}
\emph{Base.} The seven components of the primary input of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Orchard Action statement'' other than
$\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$, and its conditions A1 to A9. The input
$\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$ and condition A10 are omitted
(Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-action-status}).

\emph{Primary input.}
\[
  \mathsf{pub} = \bigl(\mathit{rt},\, \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}},\,
    \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}},\, \rk,\, \cmx,\, \mathsf{enableSpends},\,
    \mathsf{enableOutputs},\, \mathsf{enableZSA}\bigr),
\]
with the first seven components typed as in the cited definition and
$\mathsf{enableZSA} \in \{0, 1\}$.

\emph{Auxiliary input.} The auxiliary input $\mathsf{aux}$ of the cited
definition, together with
\[
  \mathsf{AssetBase} \in \Ep^{*}, \quad
  \mathsf{is\_native\_asset} \in \{0, 1\}, \quad
  \mathsf{split\_flag} \in \{0, 1\}, \quad
  \psi^{\mathsf{nf}} \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}.
\]

\emph{Notation.} Let $v' := 0$ if $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$ and
$v' := v^{\mathsf{old}}$ otherwise. With $M^{\mathsf{old}}$ and
$M^{\mathsf{new}}$ the $1086$-bit messages of the cited definition, built
from the witnessed diversified bases, write
$\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcm}(\gd^{\star}, \pkd^{\star}, v,
\rho, \psi, \mathsf{AssetBase})$ for the commitment of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} with $M(n)$ replaced by the message
built from these arguments: $\NoteCommit_{\rcm}(M)$ in the native branch,
and
$\mathsf{SinsemillaHashToPoint}(\texttt{z.cash:ZSA-NoteCommit-M}, M \,\|\,
\mathsf{AssetBase}^{\star}) + [\rcm]\,H_D$, or $\bot$, in the custom
branch. Let $L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ be as in Construction~\ref{def:split-nf}.

\emph{Relation.} The relation $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ is the set of
pairs $(\mathsf{pub}, \mathsf{aux})$ of the types above that satisfy the
following conditions.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=3.6em]
\item[A1$'$.] Old note commitment integrity:
  \begin{align*}
    &\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcm^{\mathsf{old}}}\bigl(
      (\gd^{\mathsf{old}})^{\star}, (\pkd^{\mathsf{old}})^{\star},
      v^{\mathsf{old}}, \rho^{\mathsf{old}}, \psi^{\mathsf{old}},
      \mathsf{AssetBase}\bigr) \\
    &\qquad \in \{\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}, \bot\}.
  \end{align*}
\item[A2$'$.] New note commitment integrity:
  \begin{align*}
    &\Extract_{\PP}^{\bot}\bigl(\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{
      \rcm^{\mathsf{new}}}\bigl((\gd^{\mathsf{new}})^{\star},
      (\pkd^{\mathsf{new}})^{\star}, v^{\mathsf{new}}, \rho^{\mathsf{new}},
      \psi^{\mathsf{new}}, \mathsf{AssetBase}\bigr)\bigr) \\
    &\qquad \in \{\cmx, \bot\},
  \end{align*}
  with $\rho^{\mathsf{new}} := \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}}$ and the same
  $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ as in A1$'$.
\item[C1.] Native indicator: $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 1$ if and only
  if $\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$.
\item[C2.] Pause: if $\mathsf{enableZSA} = 0$, then
  $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 1$.
\item[A3$'$.] Merkle path validity: $(v^{\mathsf{old}} = 0$ and
  $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 1)$, or $\mathit{root} = \mathit{rt}$, or
  some evaluation of $\mathsf{SinsemillaHash}$ in the fold returns $\bot$;
  the value $\mathit{root}$, the fold and the encoding bits $\mathsf{c}$
  are those of A3.
\item[A4$'$.] Value commitment integrity:
  \begin{align*}
    \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}
    &= \mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{\rcv}(\mathsf{AssetBase},
      v' - v^{\mathsf{new}}) \\
    &= [v' - v^{\mathsf{new}}]\,\mathsf{AssetBase}
      + [\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
  \end{align*}
  a variable-base multiplication by the witnessed $\mathsf{AssetBase}$
  (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-cv}).
\item[C3.] Split only for Custom Assets: if $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$,
  then $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 0$.
\item[A5$'$.] Nullifier integrity: if $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$, then
  $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} = \psi^{\mathsf{old}}$ and
  \[
    \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}} = \mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(
    \rho^{\mathsf{old}}, \psi^{\mathsf{old}}, \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}});
  \]
  if $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$, then
  \begin{align*}
    \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}} = \Extract_{\PP}\bigl(
      &[(\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho^{\mathsf{old}})
        + \psi^{\mathsf{nf}}) \bmod p_{\Pallas}]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \\
      &+ \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}} + L^{\mathsf{Orchard}}\bigr),
  \end{align*}
  the split nullifier of Construction~\ref{def:split-nf}.
\item[A6, A7.] Spend authority and diversified address integrity:
  unchanged and unconditional; no factor of $\mathsf{split\_flag}$
  relaxes them.
\item[A8, A9.] Enable spend flag and enable output flag: unchanged; A8
  reads $v^{\mathsf{old}}$, not $v'$, and A9 reads $v^{\mathsf{new}}$.
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 226, ``Circuit Statement'', paragraphs ``Asset Base Equality'', ``The
enableZSA Flag'', ``Value Commitment Correctness'' and ``Asset Identifier
Consistency for Split Actions''; ZIP 226, ``Split Notes'' and ``Note
Structure and Commitment''; protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#actionstatement}{\S``Action
Statement (Orchard)''}.) Class: specified at statement level; its status is
stated in Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-action-status}.
\end{definition}

The following consequences are read off Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action},
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} and Definition~\ref{def:native-base}.

\emph{One witness.} Conditions A1$'$, A2$'$ and A4$'$ use the single
witnessed $\mathsf{AssetBase}$, so the consumed and the created note of an
Action have one Asset Base, and its net value commitment is on that base.

\emph{Native branch.} By C1 and the case split of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}, an Action with
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$
satisfies A1$'$, A2$'$, A4$'$ and A5$'$ exactly when
$\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} = \psi^{\mathsf{old}}$ and it satisfies the Orchard A1,
A2, A4 and A5.

\emph{Pause.} With $\mathsf{enableZSA} = 0$, C2 and C1 force
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, and C3 then forces
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$, so the relation admits only Orchard Actions.

\emph{Membership.} Since $0 \le v^{\mathsf{old}} < 2^{64} < p_{\Pallas}$,
the first disjunct of A3$'$ holds only for a consumed ZEC note of value
$0$. Every consumed note of a Custom Asset, split or not and of any value,
satisfies $\mathit{root} = \mathit{rt}$ or the $\bot$ clause. The $\bot$
clause gives no escape: for witnesses extracted under
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, the argument of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Remark ``$\bot$-weakened conditions'', by that volume's Proposition
``Collision resistance of the Sinsemilla instances''~(iii) under its
Assumptions ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' and ``GroupHash as a random
oracle'', excludes it except with negligible probability; the same holds
for the $\bot$ cases of A1$'$ and A2$'$ in both branches of
$\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}$, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(c) for the custom branch.

\emph{Split Actions.} By A4$'$ the copied value never enters
$\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$. By A8, which reads $v^{\mathsf{old}}$, a Split
Action that copies a note of non-zero value requires
$\mathsf{enableSpends} = 1$, while one that copies a note of value $0$
does not.

These consequences are direct from the cited definitions and from the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``$\bot$-weakened conditions'', with
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} in place of that volume's knowledge-soundness
assumption (ZIP 226, ``Circuit Statement'' and ``Overview'').
Table~\ref{tab:action-delta} pairs each condition with the Orchard
condition it replaces and its ZIP 226 paragraph;
Figure~\ref{fig:action-zsa} draws the new witnesses and the conditions that
read them.

\midrule

  \tabularnewline

\begin{remark}[Status of the OrchardZSA Action statement]
\label{rem:zsa-action-status}
The delta is specified at statement level (ZIP 226, Draft, ``Circuit
Statement''). ZIP 226 states it against the specification's statement with
seven primary inputs and conditions A1 to A9. The current Orchard statement
has the eighth primary input $\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$ and condition
A10 (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Orchard Action statement'' and
Remark ``Status''; protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#actiondesc}{\S``Action
Descriptions''}; ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules from NU6.3 activation''), which
ZIP 226 never mentions. The composition of the delta with A10 is therefore
an open problem (Definition~\ref{def:classes}). The statement uses
$\mathsf{enableZSA}$, which ZIP 226 never lists as a primary input. The
encoded instance of the current Orchard statement has ten elements, the
nine that encode its first seven primary inputs and
$\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The Action
statement''); no live text places $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ in it, so its
position is an open problem. ZIP 226 defers ``All
modifications in the Circuit'' to an unversioned document outside the ZIPs,
``Modifications to the Orchard circuit for the OrchardZSA Protocol''; no ZIP
fixes a complete OrchardZSA circuit, which is designed but unspecified. The
reading of A8 on $v^{\mathsf{old}}$ is specified by the delta, which leaves
A8 unchanged; only its confirmation in that circuit document is designed
but unspecified, and Table~\ref{tab:classification} records it.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What the OrchardZSA statement does not check]
\label{rem:zsa-unchecked}
Items (i) to (iii) of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``What the
statement does not check'' carry over. In addition:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(iv)] The witness $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$ is constrained only through
  A5$'$ to the published nullifier. For $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$ only the
  equality $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} = \psi^{\mathsf{old}}$ is switched off, and
  no condition derives $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$ from a seed. The derivation of
  Construction~\ref{def:split-nf} is therefore a sender rule whose formula
  is specified and which nothing enforces: $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$ is
  transmitted nowhere, and no text proposes an enforcement.
  Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(i) holds for every
  $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$; part~(ii) holds for senders that follow the rule.
\item[(v)] The derivations of $\rcm$ and $\psi$ from $\mathsf{rseed}$ are
  unchecked, as for Orchard.
\item[(vi)] The witnessed $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ is checked only against the
  consumed leaf, through A1$'$ and A3$'$, and never against a record of
  issued Assets. Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}, in ``Security'', concerns the
  leaves.
\item[(vii)] The statement does not check that the prover owns the copied
  note of a Split Input beyond A6 and A7; this is the subject of
  Proposition~\ref{prop:split-ownership}.
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 226, ``Split Notes'' and ``Circuit Statement''.)
\end{remark}

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{The OrchardZSA Action proof}\label{sec:action-proof}

The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Knowledge soundness of the Action
proof'' and ``Zero knowledge of the Action proof'' name the relation
$\Rel_{\mathsf{Action}}$ and its verifying key. The corresponding
assumptions for $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ are stated here in the same
form; the \emph{OrchardZSA verifying key} denotes the verifying key of a
circuit for $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$, which no ZIP names.

\begin{assumption}[Knowledge soundness of the OrchardZSA Action proof]
\label{ass:zsa-ks}
Let the hash of the Fiat--Shamir transform be modelled as a random oracle.
For every efficient adversary that outputs primary inputs
$\mathsf{pub}_1, \dots, \mathsf{pub}_n$ and an aggregate proof $\pi$, there
is an efficient extractor, with access to the adversary and to its
random-oracle queries, that outputs auxiliary inputs
$\mathsf{aux}_1, \dots, \mathsf{aux}_n$ such that the probability of the
event
\begin{gather*}
  \text{$\pi$ is accepted for $(\mathsf{pub}_1, \dots, \mathsf{pub}_n)$
    under the OrchardZSA verifying key} \\
  \text{and } (\mathsf{pub}_i, \mathsf{aux}_i) \notin
    \Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}} \text{ for some } i
\end{gather*}
is negligible. This is the knowledge-soundness clause of the \emph{Crypto
Guide}'s Definition ``SNARK'' (\S``SNARKs: succinct non-interactive
arguments of knowledge'') for the relation of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}, for a Halo~2 proof (\emph{Halo 2 Guide},
\S``The Halo 2 proof system'' and \S``A rigorous treatment of knowledge
soundness''), the primary input bound to the proof as in the \emph{Halo 2
Guide}, \S``Instance columns and binding the public statement''. It
includes that every satisfying assignment of the circuit yields a witness
of $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$; in particular, the multiplication of A4$'$
is by the witnessed $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ and nothing else. No ZIP names the
circuit or the verifying key: the assumption concerns a designed but
unspecified object.
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[Zero knowledge of the OrchardZSA Action proof]
\label{ass:zsa-zk}
Let the hash of the Fiat--Shamir transform be modelled as a programmable
random oracle. There is an efficient simulator that, given only
$\mathsf{pub}_1, \dots, \mathsf{pub}_n$ and programming the random oracle,
outputs an aggregate proof statistically indistinguishable from an honest
proof under the OrchardZSA verifying key, for any
$\mathsf{aux}_1, \dots, \mathsf{aux}_n$ with
$(\mathsf{pub}_i, \mathsf{aux}_i) \in \Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ for every
$i$, against every distinguisher that makes polynomially many random-oracle
queries. This is zero knowledge of statistical grade in the
non-interactive, random-oracle form of the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Definition
``Zero knowledge'' (\S``The simulation paradigm and zero knowledge''), for
a Halo~2 proof (\emph{Halo 2 Guide}, \S``Zero knowledge: hiding the witness
in Halo 2''). It concerns the same designed but unspecified circuit and
key.
\end{assumption}

% Cited: 2720 + 2272 n bytes: ZIP 257 'Canonical Orchard Action proof length'
\begin{remark}[Circuit, verifying key and proof length]
\label{rem:zsa-proof-status}
The statement is specified at delta level (ZIP 226). Its arithmetisation
exists only in the document outside the ZIPs named in
Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-action-status}, so the circuit and its verifying key
are designed but unspecified, and Assumptions~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}
and~\ref{ass:zsa-zk} are the only hypotheses through which later results
depend on them. Condition A4$'$ is sound only if the circuit multiplies by
the witnessed $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ and nothing else. The Orchard
variable-base scalar multiplication was corrected at NU6.2 because it left
the base under-constrained (ZIP 257, ``NU6.2 deployment''). ZIP 226, which
turns the value base into a witness (``Value Commitment Correctness''),
does not mention the correction, which any OrchardZSA circuit must carry.
ZIP 257 made the Orchard proof length $2720 + 2272\,n$ bytes, for $n$
Actions, a consensus rule (``Canonical Orchard Action proof length''). No
ZIP fixes an OrchardZSA proof length, which the circuit determines and
which no live text contradicts: it is designed but unspecified, as the
circuit and the verifying key are.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Ownership of Split Inputs]\label{prop:split-ownership}
Consider the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Spend-authority
experiment'' with $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ in place of
$\Rel_{\mathsf{Action}}$: a key generated with
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$, the adversary holding its full viewing
key but not $\ask$. Under Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} and the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'',
``GroupHash as a random oracle'', ``The RedPallas hash as a random oracle''
and ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'', no efficient adversary outputs,
except with negligible probability, a block chain with an accepted Split
Action whose witness copies a leaf that is a note of the key, and whose
spend-authorisation signature is valid under its $\rk$ on a signature
digest for which it made no signing query with that $\rk$. Consequently the
sentence of ZIP 226, ``Split Notes'', ``we do not care about whether the
previously issued note copied to create a Split Input is owned by the
sender, or whether it was nullified before'' holds for spentness and not
for ownership: a sender can use choices~(2) and~(3) of
Remark~\ref{rem:copied-note} only with the spend authority of the copied
note, except for a note whose key is public, such as a reference note
(Proposition~\ref{prop:reference-copy}). Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here from specified
conditions; conditional on Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which concerns the
designed but unspecified circuit.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof is a reduction to the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Theorem ``Spend authority''. By Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} the extractor
yields, for the accepted Split Action, a witness of
$\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ with $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$. By C3,
$\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 0$, so A3$'$ gives $\mathit{root} = \mathit{rt}$
outside its $\bot$ case, which is excluded as in the consequences of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}; with the anchor rule, the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Proposition ``Membership soundness'' makes
$\Extract_{\PP}(\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}})$ a leaf. Condition A1$'$ with
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(b) makes the witnessed consumed note the note
of that leaf, so its witnessed expanded receiver
$(\gd^{\mathsf{old}}, \pkd^{\mathsf{old}})$ is the receiver of the leaf's
note, a note of the key; the witness therefore consumes a note of the key
in the sense of the experiment. Conditions A6 and A7, unchanged and
unconditional, then bind $\rk$ to a randomisation of $\pm\aksym^{\PP}$ of
that key, by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Binding of $\ivk$ to
$(\aksym, \nksym)$'' and Lemma ``Coordinate extraction identifies opposite
points'', and a valid signature under $\rk$ without a matching signing
query contradicts that volume's Proposition ``Unforgeability under
re-randomisation'', exactly as in steps~(1) to~(5) of the proof of Theorem
``Spend authority''. Neither $\mathsf{split\_flag}$ nor $v'$ enters A6 or
A7, the only conditions that proof uses; A1$'$ and membership serve here
only to identify the witnessed receiver with that of the copied leaf's
note, so the steps apply unchanged.

For spentness: by A4$'$ the copied value is not committed, and by
Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(i) the copied note's nullifier is not
published, except with negligible probability. Choice~(1) of
Remark~\ref{rem:copied-note} copies a note that the same bundle spends, so
its sender holds that note's spend authority in any case.
\end{proof}

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Action Groups}\label{sec:action-group}

\begin{definition}[Action Group]\label{def:action-group}
An \emph{Action Group} is a non-empty finite sequence of OrchardZSA Actions
together with one anchor $\mathit{rt}$, the flags $\mathsf{enableSpends}$,
$\mathsf{enableOutputs}$ and $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ in $\{0, 1\}$, and one
aggregate proof for the relation of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action} at the
primary inputs
\[
  \bigl(\mathit{rt},\, \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i,\, \nfsym_i,\, \rk_i,\,
    \cmx_i,\, \mathsf{enableSpends},\, \mathsf{enableOutputs},\,
    \mathsf{enableZSA}\bigr)
\]
of its Actions $i$. Each Action carries the public fields
$(\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}},\allowbreak \nfsym,\allowbreak \rk,\allowbreak
\cmx,\allowbreak \mathsf{epk},\allowbreak C_{\mathsf{enc}},\allowbreak
C_{\mathsf{out}})$ of an Orchard Action description (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
Definition ``Action description'') and its own spend-authorisation
signature under $\rk$. The term is that of ZIP 228, ``Terminology'':
Actions that share the tuple
\[
  (\mathit{rt}, \mathsf{enableSpends}, \mathsf{enableOutputs},
  \mathsf{enableZSA}, \mathsf{nAGExpiryHeight}).
\]
ZIP 227 requires $\mathsf{nAGExpiryHeight} = 0$ and at most one Action
Group per transaction (``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes''), so the
fifth component is constant and is omitted. The
nullifier $\nfsym_{0,0}$ of the first Action of the Action Group is the
value that issuance uses (ZIP 227, ``Computation of $\rho$''). The Action
Group has no burn set; the remaining components of a transaction's bundle
are defined in ``Burn sets and OrchardZSA bundles''
(\S\ref{sec:burn-set}). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

The structure is specified by ZIPs 226 to 228, all Draft. Its encoding
exists only in ZIP 230, which is Withdrawn; the layout that ZIP 230 calls
version~$6$ is not the version~$6$ of ZIP 229, which has no Action Groups
(ZIP 229, ``Transaction Format''). The byte lengths of the note ciphertext
and of an Action depend on a plaintext encoding that no current ZIP defines
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-plaintext}). The flag $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ has no
flag bit in any current format. ZIP 230 assigns it bit~$2$ of the Orchard
flags byte, which ZIP 229, ``Consensus Rules'', reserves as $0$ in
version~$6$ and which is $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$ in the Ironwood flags
byte (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Bundle flags''; ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules
from NU6.3 activation''). The design conflicts with live text, so the flag
bit is an open problem (Definition~\ref{def:classes}).

\section{Burn and the binding signature}\label{sec:burn}

This section defines the burn set and the OrchardZSA bundle that it
completes (\S\ref{sec:burn-set}); constructs the binding validating key,
which subtracts the burnt amounts Asset by Asset (\S\ref{sec:burn-bvk}); and
proves that a verifying binding signature forces per-Asset balance whenever
every witnessed Asset Base and every Asset Base of a burn pair is a
group-hash output at a suitably distinct input (\S\ref{sec:burn-balance}).
The premise on the bases concerns a single bundle; it is discharged for
valid chains in ``Security'' (\S\ref{sec:security}). The constructions are
taken from ZIP 226 and ZIP 227, both Draft. The Orchard binding signature
that they extend is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The binding
signature'', and is cited, not restated.

\subsection{Burn sets and OrchardZSA bundles}\label{sec:burn-set}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:burn-set] MAX_BURN_VALUE = 2^63 - 1 = 9223372036854775807, the
%   maximum of a signed 64-bit field [-9223372036854775808,
%   9223372036854775807]
% Cited: MAX_BURN_VALUE := 2^63 - 1; 0 < v <= MAX_BURN_VALUE: ZIP 226 'Burn
%   Mechanism', 'Additional Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set'; signed
%   64-bit valueBalance fields: ZIP 226 'Rationale for MAX_BURN_VALUE'
\begin{definition}[Burn set]\label{def:burn-set}
\emph{Burn} destroys a stated amount of a Custom Asset
(Definition~\ref{def:asset}): it sends nothing to any address, lowers the
amount of the Asset in circulation, and publishes the Asset Base and the
amount, so that the destruction is publicly verifiable. A \emph{burn set}
$\mathsf{assetBurn}$ is a finite set of pairs $(\mathsf{AssetBase}, v)$ with
$\mathsf{AssetBase} \in \Ep^{*}$ and $v \in \Z$. The author of a
transaction adds one pair for each Custom Asset that the transaction burns,
$v$ being the amount burnt. The constant
\[
  \mathsf{MAX\_BURN\_VALUE} := 2^{63} - 1 = 9\,223\,372\,036\,854\,775\,807
\]
bounds the amount of one Custom Asset burnt in one transaction, so that
every burnt amount is representable in a signed $64$-bit balance field. A
burn set is \emph{valid} when it satisfies the following rules.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(B1)] For every pair, $\mathsf{AssetBase} \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$:
  the Native Asset Base (Definition~\ref{def:native-base}), of ZEC and of
  TAZ alike, is never burnt.
\item[(B2)] For every pair, $0 < v \le \mathsf{MAX\_BURN\_VALUE}$.
\item[(B3)] Every Asset Base occurs in at most one pair. The \emph{burnt
  amount} $v_B$ of a point $B$ is therefore well defined: the $v$ of the
  pair with $\mathsf{AssetBase} = B$, or $0$ if there is none.
\end{enumerate}
Rules (B1) to (B3) constrain the pairs only. Rule (B1) excludes the one
point $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and no other, and no rule requires
$\mathsf{AssetBase}$ to be the Asset Base of an issued Asset. The state
rule that a burn cannot exceed the amount of the Asset in circulation is
ZIP 227's; it is rule (T4) of Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}, in
``State transition of a transaction'' (\S\ref{sec:state-rules}) (ZIP 226,
``Burn Mechanism'', ``Additional Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set'' and
``Rationale for MAX\_BURN\_VALUE''; ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus
Rule Changes''). Class: specified; the carrier of the burn set is stated in
``Transaction formats'' (\S\ref{sec:carrier-format}).
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[OrchardZSA bundle]\label{def:zsa-bundle}
The \emph{OrchardZSA bundle} of a transaction consists of at most one
Action Group (Definition~\ref{def:action-group}), one burn set
$\mathsf{assetBurn}$ satisfying (B1) to (B3), one balancing value
$v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ and one binding signature. It extends the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Bundle'' by the burn set; its anchor,
flags and proof are those of the Action Group. ZIP 226 places one burn set
in the transaction, and ZIP 227 limits the transaction to at most one
Action Group, so the two readings coincide (ZIP 226, ``Burn Mechanism'';
ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes''). Class: the structure
is specified; its encoding, one burn set per Action Group in withdrawn ZIP
230, belongs to the carrier and is classified in
Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[No transparent pool for Custom Assets]
\label{rem:no-transparent-asset}
ZIPs 226 and 227 leave the transparent protocol unchanged and single-asset.
Transparent inputs and outputs, and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s transparent
transaction value pool (Definition ``Transparent transaction value pool''),
carry the Native Asset only; Custom Assets have no transparent value pool
and cannot be unshielded by a transfer. ZIP 226 conditions this on future
consensus changes, which no ZIP specifies (ZIP 226, ``Burn Mechanism'',
note; ``Rationale for Value Balance Verification'': ``All Custom Assets are
contained within the shielded pool, and cannot be unshielded via a regular
transfer''). Structurally, the binding validating key of the next
subsection (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}) has, besides the Actions' net
value commitments, only the balancing value at $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and
one burn term per pair; a non-zero net amount of a Custom Asset in a bundle
can therefore be absorbed only by a burn pair of that Asset. The
computational statement is Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance}. Class:
specified.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The binding validating key}\label{sec:burn-bvk}

\begin{definition}[Binding validating key of an OrchardZSA bundle]
\label{def:zsa-bvk}
Let an OrchardZSA bundle have $n$ Actions with net value commitments
$\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_1, \dots, \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_n$
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-cv}), trapdoors $\rcv_1, \dots, \rcv_n$, balancing
value $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ and burn set $\mathsf{assetBurn}$. The
\emph{binding signing key} is unchanged:
\[
  \mathsf{bsk} := \rcv_1 + \dots + \rcv_n \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}
\]
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Binding signing key''). The verifier
computes, from public fields only, the \emph{binding validating key}
\[
  \mathsf{bvk} := \Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n} \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i\Bigr)
    - [v^{\mathsf{balance}}]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    - \sum_{(\mathsf{AssetBase}, v) \in \mathsf{assetBurn}}
      [v]\,\mathsf{AssetBase},
\]
where the subtracted points are the zero-trapdoor commitments
\[
  \mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{0}(V^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
    v^{\mathsf{balance}})
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \mathsf{ValueCommit}^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}_{0}(\mathsf{AssetBase}, v),
\]
each signed integer entering as its residue modulo $p_{\Vesta}$. Against
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Binding validating key'' two
things are new: the bases of the Actions' commitments vary, being the
witnessed Asset Bases, and one burn term is subtracted per pair.

The balancing value keeps the meaning of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definitions ``Transparent transaction value pool'' and ``Balancing value'':
the net flow of ZEC between the pool and the transparent transaction value
pool, a signed integer in $\{-2^{63}, \dots, 2^{63} - 1\}$. It enters
$\mathsf{bvk}$ at $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ only. Its meaning rests on the
key of ZIP 226; its encoding, the field \texttt{valueBalanceOrchard} of
withdrawn ZIP 230, belongs to the carrier.

The binding signature is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Binding signature'', the binding-signature instance of RedPallas on the
base $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The RedPallas
signature scheme''): the author signs $\mathsf{SigHash}$ with
$\mathsf{bsk}$, and the verifier MUST validate the signature on
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ under $\mathsf{bvk}$. Here $\mathsf{SigHash}$ is the
signature digest of the transaction (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition
``Signature digest'') as modified for OrchardZSA; the modifications and
their class are stated in ``Transaction digests and the signature message''
(\S\ref{sec:carrier-digest}, Remark~\ref{rem:sighash-status}) (ZIP 226,
``Value Balance Verification'' and ``Value Commitment''; protocol
specification, \S``Balance and Binding Signature (Orchard)''). Class:
specified; the signed message is not.
\end{definition}

For an honestly constructed bundle each $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ equals
\[
  [v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i]\,\mathsf{AssetBase}_i +
  [\rcv_i]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
by condition A4$'$ of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}. By the additive
homomorphism (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The Pedersen commitment'', Theorem
``Additive homomorphism''), the trapdoor terms sum to $[\mathsf{bsk}]\,
R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, and the value terms group by Asset Base. When the
net values of each Custom Asset sum to its burnt amount and those of ZEC to
$v^{\mathsf{balance}}$, every value term cancels, so
$\mathsf{bvk} = [\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, the pair
$(\mathsf{bsk}, \mathsf{bvk})$ is a key pair of the instance, and the
honest signature is accepted, as in the paragraph after the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Construction ``Binding signature''. In the notation of ZIP 226,
with $S_{\mathsf{CA}}$ the indices of the Actions of Custom Assets, a
correctly constructed bundle satisfies
\[
  \sum_{j \in S_{\mathsf{CA}}} \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_j
  - \sum_{(\mathsf{AssetBase}, v) \in \mathsf{assetBurn}}
    [v]\,\mathsf{AssetBase}
  = \Bigl[\sum_{j \in S_{\mathsf{CA}}} \rcv_j\Bigr]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
\]
(ZIP 226, ``Rationale for Value Balance Verification''). For a Split Action
$v' = 0$, so the value of the copied note never enters $\mathsf{bvk}$: the
commitment $[0 - v^{\mathsf{new}}]\,\mathsf{AssetBase} +
[\rcv]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ cancels against the value of the notes of the
same Asset consumed by real spends in other Actions (ZIP 226, ``Split
Notes'').

The key $\mathsf{bvk}$ is computed from the Actions' commitments, the
balancing value and the burn set, and of these only the burn set names an
Asset, by publishing each burnt Asset Base with its amount. The hiding of
