the Actions' Asset Bases is Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy}, in
``Security''.

\subsection{Per-Asset balance}\label{sec:burn-balance}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:burn-balance] Actions per transaction <= 2000000 / 32 = 62500
%   < 2^16 = 65536 (each Action carries its 32-byte nullifier)
% [sec:burn-balance] per-Asset |sum of (v' - v_new) - burnt amount|
%   <= (2^16 - 1)(2^64 - 1) + (2^63 - 1) = 1208916596242592319864832
% [sec:burn-balance] ZEC: |sum - balancing value| <= (2^16 - 1)(2^64 - 1)
%   + 2^63 = 1208916596242592319864833 (the upper end of the spec's range
%   in 'Balance and Binding Signature (Orchard)')
% [sec:burn-balance] both < 2^80 < 2^253 <= (p_Vesta - 1)/2 (about
%   2^253.00), so a sum congruent to 0 modulo p_Vesta is 0 in the integers
% Cited: block size <= 2000000 bytes: spec 'Block Header Encoding and
%   Consensus'; 32-byte nullifier per Action: spec 'Action Description
%   Encoding and Consensus'
\begin{lemma}[Modular per-Asset balance is integer balance]
\label{lem:zsa-integer}
Let $n \le 62\,500$. For $1 \le i \le n$ let $v'_i$ and $v^{\mathsf{new}}_i$
be integers in $\{0, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\}$ and $\mathsf{AssetBase}_i$ a
point. Let $\mathsf{assetBurn}$ satisfy (B2) and (B3), with burnt amounts
$v_B$, and let $v^{\mathsf{balance}}$ be an integer in
$\{-2^{63}, \dots, 2^{63} - 1\}$. For a point $B$ put
\begin{align*}
  c_B &:= \sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = B}
    (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) - v_B
    && (B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}), \\
  c_{V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}} &:= \sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i =
    V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}} (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i)
    - v^{\mathsf{balance}},
\end{align*}
rule (B1) giving $v_{V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}} = 0$ for a valid burn set. If
$c_B \equiv 0 \pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$, then $c_B = 0$ as an integer.

The hypotheses hold for every OrchardZSA bundle of a valid block. Every
Action carries its own $32$-byte nullifier (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
Definition ``Action description''; protocol specification, \S``Action
Description Encoding and Consensus''), and a block, hence a transaction,
holds at most $2\,000\,000$ bytes (protocol specification, \S``Block Header
Encoding and Consensus''), so
\[
  n \le 2\,000\,000 / 32 = 62\,500 < 2^{16}
\]
whatever the carrier. The values are $64$-bit by
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-note}, with $v' \in \{0, v^{\mathsf{old}}\}$ by the
notation of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}; burnt amounts are bounded by
(B2), and the balancing value by Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}. No
carrier-specific bound on the number of Actions is needed. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here; conditional on no
unspecified object.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The argument is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Modular balance
is integer balance''. Each difference $v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i$ has
absolute value at most $2^{64} - 1$, and $n \le 2^{16} - 1$. For
$B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, rule (B2) gives $0 \le v_B \le 2^{63} - 1$,
so
\[
  |c_B| \le (2^{16} - 1)(2^{64} - 1) + (2^{63} - 1)
  = 1\,208\,916\,596\,242\,592\,319\,864\,832;
\]
for $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, with $|v^{\mathsf{balance}}| \le 2^{63}$,
\[
  |c_{V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}}| \le (2^{16} - 1)(2^{64} - 1) + 2^{63}
  = 1\,208\,916\,596\,242\,592\,319\,864\,833,
\]
the upper end of the range in the protocol specification, \S``Balance and
Binding Signature (Orchard)''. Both bounds are below
$2^{80} < 2^{253} \le (p_{\Vesta} - 1)/2$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Fields, groups, and encodings''; protocol specification, \S``Pallas and
Vesta''). The hypothesis makes $c_B$ a multiple of $p_{\Vesta}$, the order
of the Pallas group, and the only such multiple of absolute value below
$p_{\Vesta}$ is $0$.
\end{proof}

The protocol specification's argument in \S``Balance and Binding Signature
(Orchard)'' bounds the number of Actions by $2^{16} - 1$ through a separate
consensus rule, which it notes is redundant given the $2$\,MB transaction
size limit; the lemma uses the block size itself.

Fix an OrchardZSA bundle as in Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}, and let
$\mathfrak{B}$ be the set of points consisting of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
the Asset Bases of the Actions and the Asset Bases of the burn pairs. An
\emph{opening} of $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ is a triple $(B_i, u_i, r_i)$
of a point $B_i \in \mathfrak{B}$, an integer $u_i$ and a scalar
$r_i \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ with
\[
  \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i = [u_i \bmod p_{\Vesta}]\,B_i
    + [r_i]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}.
\]
For openings, $c_B$ is defined for $B \in \mathfrak{B}$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-integer}, with $B_i$ in place of $\mathsf{AssetBase}_i$
and $u_i$ in place of $v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i$. The set $\mathfrak{B}$
meets the \emph{premise on the bases} when every $B \in \mathfrak{B}$ other
than $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is given with an input $x_B$ of $\GroupHash$
such that $B = \GroupHash(x_B)$ and $x_B$ differs from the inputs
$(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv}, \texttt{v})$ of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and
$(\texttt{z.cash:Orchard-cv}, \texttt{r})$ of $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. As in
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, a \emph{binding signature} under a point $X$ is
a signature valid under $X$ in the binding-signature instance of RedPallas.

\begin{proposition}[Per-Asset balance and key knowledge]
\label{prop:zsa-balance}
For the bundle fixed above:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] Identity. If $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i =
  [v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i]\,\mathsf{AssetBase}_i +
  [\rcv_i]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ for every $i$, and
  $\mathsf{bsk} := \sum_i \rcv_i$, then
  \[
    \mathsf{bvk} - [\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    = \sum_{B \in \mathfrak{B}} [c_B]\,B;
  \]
  hence $c_B \equiv 0 \pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$ for every $B \in \mathfrak{B}$
  implies $\mathsf{bvk} = [\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$.
\item[(ii)] Knowledge. Given openings of every $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$
  and $b \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ with $\mathsf{bvk} = [b]\,
  R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
  \[
    \sum_{B \in \mathfrak{B}} [c_B]\,B
    + \Bigl[\sum_{i=1}^{n} r_i - b\Bigr]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    = \mathcal{O}.
  \]
  If $\mathfrak{B}$ meets the premise on the bases and some $c_B$ is
  non-zero modulo $p_{\Vesta}$, this is a non-trivial relation among
  values of $\GroupHash$ at distinct inputs.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Discrete
logarithms on Pallas'', ``GroupHash as a random oracle'' and ``The
RedPallas hash as a random oracle'', an efficient party that outputs a
bundle whose set $\mathfrak{B}$ meets the premise on the bases, openings of
every net value commitment and a binding signature under the resulting
$\mathsf{bvk}$ on a message of its choice satisfies
$c_B \equiv 0 \pmod{p_{\Vesta}}$ for every $B \in \mathfrak{B}$, and knows
$b = \sum_i r_i$, except with negligible probability. The statement holds
for every message and does not depend on the signed message.

Under Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} in addition, let an efficient adversary
output OrchardZSA bundles of valid blocks, hence with at most $62\,500$
Actions each, whose Action proofs and binding signatures verify
and whose witnessed and burn-pair Asset Bases meet the premise on the
bases. Then, except with negligible probability, as integers:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] for every Asset Base $B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
  $\sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = B} (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) =
  v_B$, which is $0$ when $B$ is not burnt;
\item[(b)] $\sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}}
  (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) = v^{\mathsf{balance}}$;
\item[(c)] no burn pair has an Asset Base that no Action witnesses;
\item[(d)] the signer knows the trapdoor sum $\sum_i \rcv_i$.
\end{enumerate}
Here $\mathsf{AssetBase}_i$, $v'_i$, $v^{\mathsf{new}}_i$ and $\rcv_i$ are
read from the extracted witnesses. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified
$\mathsf{bvk}$; conditional on Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which concerns
the designed but unspecified circuit, and not on
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}. The premise on the bases is discharged
for every accepted bundle of a valid chain only in ``Security'', in
Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}: for witnessed bases by
Corollary~\ref{cor:action-bases}, for burn-pair bases by rule (T4) of
Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} and Lemma~\ref{lem:state-keys}.
ZIP 226 argues per-Asset balance in rationale only (``Rationale for Value
Balance Verification''), and that argument stays designed but unspecified.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i)~Expand each $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ and apply the additive
homomorphism (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The Pedersen commitment'', Theorem
``Additive homomorphism''):
\[
  \sum_{i=1}^{n} \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i
  = \sum_{B \in \mathfrak{B}} \Bigl[\sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = B}
    (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i)\Bigr]\,B
    + [\mathsf{bsk}]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}.
\]
By (B3) each Asset Base of a burn pair occurs in exactly one subtracted
term $[v_B]\,B$, and by (B1) none of them is $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, whose
only subtracted term is $[v^{\mathsf{balance}}]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$.
Subtracting these terms as in Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk} and grouping by
base gives the identity. If every $c_B$ vanishes modulo $p_{\Vesta}$, the
right-hand side is $\mathcal{O}$.

(ii)~With the openings in place of the honest values, the same grouping
gives $\mathsf{bvk} = \sum_{B} [c_B]\,B + [\sum_i r_i]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$;
equating with $[b]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ gives the relation. Its
coefficients are scalars modulo $p_{\Vesta}$. Under the premise, the base
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is the value at its input, and each other
$B \in \mathfrak{B}$ at $x_B$. The points of $\mathfrak{B}$ are pairwise
distinct, so the chosen inputs are pairwise distinct, one per point; two
distinct inputs with one value would themselves be a relation of the same
kind. The inputs differ from that of $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, by the premise
and since the inputs of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$
differ in the message. A non-zero $c_B$ makes the relation non-trivial.

Consequence. Let an efficient party $\Adv$ output, with probability
$\epsilon$, a bundle meeting the premise, openings, a message and a binding
signature on it under the resulting $\mathsf{bvk}$, with some $c_B$
non-zero modulo $p_{\Vesta}$; the event is efficiently decidable from the
output. The value $b$ is extracted exactly as in the proof of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Balance and key knowledge'': if
$\mathsf{bvk} = \mathcal{O}$, then $b := 0$; otherwise special soundness
with forking in the random-oracle model for the RedPallas hash yields $b$
with $\mathsf{bvk} = [b]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (\emph{Crypto Guide},
\S``Binding signatures as a proof of knowledge of a discrete logarithm'',
Theorem ``The binding signature is a proof of knowledge of $\log_P B$'').
Key prefixing places $\mathsf{bvk}$ in the critical query, so the openings
and the bases of the first run satisfy the hypothesis of part~(ii) for it.
Part~(ii) then outputs a non-trivial relation among values of $\GroupHash$
at distinct inputs, which the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Relations
among fixed generators'' excludes except with negligible probability; so
$\epsilon$ is negligible. In the model of that volume's Assumption
``GroupHash as a random oracle'' the inputs $x_B$ may equally be read from
the queries of $\Adv$: a point that equals the value at an input $\Adv$
never queried occurs with probability at most $1/p_{\Vesta}$ for each
point. For Asset Bases of Custom Assets, Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct}
places every input under \texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA} apart from those of
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and $R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. When every $c_B$
vanishes, the relation reduces to
$[\sum_i r_i - b]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = \mathcal{O}$, and
$R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ has prime order $p_{\Vesta}$, so $b = \sum_i r_i$.

Bundles. The adversary outputs polynomially many bundles, and the union
bound reduces the claim to one of them, as in the proof of the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Theorem ``Balance''. The algorithm that runs the
adversary with the extractor of Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} outputs, for
that bundle, the triples $(\mathsf{AssetBase}_i,\,
v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i,\, \rcv_i)$ read from the witnesses; by A4$'$ of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action} each is an opening of
$\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$, except with the negligible probability that a
witness fails the relation. The bundle's binding signature verifies on
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ under $\mathsf{bvk}$, so the algorithm is a party of the
consequence, and every $c_B$ vanishes modulo $p_{\Vesta}$ and
$b = \sum_i \rcv_i$, which is~(d). The hypotheses of
Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-integer} hold for the witnesses, the bundle being in a
valid block, and the lemma lifts
each congruence to an equality of integers, which is~(a) and~(b). A burn
pair of base $B$ that no Action witnesses has $c_B = -v_B$, with
$0 < v_B < p_{\Vesta}$ by (B2), so $c_B \not\equiv 0$; this is~(c).
\end{proof}

The premise on the bases cannot be dropped, for witnessed bases or for
the bases of burn pairs. For witnessed bases this is
Proposition~\ref{prop:dummy-attack}. For burn pairs, let $B$ be the Asset
Base of an issued Asset and $0 < v \le \mathsf{MAX\_BURN\_VALUE}$. Split
Actions of base $B$ contribute $v' = 0$, so Actions of base $B$ whose net
values sum to $-2v$ create $2v$ units of $B$ from nothing. The pair
$([-2]\,B, v)$ satisfies (B2) and (B3), and (B1) unless
$[-2]\,B = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, a relation that Lemma ``Relations among
fixed generators'' of the \emph{Ironwood Guide} excludes except with
negligible probability. With balancing value $0$ and $t :=
\sum_i \rcv_i$, Definitions~\ref{def:zsa-cv} and~\ref{def:zsa-bvk} give
\[
  \mathsf{bvk} = [-2v]\,B + [t]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    - [v]\,\bigl([-2]\,B\bigr)
  = [t]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
whose discrete logarithm the author knows, so the binding signature
verifies. Likewise, with balancing value $0$, ZEC Actions whose net values
sum to $-v$ and the pair $([-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}, v)$ give
\[
  \mathsf{bvk} = [-v]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + [t]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}
    - [v]\,\bigl([-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}\bigr)
  = [t]\,R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}:
\]
an amount of $v$ zatoshi of ZEC appears from nothing, and (B1) does not
exclude the pair, since $[-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \neq
V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ in a group of odd order. Rule (B1) excludes only
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$; the premise on burn-pair bases is discharged by
the issuance state, through rule (T4) of
Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} and Lemma~\ref{lem:state-keys}, not
by the burn rules (ZIP 226, ``Rationale for Split Notes'', on inputs that
are a multiple or linear combination of an existing Asset Base; ZIP 226,
``Additional Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set'').

\begin{remark}[Homomorphism across Asset Bases]\label{rem:zip-iff}
ZIP 226, ``Rationale for Value Balance Verification'', states that the
relation between $\mathsf{bvk}$ and $\mathsf{bsk}$ holds ``if and only
if'', per Custom Asset, the net values sum to the burnt amount ($0$ if
absent) and, for ZEC, to the balancing value, and that the commitments
``add up homomorphically only with respect to the same value base point''.
Neither is a literal statement about the Pallas group. The group is cyclic
of prime order $p_{\Vesta}$ (protocol specification, \S``Pallas and
Vesta''), so any two non-identity bases satisfy $B' = [\lambda]\,B$ for
some $\lambda$; commitments to distinct bases add homomorphically as group
elements; and $\sum_B [c_B]\,B = \mathcal{O}$ has non-zero solutions
$(c_B)$. The ``if'' direction is part~(i) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance}. The ``only if'' direction holds
computationally, for group-hash bases and an efficient signer, as part~(ii)
and its consequence prove, and then as integers by
Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-integer}. The argument of the ZIP is rationale and keeps
its class (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}).
\end{remark}

\section{Issuance of Custom Assets}\label{sec:issue}

This section constructs the objects of ZIP 227 that create value of a
Custom Asset: the Issue Note and the rule by which validators compute its
commitment (\S\ref{sec:issue-note}); the Issuance Action and the Issuance
Bundle that carry Issue Notes (\S\ref{sec:issue-bundle}); the element $\rho$
of an Issue Note, derived from the first nullifier of the Action Group
(\S\ref{sec:issue-rho}); the reference note (\S\ref{sec:issue-ref}); and the
rules and the digest hypothesis that bind an Issuance Bundle to its issuer
and to one transaction (\S\ref{sec:issue-auth}). Issuance is public. The one
privacy property that it keeps, the unlinkability of a later spend of an
Issue Note, is proved in \S\ref{sec:issue-note}. The rules that update the
global issuance state are those of ``The global issuance state''
(\S\ref{sec:state}). Every construction of the section is taken from ZIP
227, which is Draft, together with the objects of ZIP 226 that it uses.

\subsection{Issue Notes}\label{sec:issue-note}

\begin{definition}[Issue Note]\label{def:issue-note}
An \emph{Issue Note} represents the issuance of a value $v$ of one Custom
Asset to one recipient. It is a tuple
\[
  (d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})
\]
of type
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{Note}^{\mathsf{Issue}} := {}&
    \{0,1\}^{88} \times \bigl(\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}}) \setminus
    \{\mathcal{O}\}\bigr) \times \{0, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\} \\
  &\times \Ep^{*} \times \F_{p_{\Pallas}} \times
    \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]},
\end{align*}
with the following components.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The pair $(d, \pkd)$ is the recipient's address, of the type of the
  \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Diversified address'': the
  diversifier $d$ and the diversified transmission key $\pkd$, a point
  other than the identity (ZIP 227 types it in
  $\mathsf{KA}^{\mathsf{Orchard}}.\mathsf{Public}$, protocol specification,
  \S``Orchard Key Components'').
\item The value $v$ counts units of the Asset. The lengths $88$ and $64$
  are the constants $\ell_{\mathsf{d}}$ and $\ell_{\mathsf{value}}$ of the
  protocol specification, \S``Constants''.
\item The point $\mathsf{AssetBase} \in \Ep^{*}$ is the Asset Base of the
  Custom Asset issued (Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}).
\item The element $\rho$ is fixed by Construction~\ref{def:issued-rho}, in
  ``Computation of $\rho$ for Issue Notes'' (\S\ref{sec:issue-rho}).
\item The note seed $\mathsf{rseed}$ is a $32$-byte string that the issuer
  MUST sample uniformly at random.
\end{enumerate}
An Issue Note carries no $\psi$ and no $\rcm$; both are to be derived from
$\mathsf{rseed}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}). ZIP 227 writes the fourth
and fifth factors of the type as $\PP^{*}$ and $\F_{q_{\PP}}$
(Table~\ref{tab:notation}; ZIP 227, ``Issue Note''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\emph{Commitments of Issue Notes.} No note commitment of an Issue Note is
transmitted. Every validator MUST compute the commitment $\cmsym$ of each
Issue Note by $\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}$
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}), exactly as for an OrchardZSA note
with the same fields, and, when the transaction is included in the block
chain, append its extracted form $\cmx$ to the note commitment tree after
the commitments of the Action Group's Actions. The order is
Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}, in ``State transition of a transaction''
(\S\ref{sec:state-rules}), and the tree is the one that
Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise} leaves open (ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'',
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'' and ``Addition to the Note
Commitment Tree''). Class: specified; its evaluability is qualified by
Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}.

\begin{remark}[Commitment inputs of an Issue Note]\label{rem:issue-cm-open}
The OrchardZSA note of an Issue Note
$(d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})$ is
$(d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \psi, \rcm)$, with $\psi$ and $\rcm$
derived from $\mathsf{rseed}$ as in Definition~\ref{def:zsa-seed}. With
$\underline{\rho} := \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\rho)$ and
$\gd := \mathsf{DiversifyHash}(d)$,
\begin{align*}
  \psi &:= \mathrm{ToBase}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(
    [\mathtt{0x09}] \,\|\, \underline{\rho})\bigr), \\
  \rcm &:= \mathsf{Derive\_rcm}_{\mathsf{rseed}}(\mathsf{leadByte},
    \gd^{\star}, \pkd^{\star}, v, \underline{\rho}, \psi).
\end{align*}
The derivation of $\psi$ does not depend on the lead byte and is specified.
The derivation of $\rcm$ is designed but unspecified, and the value of its
lead byte is an open problem (Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}). An Issue
Note has no plaintext that could carry a lead byte. Three consequences
follow.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Until the value is assigned, the commitment that every validator MUST
  compute is not computable from live text. The gap is at consensus level.
  For the created note of a transfer the same gap binds only sender and
  recipient, since consensus sees only $\cmx$.
\item The recipient needs the same $\rcm$ to open the commitment when it
  spends the note, in condition A1$'$ of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}.
\item The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Hiding under the derived
  trapdoor'' does not transfer to Issue Notes. Hiding plays no role for
  them: every field, $\mathsf{rseed}$ included, is public
  (Remark~\ref{rem:public-issuance}).
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'' and ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes''; ZIP
226, ``Note Structure and Commitment''; ZIP 2005, ``Changes to the Protocol
Specification'', amending the protocol specification's \S``Sending Notes
(Orchard)''.)
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Commitment $\bot$ for an Issue Note]
The commitment $\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}$ may return $\bot$. For a
note created by an Action, the sender repeats the derivation with a new
$\mathsf{rseed}$ when the extracted commitment is $\bot$ (protocol
specification, \S``Sending Notes (Orchard)''). ZIP 227 states no rule for
an Issue Note whose commitment is $\bot$, and no text proposes one: the
consequence for the transaction is an open problem. An Issue Note is in the
custom branch of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}, its Asset Base being
a derived one (Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation}), where the
commitment is $\bot$ only if the hash point is; for uniform
$\mathsf{rseed}$ the event therefore has negligible probability, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(c) (ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'' and
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'').
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Unlinkability of spends of Issue Notes]
\label{prop:issue-unlink}
Let a key be generated with $\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$ from a
uniform spending key, with nullifier deriving key $\nksym$. Consider the
game of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Nullifier
unlinkability'', modified as follows. The adversary, which receives
$(\dk, \ivk)$ and $\aksym^{\PP}$ but not $\nksym$, submits adaptively Issue
Notes $n_i$ of its choice, in particular to addresses of the key, each
published in full as issuance publishes it, together with elements
$\psi_i \in \F_{p_{\Pallas}}$ and $\rcm_i \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$ of its
choice, such that the elements $\rho_i$ are pairwise distinct and the
OrchardZSA commitments $\cmsym_i$ of the notes
$(d_i, \mathsf{pk}_{\mathsf{d},i}, v_i, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i, \rho_i,
\psi_i, \rcm_i)$ are not $\bot$. In the real game it receives the nullifier
$\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho_i, \psi_i, \cmsym_i)$ that a later
spend of the note publishes by condition A5$'$ with
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$; in the ideal game it receives
$\Extract_{\PP}([u_i]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + \cmsym_i)$ for independent
uniform $u_i \in \F_{p_{\Vesta}}$. Then its distinguishing advantage is at
most the bound of the cited proposition, which is negligible under the
assumptions that proposition names, the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption
``Poseidon is a PRF'' among them.

The ideal answers are independent of the published notes when
$K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} \neq \mathcal{O}$, so a spend's nullifier does not
link it to its issuance for any party without $\nksym$, the issuer
included. The statement holds for every $\psi_i$ and every $\rcm_i$, in
particular for the derived $\psi_i$ and for any value that a future lead
byte gives $\rcm_i$; it is therefore independent of the open lead byte.
It concerns the nullifier only; the other published components of the spending
Action are treated in ``Hiding of the Asset
type'' (\S\ref{sec:security-privacy}). It fails for the reference note
(Definition~\ref{def:reference-note}), whose recipient key is the all-zero
spending key: its $\nksym$ is public, and its nullifier is computable by
anyone. Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the
specified construction; conditional on the assumptions of the cited
proposition. ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'', states the property as rationale.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Nullifier
unlinkability''. Its game already lets the adversary choose every field of
every submitted note, so publishing an Issue Note in full, $\mathsf{rseed}$
included, gives the adversary nothing that it does not hold. Its hybrids
use a submitted note only through the triple $(\rho_i, \psi_i, \cmsym_i)$,
with $\cmsym_i$ a point other than $\bot$ and the $\rho_i$ pairwise
distinct: the hops $H_1$ to $H_3$ change the key material only, the hop
$H_4$ uses the distinctness of the $\rho_i$ for the lazily sampled random
function, and the hop $H_5$ and the final statement use $\cmsym_i$ only as
a point to which $[s_i]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ or
$[u_i]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ is added. The nullifier of an OrchardZSA note
is $\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}$ of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition
``Nullifier'' with the OrchardZSA commitment in place of the Orchard one
(ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and Commitment''; \S\ref{sec:action-nf}). Every
hybrid and every bound therefore carries over verbatim. The hypothesis
that the $\rho_i$ are pairwise distinct is supplied, for the Issue Notes of
a valid chain, by Proposition~\ref{prop:issued-rho-unique}, except with
negligible probability.

For the reference note, $\nksym := \mathrm{ToBase}(
\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{sk}}([\mathtt{0x07}]))$ with $\mathsf{sk}$ the
all-zero string (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The spending key and the
spend-side secrets'') is a public constant, so the real answer is
computable from public data and differs from the ideal one.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Issuance Actions and Issuance Bundles}\label{sec:issue-bundle}

ZIP 227 specifies issuance through three components: the Issuance Bundle of
a transaction, in which a publicly named issuer creates Issue Notes; the
flag $\mathsf{finalize}$ of each Issuance Action, which, once set, ends
issuance of its Asset permanently; and the global issuance state kept by
every node, constructed in ``The global issuance state''
(\S\ref{sec:state}).

\begin{definition}[Issuance Action and Issuance Bundle]\label{def:issue-bundle}
Let
\[
  \mathsf{IssueNoteFields} := \{0,1\}^{88} \times \bigl(\Ep(\F_{p_{\Pallas}})
    \setminus \{\mathcal{O}\}\bigr) \times \{0, \dots, 2^{64} - 1\} \times
    \F_{p_{\Pallas}} \times \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]},
\]
the fields $(d, \pkd, v, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})$ of an Issue Note without
its Asset Base.
\begin{enumerate}
\item An \emph{Issuance Action} is a triple
  \[
    (\mathsf{assetDescHash}, \mathsf{vNotes}, \mathsf{flagsIssuance}),
  \]
  where the hash
  $\mathsf{assetDescHash} \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[32]}$ is the
  description hash of Definition~\ref{def:assetid}; $\mathsf{vNotes}$ is a
  finite sequence of elements of $\mathsf{IssueNoteFields}$, the unencrypted
  Issue Notes of the Asset; and $\mathsf{flagsIssuance}$ is a byte that
  carries the Boolean $\mathsf{finalize}$, which the issuer sets to signal
  that no further issuance of the Asset will occur.
\item An \emph{Issuance Bundle} is a triple
  \[
    (\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{vIssueActions}, \mathsf{issueAuthSig}),
  \]
  where
  $\mathsf{issuer} = \mathsf{ik\_encoding} \in \mathbb{B}^{\mathbb{Y}[33]}$
  is the issuer identifier of Definition~\ref{def:issuer};
  $\mathsf{vIssueActions}$ is a finite sequence of Issuance Actions; and
  $\mathsf{issueAuthSig} = [\mathtt{0x00}] \,\|\, \sigma$ encodes a
  signature $\sigma$ by $\mathsf{isk}$ on the transaction's
  $\mathsf{SigHash}$ (Definition~\ref{def:issue-auth}).
\end{enumerate}
The pair $(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash})$ is an Asset
Identifier and determines $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ by
Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}. With it, each element
$(d, \pkd, v, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})$ of $\mathsf{vNotes}$ determines the
Issue Note $(d, \pkd, v, \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})$ of
Definition~\ref{def:issue-note} completely. The commitment is omitted,
because every validator recomputes it (ZIP 227, ``Issuance Action'',
``Issuance Bundle'', ``Issue Note'' and ``Issuance Protocol''). Class:
specified.
\end{definition}

\emph{Encoding status.} The byte encoding of the Issuance Bundle and of the
Issuance Action exists only in withdrawn ZIP 230 and is designed but
unspecified; it is not the version-$6$ format of ZIP 229
(Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}; ZIP 227, ``Issuance Action'' and
``Issuance Bundle'').

\emph{Issuance Actions without notes.} The rules of ZIP 227 place no
requirement on the number of Issue Notes of an Issuance Action. Withdrawn
ZIP 230 admits an Issuance Action with none, so that an issuer can finalise
an issued Asset without issuing more of it. The validity of such an action
for an Asset without an entry in the issuance state is the open case of
Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action}, in ``State transition of a transaction''
(\S\ref{sec:state-rules}) (ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule
Changes''; ZIP 230, ``Issuance Action Description'').

\begin{remark}[Public issuance]\label{rem:public-issuance}
ZIP 227 enables only public issuance, so that the supply of each Asset can
be tracked (ZIP 227, ``Motivation''; ``Requirements'': the issuance
mechanism should enable public tracking of the supply). The Issue Notes are
unencrypted. The issuer, the Asset (through $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$, from
which anyone recomputes $\mathsf{AssetBase}$), every value, every recipient
address, every $\rho$ and $\mathsf{rseed}$, and every flag
$\mathsf{finalize}$ are public, so issuance publicly links each recipient
address to an Asset and an amount. Since $\mathsf{rseed}$ is public, so is
$\psi$, and so, once the lead byte is assigned, are the commitment and the
leaf of each Issue Note. With the public burn set
(Definition~\ref{def:burn-set}), the amount of each Asset in circulation,
issued less burnt, is a public function of the chain, which ZIP 227 records
as the balance of the global issuance state (``Global Issuance State''). The
later use of an Issue Note remains private, to the extent of
Proposition~\ref{prop:issue-unlink}. In this volume issuance is called
public and Issue Notes unencrypted (ZIP 227, ``Issuance Action'').
\end{remark}

\subsection{Computation of \texorpdfstring{$\rho$}{rho} for Issue Notes}
\label{sec:issue-rho}

An Issue Note is created outside any Action, so the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Construction ``Chaining rule'' (\S``Nullifier chaining'') gives it no
$\rho$.

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:issue-rho] indices i_A, i_N in {0 .. 2^32 - 1}, each encoded as
%   I2LEOSP_32 (4 bytes); expansion input [0x84] || I2LEOSP_32(i_A)
%   || I2LEOSP_32(i_N): 9 bytes
% Cited: 32-bit indices and a 32-byte nf key: ZIP 227 'Computation of rho';
%   PRF^expand = BLAKE2b-512 under Zcash_ExpandSeed: spec 'Pseudo Random
%   Functions'
\begin{construction}[Derivation of issued rho values]\label{def:issued-rho}
Define
\[
  \mathsf{DeriveIssuedRho} \colon \F_{p_{\Pallas}} \times
    \{0, \dots, 2^{32} - 1\} \times \{0, \dots, 2^{32} - 1\}
    \to \F_{p_{\Pallas}}
\]
by
\begin{align*}
  &\mathsf{DeriveIssuedRho}(\nfsym, i_A, i_N) \\
  &\quad := \mathrm{ToBase}\bigl(\mathsf{PRFexpand}_{\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}
    (\nfsym)}([\mathtt{0x84}] \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i_A) \,\|\,
    \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i_N))\bigr),
\end{align*}
with $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Expansion function'' and $\mathrm{ToBase}$ its Definition ``Field
reductions''. The expansion input after the key has $1 + 4 + 4 = 9$ bytes.
The element $\rho$ of the Issue Note at zero-based position $i_N$ of the
Issuance Action at zero-based position $i_A$ of the Issuance Bundle is
\[
  \rho := \mathsf{DeriveIssuedRho}(\nfsym_{0,0}, i_A, i_N),
\]
where $\nfsym_{0,0}$ is the nullifier published by the first Action of the
transaction's Action Group (Definition~\ref{def:action-group}), whatever
the kind of its consumed note. The definition presupposes an Action Group,
which rule (I1) of Definition~\ref{def:issue-auth} enforces. The derivation
adds one row to the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Domain bytes of
PRF expansion'':
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llllll@{}}
\toprule
$b$ & Key $\kappa$ & Remainder $t'$ & $f$ & Output & Section \\
\midrule
$\mathtt{0x84}$ & $\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nfsym_{0,0})$
  & $\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i_A) \,\|\, \mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{32}(i_N)$
  & $\mathrm{ToBase}$ & $\rho$ & \S\ref{sec:issue-rho} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The key is a public nullifier. No other ZIP and no section of the protocol
specification uses the byte $\mathtt{0x84}$, and no registry of a lower
volume lists it. Because the key is public, neither the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'' nor its Lemma
``Independence of domain-separated expansions'' applies (ZIP 227,
``Computation of $\rho$''; protocol specification,
\href{https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\#concreteprfs}{\S``Pseudo
Random Functions''} and \S``Orchard Key Components''). Class: specified.
\end{construction}

\begin{assumption}[BLAKE2b-512 as a random oracle]\label{ass:blake2b512-ro}
For each fixed $16$-byte personalisation $P$, the function
$x \mapsto \mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(P, x)$ is a random oracle with
$512$-bit output in the sense of the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s Definition
``Random oracle'' (\S``The random oracle model''), independent of the
oracles of the other personalisations. It is used for the personalisation
\texttt{Zcash\_ExpandSeed} of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$, where the key of
Construction~\ref{def:issued-rho} is public. For a secret uniform key and
an adversary that makes polynomially many oracle queries, it implies the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion''.
Its uses in this volume are Proposition~\ref{prop:issued-rho-unique} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}, whose proof programs one query of the
oracle.
\end{assumption}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:issue-rho] N + M values uniform on F_p_Pallas collide with
%   probability at most (N + M)(N + M - 1)/(2 p_Pallas) <= (N + M)^2/p_Pallas;
%   each ToBase output adds statistical distance at most p_Pallas/2^512
%   < 2^-257 (about 2^-258.00)
% Cited: birthday bound: Math Guide 'The union bound and a birthday
%   calculation'; ToBase distance m/2^L: Math Guide 'Uniform sampling and
%   the bias of modular reduction'
\begin{proposition}[Uniqueness of issued rho values]
\label{prop:issued-rho-unique}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] The expansion inputs, key and remainder, of the Issue Notes of a
  valid chain are pairwise distinct, and each differs from the input of
  every other row of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$.
\item[(b)] Under Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-ro}, consider an execution
  in which at most $q$ queries in total are made to the oracle of
  personalisation \texttt{Zcash\_ExpandSeed}. The probability that two
  Issue Notes of the chain have equal $\rho$, or that the $\rho$ of an
  Issue Note equals one of $M$ elements $\rho$ of notes created by Actions
  and fixed before the query that yields it, is at most
  \[
    \Bigl(\frac{q(q-1)}{2} + qM\Bigr)\Bigl(\frac{1}{p_{\Pallas}}
      + 2^{-512}\Bigr) \le \frac{(q + M)^2}{p_{\Pallas}}.
  \]
  With no query beyond the $N$ issued values, $q = N$.
\end{enumerate}
Literal uniqueness is not claimed, nor distinctness from the $\rho$ of
notes created by later Actions, which the derivation does not control.
Distinct OrchardZSA notes have distinct commitments by
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(b). The proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Proposition ``Nullifier uniqueness'' then applies with the hash base and
message of each note's branch of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}, which
unfold as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(i). So distinct
notes have distinct nullifiers whatever their $\rho$, and a collision can
affect only notes equal in every field. The proposition
extends to Issue Notes part~(a) of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Corollary
``Faerie Gold resistance'', which covers only notes
created by Actions. Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved
here for the specified derivation; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-ro}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~Within a transaction the key is one and the index pairs $(i_A, i_N)$
differ. Across transactions of a valid chain the keys
$\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}(\nfsym_{0,0})$ differ: $\nfsym_{0,0}$ is a
published nullifier, and the nullifier-set rule forbids a nullifier to
repeat within a pool (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets''; the
pool is that of Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}), and $\mathsf{I2LEOSP}_{256}$
is injective on $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$. The oracle input is the $32$-byte key
followed by the remainder. Every key of $\mathsf{PRFexpand}$ is a $32$-byte
string (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Construction ``Expansion function''), so the
byte after the key is the lead byte, and $\mathtt{0x84}$ differs from the
lead byte of every other row.

(b)~By~(a) each issued $\rho$ is $\mathrm{ToBase}$ of the oracle value at
an input of its own. In the lazy form of the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s
Definition ``Random oracle'', the first query at an input returns a fresh
uniform $512$-bit string, independent of all earlier values. The map
$\mathrm{ToBase}$ takes each value of $\F_{p_{\Pallas}}$ on at most
$\lceil 2^{512}/p_{\Pallas} \rceil$ strings, so the fresh output equals a
value fixed before the query with probability at most
$\lceil 2^{512}/p_{\Pallas} \rceil / 2^{512} < 1/p_{\Pallas} + 2^{-512}$.
Every issued $\rho$ is the reduced output of one of the $q$ queries. A
union bound over the at most $q(q-1)/2$ pairs of queries and the $qM$
pairs of a query and a fixed value (\emph{Math Guide}, \S``The union bound
and a birthday calculation'', Theorem ``Union bound / Boole's inequality''
and Proposition ``Birthday bound'') gives the first bound. The second
follows from $2(q(q-1)/2 + qM) \le (q + M)^2$ and
$1 + p_{\Pallas}\,2^{-512} < 2$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Reference notes}\label{sec:issue-ref}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, reference_address.py:
% [sec:issue-ref] all-zero sk: ask != 0 (ask negated for an even y of ak:
%   False); ivk not in {0, bottom}; ivk_internal not in {0, bottom}: the key
%   is accepted
% [sec:issue-ref] diversifier index 0: g_d = DiversifyHash(d) is not the
%   identity (True); pk_d = [ivk] g_d
% [sec:issue-ref] raw encoding LEBS2OSP_88(d) || LEBS2OSP_256(repr_P(pk_d))
%   = 11 + 32 = 43 bytes, equal to the array in ZIP 227 'Reference Notes'
% Cited: 43-byte array of the reference-note recipient: ZIP 227 'Reference
%   Notes'; 43 = 11 + 32 raw encoding: spec 'Orchard Raw Payment Addresses'
\begin{definition}[Reference note]\label{def:reference-note}
The first Issue Note of the Issuance Action in which a Custom Asset is
issued for the first time MUST be a \emph{reference note}: an Issue Note
with $v = 0$, the Asset Base of that Asset, and recipient $(d, \pkd)$ the
default diversified address, of diversifier index $0$, of the all-zero
Orchard spending key $\mathsf{sk} = [\mathtt{0x00}]^{32}$. The address is
derived by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s constructions of \S``The spending
key and the spend-side secrets'', \S``Viewing keys'' and Construction
``Diversified address''. The all-zero key passes every acceptance condition
of key generation: $\ask \neq 0$; $\ivk \notin \{0, \bot\}$; and the
incoming viewing key of its internal key (ZIP 32, ``Orchard internal key
derivation'') is not in $\{0, \bot\}$, the condition of the protocol
specification, \S``Orchard Key Components''. At index $0$ the diversified
base $\gd$ is not the identity. The raw encoding of the address,
$\mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{88}(d) \,\|\, \mathsf{LEBS2OSP}_{256}(\pkd^{\star})$
(protocol specification, \S``Orchard Raw Payment Addresses''), is the
$43$-byte string, given in ZIP 227, ``Reference Notes'', as
\begin{align*}
  (&204, 54, 96, 25, 89, 33, 59, 107, 12, 219, 150, 167, 92, 23, 195,
    166, \\
   &104, 169, 127, 13, 106, 140, 92, 225, 100, 165, 24, 234, 155, 169,
    165, \\
   &14, 167, 81, 145, 253, 134, 27, 15, 241, 14, 98, 176).
\end{align*}
Recomputing the address and every acceptance check from the cited
constructions reproduces this array. Validators enforce the rule and store the
reference note as $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ in the global issuance
state, by rule (I5) of Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}, in ``State
transition of a transaction'' (\S\ref{sec:state-rules}) (ZIP 227,
``Reference Notes'' and ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes''). Class:
specified; the reference note is mandated and stored in consensus state,
and no section of ZIP 227 states its purpose.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Reference notes as universal Split Inputs]
\label{prop:reference-copy}
Let $n_{\mathsf{ref}}$ be the reference note of a Custom Asset with Asset
Base $B$, its extracted commitment a leaf of the note commitment tree.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Any party can construct a Split Action
  (Definition~\ref{def:split-input}) whose Split Input copies
  $n_{\mathsf{ref}}$, with a witness of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}
  and a spend-authorisation signature that verifies under its $\rk$, for a
  created note of Asset Base $B$ and value $0$ to any receiver of its
  choice. Proposition~\ref{prop:split-ownership} therefore imposes nothing
  on $n_{\mathsf{ref}}$, whose recipient key is public by construction.
\item[(b)] Let an efficient adversary output OrchardZSA bundles of valid
  blocks whose Action proofs and binding signatures verify and whose witnessed
  and burn-pair Asset Bases meet the premise on the bases of
  \S\ref{sec:burn-balance}. Except with negligible probability, in every
  such bundle in which no extracted witness of Asset Base $B$ has
  $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$, every created note of Asset Base $B$ has
  value $0$, and the burn set has no pair of Asset Base $B$.
\end{enumerate}
Hence $n_{\mathsf{ref}}$ serves exactly the created notes of $B$ that a
party holding no note of $B$ can produce. Under
Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}, a copy to a receiver other than that of the
copied note would violate the cross-address rule if the notes were in the
Orchard pool. Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for
the specified constructions; part~(a) is conditional on the open lead byte
(Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}); part~(b) is conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} through Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance}, and
its premise on the bases is discharged for valid chains in ``Security''
(\S\ref{sec:security}). The reading of the purpose is the volume's; ZIP 227
states none.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~The construction is explicit. The all-zero spending key is accepted
(Definition~\ref{def:reference-note}), so its $\ask$, $\nksym$, $\rivk$
and $\aksym^{\PP}$ are public constants. The note $n_{\mathsf{ref}}$ is
published unencrypted with $\rho$ and $\mathsf{rseed}$
(Remark~\ref{rem:public-issuance}), so $\psi$ is public and, once the lead
byte is assigned (Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}), so are $\rcm$ and the
commitment. The position of its leaf in the tree is public, by
Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}. The party takes a valid anchor
$\mathit{rt}$ whose tree contains the leaf and computes its authentication
path, which satisfies A3$'$; it opens A1$'$ with the fields of
$n_{\mathsf{ref}}$ and $v^{\mathsf{old}} = 0$; it sets
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$, $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 0$ and
$\mathsf{enableZSA} = 1$, which satisfy C1 to C3 since
$B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$; it draws $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$ and computes
the split nullifier of A5$'$ with the public $\nksym$; it creates a note of
base $B$, value $0$ and any receiver, whose commitment satisfies A2$'$, and
takes any $\rcv$ for A4$'$, which reads $v' = 0$ and $v^{\mathsf{new}} =
0$. Conditions A6 and A7 hold with the public $(\aksym, \nksym, \rivk)$,
from which the reference address is derived; A8 holds since
$v^{\mathsf{old}} = 0$, and A9 since $v^{\mathsf{new}} = 0$. The party
signs under $\rsk = \ask + \alpha$ for its randomiser $\alpha$, as any
owner does.

(b)~By Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance}(a), except with negligible
probability, the net values of the Actions of base $B$, read from the
extracted witnesses, sum as integers to the burnt amount $v_B$, which is
$0$ if $B$ is not burnt. Every such Action has $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$,
so its value $v'$ is $0$ by the notation of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}, and the sum is
$-\sum_i v^{\mathsf{new}}_i \le 0$. A burn pair has $v_B > 0$ by rule (B2)
of Definition~\ref{def:burn-set}, so $v_B \ge 0$. Both sides therefore
vanish: every $v^{\mathsf{new}}_i$ of base $B$ is $0$, and $v_B = 0$
excludes a pair of base $B$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Issuance authorisation}\label{sec:issue-auth}

\begin{definition}[Issuance authorisation rules]\label{def:issue-auth}
For a transaction that contains an Issuance Bundle
$(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{vIssueActions}, \mathsf{issueAuthSig})$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(I1)] It MUST contain an OrchardZSA Action Group; by
  Definition~\ref{def:action-group} it contains exactly one.
\item[(I2)] The encoding $\mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ of the issuance validating
  key $\mathsf{ik}$ MUST be taken from the field $\mathsf{issuer}$ and MUST
  start with the byte $\mathtt{0x00}$, which indicates a BIP-340 public
  key; $\mathsf{ik}$ is the remaining $32$ bytes
  (Definition~\ref{def:issuer}).
\item[(I3)] The first byte of $\mathsf{issueAuthSig}$ MUST be
  $\mathtt{0x00}$, and
  \[
    \mathsf{IssueAuthSig}.\mathsf{Validate}(\mathsf{ik}, \mathsf{SigHash},
    \sigma) = 1
  \]
  MUST hold for the $64$-byte $\sigma$ that follows it
  (Definition~\ref{def:issueauthsig}). ZIP 227 writes the rule with
  $\mathsf{issueAuthSig}$ in place of $\sigma$; the scheme byte is stripped
  because $\mathsf{Validate}$ is typed over $32$-byte keys and $64$-byte
  signatures.
\end{enumerate}
For every Issuance Action the Issue Notes are then constructed from the
elements of its $\mathsf{vNotes}$, with $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ derived from
its $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ and from $\mathsf{issuer}$
(Remark~\ref{rem:assetbase-recompute}). The rules on the issuance state,
(T1) to (T4) and (I4) to (I7), are Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}.
The message $\mathsf{SigHash}$ is the transaction's signature digest
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Signature digest''), which ZIP 227
defines by the protocol specification's \S``SIGHASH Transaction Hashing''
``with the modifications described in ZIP 226'', under
$\mathsf{SIGHASH\_ALL}$. The cited section of ZIP 226 does not exist; ZIP
226, ``Sighash modifications relative to ZIP 244'', defers to ZIP 246,
which is Withdrawn; and the version-$6$ digest of ZIP 229 has no issuance
child (ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'' and ``Issuance
Authorization Signing and Validation''). Class: rules (I1) to (I3)
specified; the signed message designed but unspecified
(Remark~\ref{rem:sighash-status}).
\end{definition}

\begin{assumption}[Coverage of the signature digest]\label{ass:sighash-cover}
The signature digest $\mathsf{SigHash}$ of an OrchardZSA transaction is
computed without any signature of the transaction: neither
$\mathsf{issueAuthSig}$, nor a spend-authorisation signature, nor the
binding signature is an input. No efficient algorithm outputs, except with
negligible probability, two transactions with equal $\mathsf{SigHash}$ that
differ in
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] the effecting data of the Issuance Bundle: $\mathsf{issuer}$,
  the number and order of the Issuance Actions, and, per Issuance Action,
  $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$, $\mathsf{finalize}$ and every field
  $(d, \pkd, v, \rho, \mathsf{rseed})$ of every Issue Note;
\item[(ii)] the burn set; or
\item[(iii)] the published data of the Actions of the Action Group, in
  particular $\nfsym_{0,0}$.
\end{enumerate}
This hypothesis (H-SIG) concerns a designed but unspecified object.
``Transaction digests and the signature message''
(\S\ref{sec:carrier-digest}) shows that the digest of withdrawn ZIP 246 meets it
under the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b'', and states it as
the obligation on any future digest (Remark~\ref{rem:digest-commitments}).
The version-$6$ digest of ZIP 229 does not meet~(i).
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[Recomputation of issued Asset Bases]
\label{rem:assetbase-recompute}
Of the identity chain of Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}, consensus sees
exactly two links on the issuance side: $\mathsf{issuer}$ in the Issuance
Bundle and $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ in each Issuance Action. The Asset
Identifier, the Asset Digest and the Asset Base of issued notes are never
transmitted. Every validator derives
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{AssetBase} := \mathsf{ZSAValueBase}\bigl(
    &\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}512}(\texttt{ZSA-Asset-Digest}, \\
    &\mathsf{EncodeAssetId}(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash}))\bigr)
\end{align*}
by that construction. The same bytes $\mathsf{issuer}$ name the Asset and
yield, by (I2), the key under which (I3) verifies the signature; under
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}(i), both $\mathsf{issuer}$ and
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ are in the signed message. The consequence for
authority over issuance is Theorem~\ref{thm:issue-authority}, in
``Authority over issuance'' (\S\ref{sec:security-authority}) (ZIP 227,
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'' and ``Issuance Bundle'').
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Signing order of an issuance bundle]\label{rem:signing-order}
Every $\rho$ of an Issue Note is a function of $\nfsym_{0,0}$
(Construction~\ref{def:issued-rho}), fixed once the first Action of the
Action Group is fixed. Under Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover} the signed
digest covers every $\rho$ and every other effecting field of the
transaction. The issuer can therefore sign only after the nullifier of the
first Action and all effecting data are fixed. Its message is the
$\mathsf{SIGHASH\_ALL}$ digest that every spend-authorisation signature and
the binding signature sign (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Signature
digest''; ZIP 227, ``Computation of $\rho$'', ``Issuance Protocol'' and
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'').
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Replay protection of issuance]\label{prop:issue-replay}
Let $\mathsf{ik}$ be the issuance validating key of an honest issuer, which
signs only digests $\mathsf{SigHash}$ of transactions it builds. Except
with negligible probability, in a valid chain produced by an efficient
adversary that obtains the honest issuer's signatures:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] every Issuance Bundle whose $\mathsf{issuer}$ is
  $\mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ of $\mathsf{ik}$ belongs to a transaction whose
  $\mathsf{SigHash}$ the issuer signed;
\item[(b)] no transaction of the chain that contains an Issuance Bundle has
  the $\mathsf{SigHash}$ of another transaction of the chain.
\end{enumerate}
Hence each digest that the issuer signs authorises the issuance of at most
one transaction of the chain. Rule (I1) is what makes~(b) hold: without an
Action Group, the digest of a transaction that carries only an Issuance
Bundle depends on no value that the chain forbids to repeat, and a
duplicate of the bundle in a transaction with the same remaining digest
inputs has the same $\mathsf{SigHash}$, so its signature verifies again
(ZIP 227, ``Rationale''). Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}):
proved here for the specified rules (I1) to (I3); conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}, a hypothesis on the designed but
unspecified signed message, and on
Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf}. ZIP 227, ``Rationale'', states the
replay argument informally.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a)~An accepted bundle satisfies (I2) and (I3), so its signature verifies
on the transaction's $\mathsf{SigHash}$ under the key whose encoding is
$\mathsf{issuer}$, which is $\mathsf{ik}$ by the injectivity of
$\mathsf{ik} \mapsto \mathsf{issuer}$ (Definition~\ref{def:issuer}). A
verifying signature on a digest that the issuer never signed is an
existential forgery against Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf}: the
reduction answers the adversary's requests for the issuer's signatures with
its signing oracle and outputs the digest and the signature of the chain.

(b)~By (I1) a transaction with an Issuance Bundle has an Action Group, and
so a nullifier $\nfsym_{0,0}$. Another transaction of the chain with equal
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ has, by Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}(iii), the
same published Action data, except with negligible probability, since the
chain is the output of an efficient algorithm; in particular it publishes
$\nfsym_{0,0}$ as well. The nullifier-set rule forbids a nullifier to
repeat across the transactions of a valid chain (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
\S``Nullifier sets''), so no such transaction exists. By~(a) and~(b) the
map from the transactions of the chain that carry a bundle under
$\mathsf{ik}$ to the digests that the issuer signed is injective, which is
the final claim.
\end{proof}

\section{The global issuance state}\label{sec:state}

This section constructs the record that every node keeps of the issued
Custom Assets: the global issuance state of ZIP 227 and its chaining through
blocks and transactions (\S\ref{sec:state-map}); the transition of that state
under one transaction, in which burn precedes issuance, together with the
order in which the note commitments of a transaction enter the note
commitment tree (\S\ref{sec:state-rules}); the properties of finalisation
and of the recorded balance (\S\ref{sec:state-final}); and the complete
verification of an OrchardZSA transaction, with each check paired with the
requirement that it serves (\S\ref{sec:state-verify}). The rules are those of
ZIP 227, which is Draft; one case that they leave undetermined is an open
problem (Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action}).

\subsection{The issuance state and its chaining}\label{sec:state-map}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:state-map] MAX_ISSUE = 2^64 - 1 = 18446744073709551615
%   = 2^l_value - 1, the largest value of one Issue Note, so one note can
%   issue a whole supply
% Cited: MAX_ISSUE := 2^64 - 1: ZIP 227 'Global Issuance State'
\begin{definition}[Global issuance state]\label{def:issued-assets}
Let
\[
  \mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE} := 2^{64} - 1 = 18\,446\,744\,073\,709\,551\,615,
\]
the largest value of one Issue Note (Definition~\ref{def:issue-note}). The
\emph{global issuance state} is a finite partial map
\begin{align*}
  \mathsf{issued\_assets} \colon \Ep^{*} &\rightharpoonup
  \{0, \dots, \mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}\} \times \{0, 1\} \times
  \bigl(\mathsf{Note}^{\mathsf{Issue}} \cup \{\bot\}\bigr), \\
  \mathsf{AssetBase} &\mapsto
  (\mathsf{balance}, \mathsf{final}, \mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}),
\end{align*}
from Asset Bases to triples, with $\Ep^{*}$ as in \S\ref{sec:asset-base}.
The components are written
\begin{gather*}
  \mathsf{issued\_assets}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance}, \qquad
  \mathsf{issued\_assets}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final}, \\
  \mathsf{issued\_assets}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}.
\end{gather*}
An Asset Base outside the domain of the map, written
\[
  \mathsf{issued\_assets}(\mathsf{AssetBase}) = \bot,
\]
reads as $(0, 0, \bot)$, and a write to a component of such an Asset Base
creates its entry from that default. The components mean the following.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The \emph{balance} is the amount of the Asset in circulation: the
  amount issued less the amount burnt.
\item The \emph{finalisation bit} $\mathsf{final}$ records whether
  $\mathsf{finalize} = 1$ occurred in an earlier issuance of the Asset; it
  never changes from $1$ to $0$.
\item The \emph{reference note} $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ is the
  Asset's reference note (Definition~\ref{def:reference-note}), or $\bot$.
\end{enumerate}
ZIP 227 types the third component in $\mathsf{Note}^{\mathsf{Issue}}$ while
its default reads $\bot$; it is typed here in
$\mathsf{Note}^{\mathsf{Issue}} \cup \{\bot\}$. The bound
$\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$ applies to the balance, the amount in circulation, and
not to cumulative issuance: after burns an unfinalised Asset can be issued
again, so the sum of all values issued of one Asset may exceed
$\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$. The phrases ``maximum total supply'' and ``limit the
total issuance'' of ZIP 227 are looser than its rule (I6) of
Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}, which the volume follows.

\emph{Chaining.} Every node MUST update the map while processing any
transaction that contains a burn set or an Issuance Bundle. A transaction has
an \emph{input state} $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{IN}}$ and an
\emph{output state} $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the input state of the first transaction of the OrchardZSA activation
  block is the empty map;
\item the input state of the first transaction of any later block is the
  final state of the preceding block;
\item the input state of every later transaction of a block is the output
  state of the preceding transaction;
\item the \emph{final state} of a block is the output state of its last
  transaction.
\end{enumerate}
The chaining is that of treestates in the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Definition ``Treestate and anchor'' (\S``Anchors'') (ZIP 227, ``Global
Issuance State'' and ``Management of the Global Issuance State''). Class:
specified. The OrchardZSA activation block is not assigned, since ZIP 227,
``Deployment'', reads ``TBD''; this is an open problem, listed in ``Open
problems'' (\S\ref{sec:status-open}).
\end{definition}

ZIP 227, ``Rationale for Global Issuance State'', gives three reasons, which
are rationale of the ZIP. The balance of an issued Custom Asset must never
become negative, along the lines of ZIP 209, and no single transaction field
carries both the issued and the burnt amounts, so nodes keep a running record
of the amount in circulation. The bound $\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$ is a practical
limit that lets an issuer issue the complete supply of an Asset in one
transaction, indeed in one Issue Note. The finalisation bit lets nodes reject
further issuance of a finalised Asset. The properties themselves are proved
in ``Finalisation'' (\S\ref{sec:state-final}).

\begin{remark}[Issuance state under reorganisation]\label{rem:reorg-state}
By the chaining of Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}, the final state of a
block is a function of the branch from the OrchardZSA activation block to
that block. A reorganisation (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition
``Reorganisation'') therefore replaces the state by the one obtained by
chaining along the new branch from the last common block, as it replaces the
treestates. ZIP 227 states forward chaining only and no rule for
reorganisations: the statement here is a consequence of the definition, not
a rule of the ZIP. Every result of this section, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:supply}, holds per branch.
\end{remark}

\subsection{State transition of a transaction}\label{sec:state-rules}

\begin{construction}[Transition of the issuance state]
\label{def:state-transition}
\emph{Input.} A transaction $T$ that may contain an OrchardZSA bundle
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bundle}), with burn set $\mathsf{assetBurn}$, empty
when $T$ has none, and an Issuance Bundle
$(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{vIssueActions}, \mathsf{issueAuthSig})$
(Definition~\ref{def:issue-bundle}); and its input state
$\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{IN}}$.

\emph{Output.} The output state $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}$,
defined only when every requirement below holds; otherwise $T$ is invalid
and contributes no state. Every write below is to
$\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}$, and every read is of its current
value. The labels follow the order of ZIP 227, which numbers none.

\emph{For every transaction:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=3.2em]
\item[(T1)] The transaction carries at most one Action Group, and that Action
  Group has no expiry height of its own.
\item[(T2)] $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}} :=
  \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{IN}}$.
\item[(T3)] The burn set $\mathsf{assetBurn}$ satisfies (B1) to (B3) of
  Definition~\ref{def:burn-set}.
\item[(T4)] For every $(\mathsf{AssetBase}, v) \in \mathsf{assetBurn}$, it
  MUST hold that
  \[
    \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance}
    \ge v;
  \]
  the node then sets, for every pair,
  \[
    \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance}
    := \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).
    \mathsf{balance} - v,
  \]
  before any issuance is processed. By (B3) the pairs have distinct Asset
  Bases, so checking every pair and then subtracting equals processing the
  pairs one by one.
\end{enumerate}

\emph{If $T$ contains an Issuance Bundle:} rules (I1) to (I3) of
Definition~\ref{def:issue-auth} hold, and for each Issuance Action
$(\mathsf{assetDescHash},\allowbreak \mathsf{vNotes},\allowbreak
\mathsf{flagsIssuance})$ of $\mathsf{vIssueActions}$, in order, with
zero-based position $i_A$ in the bundle, let $\mathsf{AssetBase}$ be
derived from $\mathsf{issuer}$ and $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ by
Construction~\ref{def:assetbase} (Remark~\ref{rem:assetbase-recompute}).
Then:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=3.2em]
\item[(I4)] Every Issue Note description of the action MUST be a valid
  encoding, and each yields the Issue Note of
  Definition~\ref{def:issue-bundle} with that $\mathsf{AssetBase}$. It MUST
  hold that
  $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final}
  \neq 1$, evaluated against the state as updated by the earlier actions of
  the same bundle.
\item[(I5)] If
  $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).
  \mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} = \bot$, the first Issue Note of the action
  MUST be a reference note (Definition~\ref{def:reference-note}): its value
  MUST be $0$, and its recipient $(d, \pkd)$ MUST be the default diversified
  address of the all-zero Orchard spending key. The node then sets
  $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).
  \mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ to that note.
\item[(I6)] For each Issue Note of the action, at zero-based position $i_N$
  and of value $v$: its element $\rho$ MUST equal
  $\mathsf{DeriveIssuedRho}(\nfsym_{0,0}, i_A, i_N)$
  (Construction~\ref{def:issued-rho}); it MUST hold that
  \[
    \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance}
    + v \le \mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE},
  \]
  and the node then adds $v$ to that balance; and the node MUST compute the
  note commitment of the Issue Note by Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit},
  whose inputs are qualified in Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}.
\item[(I7)] If $\mathsf{finalize} = 1$ in $\mathsf{flagsIssuance}$, the
  node sets
  \[
    \mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final}
    := 1.
  \]
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes''; ZIP 226, ``Additional
Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set''.) Class: specified. The encodings
that (T1) and (I4) name belong to the carrier: ZIP 227 states (T1) ``for
every transaction'' as $\mathtt{nActionGroupsOrchard} \in \{0, 1\}$ and
$\mathtt{nAGExpiryHeight} = 0$, fields that exist only in withdrawn ZIP 230,
and (I4) by reference to the field encodings of ZIP 230; that carrier is open
(Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}). ZIP 227 writes the balance of (I6) as
$\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}.\mathsf{balance}$, without the
argument $\mathsf{AssetBase}$, which is restored here.
\end{construction}

Four consequences are read off Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}.
Burns are checked against the balance before issuance, so a transaction
cannot burn units that it issues. A burn of an Asset Base without an entry
fails (T4), since the default balance is $0$ and (B2) gives $v > 0$. A
finalised Asset remains burnable, since no rule of the burn half reads
$\mathsf{final}$. Since (I4) precedes (I7) within an action, one action may
issue notes and finalise its Asset.

\begin{definition}[Insertion order of note commitments]\label{def:tree-order}
When a transaction $T$ that passes Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}
is added to the block chain, the leaves that it appends to the note
commitment tree of its treestate are, in this order:
\begin{enumerate}
\item for each Action of the Action Group of $T$, in order, the extracted
  commitment $\cmx$ of the note that the Action creates, whatever its value
  and whether or not the Action is a Split Action;
\item then, for each Issuance Action of the Issuance Bundle of $T$, in
  order, and each of its Issue Notes, in order, the extracted commitment of
  the note commitment computed by (I6).
\end{enumerate}
The transactions of a block are taken in block order. The definition
extends the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``State update''
(\S``Verification of a transaction''), which appends the commitments of
Actions only. The position of every leaf, and hence every authentication
path, is a function of the chain under this order, so nodes and wallets
agree on positions only under it. Which pool's tree the order extends is the
open problem of Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise} (ZIP 227, ``Addition to the
Note Commitment Tree''; protocol specification, \S``Note Commitment
Trees''). Class: specified.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Empty Issuance Actions]\label{rem:empty-action}
The rules of ZIP 227 place no requirement on the number of Issue Notes of an
Issuance Action, and withdrawn ZIP 230, ``Issuance Action Description
(\texttt{IssueAction})'', admits an action without notes so that an issuer
can finalise an Asset without issuing more of it. Consider such an action on
the Asset Base $\mathsf{AssetBase}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} \neq \bot$ when the action is
  processed, an action with $\mathsf{finalize} = 0$ changes nothing, and one
  with $\mathsf{finalize} = 1$ sets $\mathsf{final}$ by (I7).
\item If $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} = \bot$, rule (I5) refers to a first
  Issue Note that does not exist, and the rules do not determine whether the
  action, finalising or not, is valid. If a finalising one were accepted, it
  would create the entry $(0, 1, \bot)$: an Asset finalised with no
  reference note and no issuance.
\end{enumerate}
Class: case~(b) is an open problem, listed in ``Open problems''
(\S\ref{sec:status-open}); a rule that fixes the validity of such an action
closes it.
\end{remark}

An issuance state is \emph{reachable} if it is the input or the output state
of a transaction in a sequence of transactions, chained from the empty map as
in Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}, each of which has an output state
under Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}. A \emph{valid chain} in the
results of this section is such a sequence; an invalid transaction
contributes no state. Every chain accepted by
Construction~\ref{def:zsa-verify} is a valid chain in this sense, since that
procedure includes the transition.

\begin{lemma}[First-issuance detection]\label{lem:first-issuance}
Assume that no valid chain contains an Issuance Action without Issue Notes
on an Asset Base whose $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ is $\bot$ when the
action is processed, which is case~(b) of Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action}.
Then in every reachable issuance state $S$, and for every
$\mathsf{AssetBase}$, the Asset Base is in the domain of $S$ if and only if
$S(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} \neq \bot$. Hence
conditioning (I5) on the absence of an entry is equivalent to conditioning it
on $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} = \bot$. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified rules;
conditional on the hypothesis, which concerns the open case of
Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Induction over the transactions of a valid chain, the invariant being
checked after every write of Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}. It
holds for the empty map. Rule (T2) copies a state. Rule (T4) writes only
Asset Bases with $\mathsf{balance} \ge v > 0$, by (B2), hence Asset Bases
already in the domain, and leaves $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ unchanged.
Rule (I4) only reads. For an action on an Asset Base outside the domain,
$\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} = \bot$ and the action has at least one Issue
Note by the hypothesis, so (I5) sets $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ to that
note before (I6) and (I7) write: the entry is created together with its
reference note. For an action on an Asset Base in the domain,
$\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}} \neq \bot$ by the invariant, and (I5) does not
apply. No rule writes $\bot$ to $\mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}}$ or removes an
entry. Both the burn step and the hypothesis are needed: without the
hypothesis, a finalising action of case~(b), if accepted, creates the entry
$(0, 1, \bot)$ by (I7).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Keys of the issuance state]\label{lem:state-keys}
In every reachable issuance state, every Asset Base in the domain equals
$\mathsf{ZSAValueBase}(\mathsf{AssetDigest})$ for the Asset Identifier
$(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash})$ of some accepted Issuance
Action (Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}), and is therefore the value of
$\GroupHash$ at an input under \texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}
(Table~\ref{tab:grouphash-zsa}). For every transaction of a valid chain, the
Asset Base of every pair of its burn set is in the domain of its input state,
and is therefore of the same form. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified rules;
unconditional, case~(b) of Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action} included. The
lemma discharges, in Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}, the premise of
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance} on the bases of burn pairs, which (B1)
alone does not discharge.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Induction over the transactions of a valid chain. The initial map is empty.
Rules (I4) to (I7) write only the entry at the Asset Base that
Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} derives from $\mathsf{issuer}$ and
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ by Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}, whether or not
the action has Issue Notes, and so does (I7) in case~(b) of
Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action} if such an action is accepted. Rule (T4) writes
only an Asset Base in the domain: an Asset Base outside it reads
$\mathsf{balance} = 0$, and (B2) requires $v > 0$, so the check of (T4)
fails. The same argument gives the second claim, since (T4) reads the input
state, copied by (T2), before any issuance of the transaction.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finalisation}\label{sec:state-final}

Every Issuance Action carries the Boolean $\mathsf{finalize}$ in
$\mathsf{flagsIssuance}$ (Definition~\ref{def:issue-bundle}). An Asset is
\emph{finalised} in a reachable issuance state $S$ when
$S(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final} = 1$ for its Asset Base. Setting
$\mathsf{finalize}$ finalises the Asset by (I7), and every later Issuance
Action for its Asset Base, with or without Issue Notes, fails (I4). Only (I7)
writes $\mathsf{final}$, and it writes $1$, so $\mathsf{final}$ never returns
from $1$ to $0$, as Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets} states. Finalisation is
the only mechanism of the issuance life cycle that ZIP 227 specifies (ZIP 227,
``Requirements'', ``Issuance Action'' and ``Specification: Consensus Rule
Changes''). Class: specified.

\begin{proposition}[Finalised supply never grows]\label{prop:final-supply}
In a reachable issuance state $S$ of a valid chain, if
$S(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final} = 1$, then in every later reachable
state $S'$ of the same chain $S'(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{final} = 1$ and
$S'(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance} \le
S(\mathsf{AssetBase}).\mathsf{balance}$, and the balance is non-increasing
from $S$ on. The Asset can still be burnt. This is a property of the map; its
relation to the notes in the tree is Theorem~\ref{thm:supply}. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified rules;
unconditional.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Induction over the later transactions. Only (I7) writes $\mathsf{final}$, and
it writes $1$. Rule (I4) rejects every later Issuance Action for
$\mathsf{AssetBase}$, with or without Issue Notes, since it reads
$\mathsf{final} = 1$, so (I6) never runs for that Asset Base again. The only
other rule that writes $\mathsf{balance}$ is (T4), which subtracts. No rule
of the burn half reads $\mathsf{final}$, so a burn of the Asset is accepted
whenever (T3) and (T4) hold.
\end{proof}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:state-map] MAX_ISSUE = 2^64 - 1 = 18446744073709551615
%   = 2^l_value - 1, the largest value of one Issue Note, so one note can
%   issue a whole supply
\begin{proposition}[The recorded balance]\label{prop:recorded-balance}
In every reachable issuance state $S$ of a valid chain and for every Asset
Base $B$,
\[
  S(B).\mathsf{balance} = \sum v_{\mathsf{iss}} - \sum v_{\mathsf{burn}}
  \in \{0, \dots, \mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}\},
\]
the first sum over the values of the Issue Notes of Asset Base $B$ of the
accepted Issuance Actions up to $S$, the second over the values of the pairs
$(B, v_{\mathsf{burn}})$ of the burn sets of the accepted transactions up to
$S$. The balance increases only under (I6) and decreases only under (T4), so
burn is the only mechanism that lowers it, and a burn of an Asset Base that
has never been issued is rejected. The bound is on circulation, not on
cumulative issuance (Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}). This is a property
of the map; its equality with the value of the notes created and not
consumed is Theorem~\ref{thm:supply}. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified rules;
unconditional.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Induction over the transactions, the claim being checked after every write.
For the empty map both sides are $0$. Rule (T2) copies a state. Rule (T4)
subtracts $v$ after checking $\mathsf{balance} \ge v$, which keeps the
identity and $\mathsf{balance} \ge 0$; an Asset Base without an entry reads
$0$, and (B2) gives $v > 0$, so a burn of a never-issued Asset Base fails.
Rule (I6) adds $v$ after checking that the sum is at most
$\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$, which keeps the identity and the upper bound; the
reference note of (I5) has value $0$ and enters both sides as $0$. No other
rule writes $\mathsf{balance}$, and a rejected transaction contributes no
state.
\end{proof}

Figure~\ref{fig:issuance-state} draws the transitions of one entry of the
map.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[
  font=\footnotesize,
  st/.style={draw=#1, fill=#1, fill opacity=0.12, text opacity=1,
    line width=0.7pt, rounded corners=3pt, inner sep=4pt, align=center,
    minimum height=11mm, minimum width=27mm},
  ed/.style={line width=0.6pt, draw=#1, -{Stealth[length=5pt]}},
  rj/.style={line width=0.6pt, draw=#1, dashed, -{Stealth[length=5pt]}},
  lb/.style={fill=white, inner sep=1.5pt, align=center},
]
\node[st=darkgray] (A) at (0,0)
  {absent\\ read as $(0, 0, \bot)$};
\node[st=tierin] (U) at (4.35,0)
  {$(\mathsf{balance}, 0, \mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}})$\\
   {\scriptsize unfinalised}};
\node[st=tierrecv] (F) at (8.7,0)
  {$(\mathsf{balance}, 1, \mathsf{note}_{\mathsf{ref}})$\\
   {\scriptsize finalised}};
\node[st=tierspend] (R) at (8.7,3.0)
  {transaction\\ rejected};
\node[st=tierfull] (O) at (0,-3.0)
  {$(0, 1, \bot)$\\ {\scriptsize open case}};
\draw[ed=darkgray] (A) -- node[lb, above=2pt]
  {(I5), (I6)\\ $\mathsf{finalize} = 0$} (U);
\draw[ed=darkgray] (U) -- node[lb, above=2pt] {(I7)} (F);
\draw[ed=darkgray] (A.south east) .. controls +(1.2,-2.9) and
  +(-1.2,-2.9) .. node[lb, pos=0.5, below=1pt]
  {(I5), (I6), (I7) in one action} (F.south west);
\draw[ed=tierin] (U) to[out=115, in=65, looseness=5]
  node[lb, above] {(I6): $+v$,\\ at most $\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$} (U);
\draw[ed=tierin] (U) to[out=-65, in=-115, looseness=5]
  node[lb, below] {(T4): $-v$, at most $\mathsf{balance}$} (U);
\draw[ed=tierrecv] (F) to[out=25, in=-25, looseness=5]
  node[lb, right] {(T4):\\ $-v$} (F);
\draw[rj=tierspend] (F) -- node[lb, right=2pt]
  {(I4): any\\ Issuance Action} (R);
\draw[rj=tierspend] (A.north) to[out=90, in=180] node[lb, pos=0.35]
  {(T4): any burn} (R.west);
\draw[rj=tierfull] ([xshift=7mm]A.south) -- node[lb, left=2pt, align=right]
  {(I7) without notes:\\ validity open\\
   (Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action})} ([xshift=7mm]O.north);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{Transitions of one entry of the global issuance state of
Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}. Solid edges are accepted transitions,
labelled with the rules of Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} that take
them. The first issuance, by (I5) and (I6), creates the entry together with its
reference note, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:first-issuance}; one action may also issue
and finalise at once. The unfinalised entry grows under (I6), up to
$\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$, and shrinks under (T4), down to $0$; the finalised entry
only shrinks under (T4), again down to $0$, and every Issuance Action on it is
rejected by (I4), as in Proposition~\ref{prop:final-supply}. A burn of an absent
entry is rejected by (T4). The dashed edge to $(0, 1, \bot)$ is case~(b) of

\label{fig:issuance-state}
