\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Verification of an OrchardZSA transaction}
\label{sec:state-verify}

The following procedure has the form of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Construction ``Verification'' (\S``Verification of a transaction''). Each
check is tagged with the requirements of Definition~\ref{def:req-zsa} that
it serves and with the conditions of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action} that it
completes, and with its class in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:classes}:
``specified'' for the rule, followed by ``carrier designed but
unspecified'' when its encoding exists only in withdrawn text, or ``carrier
open'' when no design without conflict exists (Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}).

\begin{construction}[Verification procedure for OrchardZSA]
\label{def:zsa-verify}
Given the note commitment tree and the nullifier set of the pool that carries
OrchardZSA notes (Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}), and the issuance state
$\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{IN}}$, a transaction $T$ is accepted, as
far as its OrchardZSA bundle and its Issuance Bundle are concerned, if and
only if the following checks pass.

\emph{Per Action.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*), leftmargin=2.4em]
\item \emph{Proof.} The aggregate proof of the Action Group verifies for the
  relation $\Rel_{\mathsf{ActionZSA}}$ of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action},
  under the OrchardZSA verifying key of Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, at the
  primary input
  \[
    (\mathit{rt}, \cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}, \nfsym, \rk, \cmx,
    \mathsf{enableSpends}, \mathsf{enableOutputs}, \mathsf{enableZSA})
  \]
  of each Action. [Z1, Z2, Z3 and Z5, through A1$'$ to A5$'$ and C1 to C3;
  statement specified, circuit and verifying key designed but unspecified.]
\item \emph{Spend authorisation.} Each Action's spend-authorisation signature
  is valid under its $\rk$ on $\mathsf{SigHash}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
  \S``Randomised validating keys''; unchanged by ZIP 226). [Requirement R5
  of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Requirements R1 to R5'', with
  A6 and A7; specified, signed message designed but unspecified.]
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Per Action Group and OrchardZSA bundle.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*), leftmargin=2.4em, resume]
\item \emph{Flags.} The values $\mathsf{enableSpends}$,
  $\mathsf{enableOutputs}$ and $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ are read from the Action
  Group into every primary input of step~(1)
  (Definition~\ref{def:action-group}). [Z2, through C2; flags specified,
  the bit of $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ open.]
\item \emph{Anchor.} The anchor $\mathit{rt}$ of the Action Group is the root
  of the carrying pool's note commitment tree in the final treestate of an
  earlier block of the chain (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Anchors''). [Z2,
  through A3$'$; specified, the pool open.]
\item \emph{Nullifiers.} The nullifiers of $T$, real and split, are pairwise
  distinct and absent from the carrying pool's nullifier set
  (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Nullifier sets''). [Z3, through A5$'$;
  specified.]
\item \emph{Burn set.} The burn set satisfies (B1) to (B3) of
  Definition~\ref{def:burn-set}; this is (T3). [Z1 and Z3; specified,
  carrier designed but unspecified.]
\item \emph{Balance.} The binding signature is valid on $\mathsf{SigHash}$
  under the key $\mathsf{bvk}$ of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}, computed from
  the Actions' net value commitments, the balancing value and the burn set;
  the balancing value enters the transparent transaction value pool as in the
  \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Balancing value''. [Z1 and Z2,
  through A4$'$; specified, carrier designed but unspecified; the fee terms
  are classified in Remark~\ref{rem:fees-status}.]
\end{enumerate}

\emph{Per transaction.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*), leftmargin=2.4em, resume]
\item \emph{Issuance authorisation.} If $T$ contains an Issuance Bundle,
  rules (I1) to (I3) of Definition~\ref{def:issue-auth} hold. [Z4;
  specified, signed message designed but unspecified.]
\item \emph{Issuance state.} The output state
  $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}$ of
  Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} is defined: rules (T1), (T2) and
  (T4), and (I4) to (I7) for every Issuance Action. [(T1): carrier open;
  (T4): Z2 and Z3; (I4): Z2, Z3 and Z4; (I5) to (I7): Z3; specified.]
\end{enumerate}

\emph{State update, on accepting the block that contains $T$.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*), leftmargin=2.4em, resume]
\item The leaves of Definition~\ref{def:tree-order} are appended to the
  carrying pool's note commitment tree. [Z2 and Z3; specified, the pool
  open.]
\item Every nullifier of $T$, real and split, enters the carrying pool's
  nullifier set. [Z3; specified.]
\item The issuance state becomes $\mathsf{issued\_assets}_{\mathsf{OUT}}$,
  chained as in Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}; the ZEC chain value
  pool balance of the carrying pool decreases by the balancing value, as in
  the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``State update''. [Z3;
  specified.]
\end{enumerate}
The remaining checks of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction
``Verification'', on the transaction's other components and on its expiry,
apply unchanged; the parsing of the OrchardZSA components depends on the
carrier, which is open (Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}) (ZIP 226,
``Circuit Statement'', ``Additional Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set''
and ``Value Balance Verification''; ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule
Changes'' and ``Addition to the Note Commitment Tree''; protocol
specification, \S``Transactions and Treestates'', \S``Nullifier Sets'' and
\S``Note Commitment Trees''). Class: every rule specified; the classes of the
carrier, the circuit, the signed message and the pool are as tagged.
\end{construction}

The division of the checks follows the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark
``Division of the checks''. Under Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} the proof of
step~(1) yields, for each Action, a witness of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}. Whether $\mathit{rt}$ is a root that the
chain recorded, whether a nullifier is new, and whether a burn or an
issuance is admitted by the issuance state are facts about the chain's
history, which no proof over one Action's data attests; the verifier checks
them against its state, in steps~(4), (5) and~(9). Issuance is checked
entirely outside the proof, from public data
(Remark~\ref{rem:public-issuance}). Table~\ref{tab:verify-division} maps
each requirement to the conditions of the statement, the issuance and state
rules, the checks outside the proof and the result that discharges it, the
last as in Table~\ref{tab:req-results}.

\section{The transaction carrier}\label{sec:carrier}

This section states the status of every text that would carry OrchardZSA in
a transaction: the transaction formats (\S\ref{sec:carrier-format}), the
digests and the message that the three kinds of signature sign
(\S\ref{sec:carrier-digest}), and the fee terms (\S\ref{sec:carrier-fees}).
No carrier is constructed here. The withdrawn design is described
abstractly and classified as designed but unspecified; the absence of a
live carrier is classified as an open problem
(Definition~\ref{def:classes}).

\subsection{Transaction formats}\label{sec:carrier-format}

The carrier of a construction is the set of transaction fields that encode
it together with the digests that commit to it
(Definition~\ref{def:carrier}).

\emph{Deferral of the encodings.} ZIP 226, ``OrchardZSA Transaction
Structure'', states that the transaction format that carries OrchardZSA
``is described in ZIP 230'', and defers to it the transaction structure and
the note plaintexts. ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'', ``Issuance Action'' and
``Issuance Bundle'', defers to ZIP 230 the encodings of Issue Notes, of
Issuance Actions and of the Issuance Bundle. ZIP 230, now titled
``Withdrawn Version 6 Transaction Format'', has status Withdrawn
(Definition~\ref{def:zip-status}): it ``has been obsoleted by ZIP 229, and
will not be deployed'', and transaction version~$6$ ``is now defined by ZIP
229'' (ZIP 230, ``Abstract''). The consensus rules of ZIP 227 nonetheless
name fields that exist only in ZIP 230, the Action-Group count and the
per-group expiry field of rule (T1) of Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}
(``State transition of a transaction'', \S\ref{sec:state-rules}), and
require every Issue Note description to be ``a valid field encoding as
defined in ZIP 230'' (ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'').
A Draft consensus rule thus delegates to withdrawn text. ZIP 226,
``Privacy Implications'' and ``Implications for Wallets'', still identifies
OrchardZSA with version-$6$ transactions.

\emph{Version 6.} Version~$6$ is the transaction format of ZIP 229,
``Version 6 Transaction Format'' (Draft), which the active NU6.3 deploys
(ZIP 258, Draft). It carries the Ironwood component beside
the Orchard component and no bundle of OrchardZSA or of issuance
(\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The transaction format'', Construction
``Version-6 transaction''). By ZIP 229, ``Non-requirements'', the format
``need not support ZSAs''. The withdrawn format and the format of
ZIP 229 share the version number~$6$ and are distinct and incompatible.

\emph{Version 7.} ZIP 248, ``Extensible Transaction Format'' (Draft),
defines transaction version~$7$, whose version group identifier is not yet
assigned and whose ``Deployment'' section is empty. Its bundle registry
allocates no bundle to ZSA. OrchardZSA, proposed as a provisional variant
of the Orchard bundle type (ZIP 226), and ZSA Issuance, with no bundle type
(ZIP 227), appear only under ``Potential Future Bundle Types'', kinds that
``have no allocated $(\mathsf{bundleType}, \mathsf{bundleVariant})$
pair''. ZIP 248 specifies no encoding and no digest for either (ZIP 248,
``Terminology'', ``Abstract'', ``Potential Future Bundle Types'' and
``Deployment'').

\emph{Content and withdrawn design.} The abstract content that the rules of
this volume need is fixed earlier and does not depend on a carrier: the
OrchardZSA bundle of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bundle}, with at most one
Action Group, one burn set, the balancing value and the binding signature,
and the Issuance Bundle of Definition~\ref{def:issue-bundle}. The withdrawn
design of ZIP 230 encodes this content as follows; no part of it is a live
layout.
% compute/OUTPUT.md, encodings.py:
% [sec:carrier-format] check only (withdrawn ZIP 230): burn entry = 32
%   (asset_base) + 8 (value) = 40 bytes
% Cited: 40 = 32 + 8 (burn entry): ZIP 230 'OrchardZSA Asset Burn
%   Description'; 40-byte burn entry: ZIP 230 'OrchardZSA Action Group
%   Description'
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The burn set is encoded inside the Action Group as a list of
  $40$-byte entries, each the $32$-byte encoding $\mathsf{asset\_base}$ of
  Definition~\ref{def:assetbase-bytes} followed by the amount as an
  unsigned $64$-bit little-endian integer. ZIP 230 thus places one burn set
  in each Action Group, which under the limit of ZIP 227 of one Action
  Group per transaction is one burn set per transaction. Its field
  description of the amount, ``non-zero, and less than
  $\mathsf{MAX\_BURN\_VALUE}$'', is superseded by rule (B2) of
  Definition~\ref{def:burn-set}, $0 < v \le \mathsf{MAX\_BURN\_VALUE}$,
  which is ZIP 226's (ZIP 230, ``OrchardZSA Action Group Description''
  and ``OrchardZSA Asset Burn Description''; ZIP 226, ``Additional
  Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set'').
\item The flags byte of the Action Group assigns bit~$2$ to
  $\mathsf{enableZSA}$, an assignment without live counterpart
  (``Action Groups'', \S\ref{sec:action-group}).
\item Each Action carries the public fields of an Orchard Action
  description; the Action Group carries, in Action order, one
  spend-authorisation signature per Action, and the aggregate proof. The
  byte lengths of an Action depend on a plaintext encoding that no current
  ZIP defines (``Action Groups'', \S\ref{sec:action-group}).
\end{enumerate}
The pool into which a carrier would place OrchardZSA notes is the subject
of Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}.

\begin{remark}[Status of the carrier]\label{rem:carrier-status}
Under Definition~\ref{def:classes}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item the transfer, burn and issuance constructions are specified, in ZIPs
  226 and 227, both Draft;
\item the field sets and digests of withdrawn ZIPs 230 and 246, read as a
  composition of those constructions into one transaction and abstracted
  from the version number, the version group and the positions of the
  flag bits, are designed but unspecified: a complete design exists in
  withdrawn text, and no live text fixes it. The exception is the
  authorising-data digest, which ZIP 246 itself marks as not updated for
  $\mathsf{sighashInfo}$ (ZIP 246, ``v0 Digests''), and whose issuance
  child states its empty case for a transaction with no Orchard Actions
  (ZIP 246, ``A.4: issuance\_auth\_digest''); no result of this volume
  uses that digest;
\item which live transaction format carries OrchardZSA bundles and
  Issuance Bundles is an open problem. The only design assigns the version
  number~$6$ and bit~$2$ of the Orchard flags, both of which live text
  assigns otherwise: version~$6$ is the format of ZIP 229, in which that
  bit is reserved. ZIP 229 need not support ZSAs, and ZIP 248 allocates no
  bundle to them.
\end{enumerate}
The texts that leave (iii) open and the obligation that closes it are
enumerated in ``Open problems'' (\S\ref{sec:status-open}) (ZIP 229,
``Non-requirements''; ZIP 230 and ZIP 246, ``Abstract''; ZIP 248,
``Potential Future Bundle Types'').
\end{remark}

\subsection{Transaction digests and the signature message}
\label{sec:carrier-digest}

\emph{The signed message.} Every signature that OrchardZSA uses signs one
message, $\mathsf{SigHash}$: the issuance authorisation signature, by rule
(I3) of Definition~\ref{def:issue-auth}, the spend-authorisation signature
of each Action, and the binding signature of the bundle
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}; ZIP 226, ``Value Balance Verification'';
\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Signature digest''). ZIP 227 defines
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ as the digest of the protocol specification,
\S``SIGHASH Transaction Hashing'', under $\mathsf{SIGHASH\_ALL}$, ``with the
modifications described in ZIP 226'', and cites for them a ZIP 226 section
``TxId Digest'' that does not exist. ZIP 226, ``Sighash modifications
relative to ZIP 244'', and ZIP 227, ``Modifications relative to ZIP 244'',
defer those modifications to ZIP 246, ``Digests for the Withdrawn Version 6
Transaction Format'', whose status is Withdrawn and whose digests ``are now
specified by ZIP 229'' (ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule
Changes''; ZIP 246, ``Abstract'').

\emph{Withdrawn design of the transaction identifier.} ZIP 246 extends the
digest tree of ZIP 244 (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Transaction
identifier''). Each node is the function $\mathsf{h} =
\mathsf{BLAKE2b\text{-}256}$ under a personalisation of its own, applied
to the concatenation of fixed-length digests and field encodings; a node
that ZIP 246 does not redefine keeps its definition in ZIP 244. The root
has six children:
\[
  \mathsf{txid} := \mathsf{h}\bigl(\texttt{ZcashTxHash\_} \,\|\, \beta,\
    d_{\mathsf{hdr}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{tr}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Sap}}
    \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Orc}} \,\|\, d_{\mathsf{Iss}} \,\|\,
    d_{\mathsf{memo}}\bigr),
\]
with $\beta$ the encoding of the consensus branch identifier as in the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}. The header digest $d_{\mathsf{hdr}}$, redefined with
fields of ZIPs outside this volume, the transparent digest $d_{\mathsf{tr}}$
and the Sapling digest $d_{\mathsf{Sap}}$ carry no ZSA content. The other
three children do.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The \emph{Orchard branch} $d_{\mathsf{Orc}}$ hashes one digest over
  the Action Groups and the balancing value. For each Action Group, the
  digest over the Action Groups commits to every Action's nullifier $\nfsym$,
  note commitment $\cmx$, ephemeral key $\mathsf{epk}$ and ciphertexts
  $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ and $C_{\mathsf{out}}$, value commitment
  $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$ and randomised validating key $\rk$, in Action
  order, and to the flags byte, the anchor, the per-group expiry field and a
  \emph{burn digest}. The burn digest hashes every burn pair
  $(\mathsf{asset\_base}, v)$ in encoding order, and is the hash of the
  empty string when the burn set is empty. Without an Action Group the
  Orchard branch is the hash of the empty string; since the burn set is
  encoded inside the Action Group, a transaction then has no burn set. With
  at most one Action Group per transaction (ZIP 227), the digest over the
  Action Groups is one hash over the elements of that group.
\item The \emph{issuance branch} $d_{\mathsf{Iss}}$ hashes the
  length-prefixed $\mathsf{issuer}$ and an \emph{actions digest}, which
  commits, per Issuance Action and in order, to $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$,
  to a \emph{notes digest} over every Issue Note's recipient, value, $\rho$
  and $\mathsf{rseed}$, and to $\mathsf{flagsIssuance}$. ZIP 246 states
  empty cases for the issuance digest, ``no issuance components'', and for
  the notes digest, and none for the actions digest.
\item The \emph{memo branch} $d_{\mathsf{memo}}$ hashes a nonce and a
  digest over memo chunks, and presupposes the memo bundles of ZIP 231.
\end{enumerate}
The personalisation strings of the nodes below the root are not listed,
since no proof or rule of this volume uses them (ZIP 246, ``TxId Digest'',
nodes T.4, T.4a, T.4a.vi, T.5, T.5a, T.5a.i and T.6; ZIP 244, ``TxId
Digest'').

\emph{Withdrawn design of the signature digest and the authorising-data
digest.} The signature digest has the same six children as the transaction
identifier. Its Orchard, issuance and memo children are those of
$\mathsf{txid}$, and its transparent child is specific to the input, as in
ZIP 244 (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition ``Signature digest''). ZIP 246
produces a signature digest for each transparent input, each Sapling input,
each Action and each Issuance Action; all of these except those of
transparent inputs equal the $\mathsf{SIGHASH\_ALL}$ digest
$\mathsf{SigHash}$, since no other child depends on the signer. The
authorising-data digest (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition
``Authorising-data digest; effecting and authorising data'') has
transparent, Sapling, Orchard and issuance children. The Orchard child
hashes, per Action Group, the proof and the spend-authorisation
signatures, and then the binding signature; the issuance child hashes the
encoding of $\mathsf{issueAuthSig}$. Proofs and signatures enter this
digest only. ZIP 246 marks this digest as not yet updated for the
$\mathsf{sighashInfo}$ fields of its format (ZIP 246, ``v0 Digests''). ZIP 246
states that the pair of the transaction identifier
and the authorising-data digest commits to all data of the transaction
(ZIP 246, ``Signature Digest'' and ``Authorizing Data Commitment'', nodes
A.3, A.3a and A.4; ZIP 244, ``Signature Digest'' and ``Authorizing Data
Commitment'').

\begin{remark}[Commitments required of a digest]\label{rem:digest-commitments}
Under the withdrawn design, the digest $\mathsf{SigHash}$ commits
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item to the effecting data of the Issuance Bundle: $\mathsf{issuer}$ and,
  per Issuance Action and in order, $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$,
  $\mathsf{finalize}$ and every field of every Issue Note, namely the
  recipient $(d, \pkd)$, $v$, $\rho$ and $\mathsf{rseed}$; the Asset Base
  is derived from $\mathsf{issuer}$ and $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$
  (Remark~\ref{rem:assetbase-recompute});
\item to the burn set;
\item to the published data of the Actions of the Action Group, hence to
  $\nfsym_{0,0}$;
\end{enumerate}
and to no signature. The argument runs node by node. The signature
digest's Orchard child is the Orchard branch~(a), and its issuance child
is the issuance branch~(b); their inputs include the data listed in (i)
to (iii), the flag $\mathsf{finalize}$ as a bit of
$\mathsf{flagsIssuance}$. Signatures and proofs enter the authorising-data
digest only. In the withdrawn design every node input is a concatenation
of fixed-length digests and of fixed-length or length-prefixed field
encodings, so that it parses uniquely, and the number of records of each
list is determined by the length of the input. Let two transactions
with equal $\mathsf{SigHash}$ differ in a listed datum. If their consensus
branches differ, the root personalisations differ and equal outputs are
already a collision in the sense of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption
``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b''; otherwise the differing datum yields
two distinct inputs with equal outputs at some node, a collision of
$\mathsf{h}$. The withdrawn design thus meets
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover} under the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Assumption ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b''. The three commitments
(i) to (iii), together with the exclusion of every signature, are the
obligation on any future signature digest that carries OrchardZSA:
Proposition~\ref{prop:issue-replay} and Theorem~\ref{thm:issue-authority}
assume exactly them and nothing more (ZIP 246, ``TxId Digest'' and
``Signature Digest'').
\end{remark}

\emph{Current text.} The version-$6$ digests of ZIP 229 have header,
transparent, Sapling, Orchard and Ironwood children and no issuance, burn
or memo-bundle data (no counterpart of the branch $d_{\mathsf{memo}}$ of
ZIP 246); their Orchard child carries Orchard-pool Actions, which have
no Asset Bases (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Transaction digests and
signatures''; ZIP 229, ``Transaction Identifiers, Auth Digests, and
Signature Digests'' and ``Summary of the resulting digest structure'').
ZIP 248 lets each bundle type define its own versions of the signature
digest, carried with each signature as $\mathsf{sighashInfo}$, and defines
none for a ZSA bundle (ZIP 248, ``Sighash Versioning'' and ``Potential
Future Bundle Types''). No current text specifies a digest that commits to
OrchardZSA or issuance data.

\begin{remark}[Status of the signature message]\label{rem:sighash-status}
The rules by which the three kinds of signature sign $\mathsf{SigHash}$
are specified, in ZIPs 226 and 227, both Draft. The message
$\mathsf{SigHash}$ itself is designed but unspecified: its only definition
is withdrawn ZIP 246, a complete design. Its live definition, as part of a
live carrier, is an open problem (Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}). Every
result of this volume that uses the message is conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover} (Definition~\ref{def:result-status})
(ZIP 226, ``Sighash modifications relative to ZIP 244''; ZIP 227,
``Modifications relative to ZIP 244''; ZIP 246).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Fees}\label{sec:carrier-fees}

\emph{The conventional fee.} The transaction fee is the value remaining in
the transparent transaction value pool, and ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'',
specifies the conventional fee
% Cited: marginal_fee 5000, grace_actions 2: ZIP 317 'Fee calculation', as
%   cited by the Ironwood Guide, 'The balancing value' (no computed number)
\begin{gather*}
  \mathit{marginal\_fee} \cdot \max(\mathit{grace\_actions},\,
    \mathit{logical\_actions}) \text{ zatoshi}, \\
  \mathit{marginal\_fee} = 5000, \qquad \mathit{grace\_actions} = 2,
\end{gather*}
where $\mathit{logical\_actions}$ is a sum of contributions per component,
fixed by the applicable revision of ZIP 317. The fee and the formula are
those of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``The balancing value'', and are
cited, not re-derived. Paying the conventional fee is not a consensus
requirement: ``It is not a consensus requirement that fees follow this
formula; however, wallets SHOULD create transactions that pay this fee''
(ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'').

\emph{Fees in ZEC.} Fees are paid in ZEC only. The balancing value is an
amount of ZEC and enters the binding validating key at the base
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ only (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}), and a Custom
Asset has no transparent value pool
(Remark~\ref{rem:no-transparent-asset}), so no Custom Asset reaches the
pool whose remainder is the fee. ZIP 227 records the alternative, a fee
denominated in the Custom Asset, as not adopted, since for shielded
transactions any design that lifts value from the transaction leaks
information about it (ZIP 226, ``Value Balance Verification''; ZIP 227,
``Rationale for paying fees in ZEC'').

\emph{Missing ZSA terms.} ZIP 227, ``Changes to ZIP 317'', states that the
conventional fee in ZEC is altered for the Issuance Actions of a
transaction and for Custom Assets newly created in the global issuance
state, and refers to ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation''. No revision of ZIP 317
has such a term; Revision~0 is Active and Revisions~1 and~2 are Draft. The
Change History of ZIP 317 records ``Removed the ZSA issuance and
asset-creation fee contributions (ZIP 227)''. ZIP 226, ``Transaction
Fees'', links a ZIP 227 section ``OrchardZSA Fee Calculation'' that does
not exist. Both references dangle. The removed design had one
contribution per Issue Note and one per newly created Custom Asset; their
values are not quoted (ZIP 317, ``Revisions'', ``Fee calculation'' and
``Change History'').

\begin{remark}[Status of the fee terms]\label{rem:fees-status}
The ZSA fee terms have one class, designed but unspecified
(Definition~\ref{def:classes}): a complete design existed as text of ZIP
317 and was removed, and it conflicts with no live text. Live ZIP 227,
``Changes to ZIP 317'', states that the conventional fee is altered, and
live ZIP 317, ``Fee calculation'', has no such term. The conventional fee
is not a consensus rule, so the protocol of this volume does not need the
fee terms in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:classes}(iii), and they are
not an open problem. That fees are paid in ZEC is specified (ZIP 317,
``Change History''; ZIP 227, ``Changes to ZIP 317'').
\end{remark}

\section{Security}\label{sec:security}

This section composes the propositions stated at their objects into the
five requirements of Definition~\ref{def:req-zsa}, as
Table~\ref{tab:req-results} names them: Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}
discharges Z1 and Z2; Theorem~\ref{thm:supply}, with
Proposition~\ref{prop:final-supply}, discharges Z3;
Theorem~\ref{thm:issue-authority} discharges Z4; and
Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy} discharges Z5. The model and the
assumptions are fixed in \S\ref{sec:security-model}, and the four
theorems are proved in \S\ref{sec:security-counterfeit} to
\S\ref{sec:security-privacy}.

The conventions are those of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Security'',
cited, not restated. Efficient adversaries and negligible functions are
those of the \emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``Adversaries and the security
parameter'', and computational indistinguishability is that of its
Definition ``Perfect, statistical, and computational indistinguishability''
(\S``Distribution ensembles and indistinguishability''). Every reduction
simulates the honest parties and the random oracles, which are independent
of one another. Every result holds except with negligible probability, and
every result that uses a property of key generation is claimed for keys
generated as the \emph{Ironwood Guide}, \S``Security'', claims them, with
$\mathsf{use\_qsk} = \mathsf{false}$.

\subsection{Accepted transactions and assumptions}\label{sec:security-model}

\begin{definition}[Accepted OrchardZSA transactions and witnesses]
\label{def:zsa-accepted}
The definition extends the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Definition ``Pools,
accepted Actions and witnesses'' to OrchardZSA.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] \emph{Pool.} The pool is the pool that carries OrchardZSA notes,
  which Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise} leaves open. It has one note
  commitment tree, one nullifier set and one issuance state
  $\mathsf{issued\_assets}$ (Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}); every
  statement below uses these.
\item[(b)] \emph{Valid chain.} An adversary outputs a \emph{valid chain}: a
  sequence of blocks each of whose transactions passes
  Construction~\ref{def:zsa-verify} against the tree, the nullifier set and
  the issuance state that the transactions before it produce, the tree
  being extended in the order of Definition~\ref{def:tree-order} and the
  issuance state by Construction~\ref{def:state-transition}. An Action, an
  Action Group (Definition~\ref{def:action-group}), an OrchardZSA bundle
  (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bundle}) or an Issuance Bundle
  (Definition~\ref{def:issue-bundle}) of a transaction of a valid chain is
  \emph{accepted}.
\item[(c)] \emph{Witnesses.} The \emph{witness} of an accepted OrchardZSA
  Action is the auxiliary input that the extractor of
  Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} outputs for the proof of its Action Group,
  the assumption being applied to each Action Group as the \emph{Ironwood
  Guide} applies its knowledge-soundness assumption to each bundle (the
  paragraph after its Definition ``Pools, accepted Actions and
  witnesses''). It contains $\mathsf{AssetBase}$,
  $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset}$, $\mathsf{split\_flag}$ and
  $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$ besides the Orchard witness, and satisfies
  Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}. The Action \emph{consumes} the note
  \[
    (\gd^{\mathsf{old}}, \pkd^{\mathsf{old}}, v^{\mathsf{old}},
    \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho^{\mathsf{old}}, \psi^{\mathsf{old}},
    \rcm^{\mathsf{old}})
  \]
  of its witness, with value $v' := 0$ if $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$ and
  $v' := v^{\mathsf{old}}$ otherwise (the notation of A4$'$), and \emph{creates}
  the note
  \[
    (\gd^{\mathsf{new}}, \pkd^{\mathsf{new}}, v^{\mathsf{new}},
    \mathsf{AssetBase}, \rho^{\mathsf{new}}, \psi^{\mathsf{new}},
    \rcm^{\mathsf{new}}),
  \]
  with $\rho^{\mathsf{new}}$ the published nullifier. Notes are given by
  expanded receivers as in item~(d) of the cited definition. An accepted
  Action with $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$ is a \emph{real spend} of its
  consumed note; one with $\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$ is a Split Action
  (Definition~\ref{def:split-input}). The shared pool also admits
  Orchard-protocol Actions (ZIP 226, ``Privacy Implications''); the
  witness of such an Action is that of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
  Assumption ``Knowledge soundness of the Action proof'', and every such
  Action is a real spend.
\item[(d)] \emph{Issue Notes.} An Issue Note of an accepted Issuance Bundle
  is given by its published fields and the Asset Base that rule (I4) of
  Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} derives. Its note commitment is
  the one that the validator computes by rule (I6), which presupposes a
  fixed deterministic derivation of $\rcm$
  (Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}, Definition~\ref{def:result-status}).
\item[(e)] \emph{Committed notes and openings.} A \emph{committed note} is
  a note whose extracted commitment is a leaf of the tree. An
  \emph{opening} of a leaf is a note whose extracted commitment equals it.
\item[(f)] \emph{Adversary.} The adversary controls every party except the
  honest ones that it attacks. It may generate issuance keys, issue Assets
  under them with any descriptions, choose every field of its own
  transactions, copy any leaf as a Split Input, and obtain from each honest
  issuer the signed transactions that the issuer composes, in an order that
  it schedules.
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 226, ``Specification''; ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus Rule
Changes''.)
\end{definition}

By the union bound over the polynomially many Action Groups of a valid
chain, every witness satisfies Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}, except with
negligible probability. Conditions A1$'$, A2$'$, A3$'$ and A7 then hold in
their unweakened forms, without the $\bot$ case, except with negligible
probability, by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Remark ``$\bot$-weakened
conditions'' (\S``The Action statement'') and, for the custom branch of
A1$'$ and A2$'$, by Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(c), under the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' and
``GroupHash as a random oracle''. The results below include this event in
their negligible terms.

\begin{remark}[Standing assumptions]
The results of this section rest on the following assumptions, each stated
in the text named.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``GroupHash as a random
  oracle'' (\S``GroupHash, domain separation, and nothing-up-my-sleeve
  generators'') and ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' (\S``Sinsemilla:
  hash, commitment, and short forms'').
\item The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance of
  BLAKE2b'' (\S``Transaction digests and signatures''), and
  Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr} (``Asset Digests and Asset Bases'').
\item Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-ro} (``Computation of $\rho$ for Issue
  Notes'').
\item Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf} (``Issuance keys and the issuer
  identifier'').
\item Assumptions~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} and~\ref{ass:zsa-zk} (``The OrchardZSA
  Action proof''), and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Knowledge
  soundness of the Action proof'' for Orchard-protocol Actions.
\item The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``The RedPallas hash as a
  random oracle'' (\S``The RedPallas signature scheme'').
\item The assumptions of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition
  ``Confidentiality and key privacy of note encryption'' (\S``Encryption to
  the recipient''), and its Assumptions ``Poseidon is a PRF'' (\S``The
  nullifier''), ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'' (\S``Pseudorandom
  functions and field reductions'') and ``Joint hiding of the viewing keys''
  (\S``Viewing keys'').
\item The hypothesis H-SIG, Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover} (``Issuance
  authorisation''), in the form that Remark~\ref{rem:digest-commitments}
  states as the obligation on a digest.
\end{enumerate}
Following Definition~\ref{def:result-status}, three of them concern designed
but unspecified objects: Assumptions~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} and~\ref{ass:zsa-zk},
the circuit and its verifying key, and Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover},
the signed message. Each result below names which of these it is
conditional on, and the results about Issue Notes add the fixed derivation
of $\rcm$. The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s results on its own statement, for
example its Theorems ``Balance'' and ``Privacy of an Action'', are not cited
as proved for OrchardZSA; only their proof steps are reused, each cited at
its use.
\end{remark}

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Asset Bases and balance of accepted bundles}
\label{sec:security-counterfeit}

\begin{lemma}[Provenance of Asset Bases]\label{lem:provenance}
Under Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions
``Knowledge soundness of the Action proof'', ``GroupHash as a random
oracle'' and ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'', in every valid chain, except
with negligible probability:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] every leaf of the tree has at most one opening that an efficient
  algorithm finds;
\item[(ii)] every leaf whose opening has $\mathsf{AssetBase} \neq
  V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ opens to a note whose Asset Base is
  \[
    \mathsf{ZSAValueBase}\bigl(
    \mathsf{AssetDigest}_{(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash})}\bigr)
  \]
  (Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}) for the $\mathsf{issuer}$ of an
  accepted Issuance Bundle and the $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ of one of its
  Issuance Actions, whose Issue Notes precede the leaf, or include it, in
  the order of Definition~\ref{def:tree-order};
\item[(iii)] every leaf appended by an Orchard-protocol Action, before or
  after OrchardZSA, opens only with $\mathsf{AssetBase} =
  V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, and the empty leaf opens to nothing.
\end{enumerate}
Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified
statement and rules; conditional on Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which
concerns the designed but unspecified circuit, and, for the leaves of Issue
Notes, on a fixed derivation of $\rcm$, any deterministic one.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every step is carried out by the efficient algorithm that runs the
adversary with the extractors and inspects the chain; the chain has
polynomially many leaves, and the union bound collects the negligible
events.

\emph{Uniqueness of openings.} By Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(b), no
efficient algorithm outputs two distinct OrchardZSA notes with equal
extracted commitments other than $\bot$, within one branch of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit} or across the two; the sign ambiguity
of extraction is handled in its proof by the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma
``Coordinate extraction identifies opposite points''. An Orchard note is
the native-branch OrchardZSA note with the same components, by the case
split of that definition. Hence~(i), and an opening found in the native
branch excludes one in the custom branch.

\emph{Induction.} The claim is proved for the leaves in the order of
Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}, the hypothesis being (ii) and (iii) for
all earlier leaves. A position beyond the appended leaves holds
$\mathsf{Uncommitted}^{\mathsf{Orchard}} = 2$, which by the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Lemma ``Uncommitted leaves'' is the extracted commitment of no
note, since in both branches $\cmx$ is $\bot$ or the extraction of a point.
Every appended leaf is of one of three kinds.

\emph{Orchard-protocol Action.} The witness of the Action that appended the
leaf creates a note whose commitment $\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, by
the unweakened condition A2, extracts to the leaf. That commitment is the
native branch of $\NoteCommit^{\mathsf{OrchardZSA}}$
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-notecommit}), so the leaf has an opening with
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, and by~(i) no other. This
holds for leaves appended before OrchardZSA and after it.

\emph{OrchardZSA Action.} Let the witness have $\mathsf{AssetBase} = B$.
If $B = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ there is nothing to show. Otherwise
$\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 0$ by C1, so the first disjunct of A3$'$
fails, and the unweakened A3$'$ gives a valid path from
$\Extract_{\PP}(\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}})$ to the anchor $\mathit{rt}$ of the
Action Group. By check~(4) of Construction~\ref{def:zsa-verify}, the anchor
is the root of the tree in the final treestate of an earlier block. By the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Membership soundness'', and since
$\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}} \neq \bot$ by the unweakened A1$'$, the value
$\Extract_{\PP}(\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}})$ is a leaf appended by an earlier
transaction. By A1$'$, the consumed note, of Asset Base $B$, opens that
leaf, and by~(i) it is its opening. By the hypothesis, $B$ is the Asset Base
derived from the $\mathsf{issuer}$ and an $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ of an
earlier accepted Issuance Action whose Issue Notes precede that leaf. By
A2$'$, the created note carries the same $B$ and opens the new leaf, and by
(i) it is its opening. The case covers Split Actions: A3$'$ demands the
path for every consumed note with $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 0$,
whatever its value and $\mathsf{split\_flag}$, and C3 forbids
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$ with $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 1$.

\emph{Issue Note.} Rule (I4) derives the Asset Base from the bundle's
$\mathsf{issuer}$ and the action's $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ by
Construction~\ref{def:assetbase}; rule (I6) makes the validator compute
the commitment from that base and the published fields
(Remark~\ref{rem:assetbase-recompute}), with the fixed derivation of
$\rcm$; its commitment is not $\bot$ except with negligible probability,
by Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(c), its Asset Base being a custom one
(Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation}). The Issue Note therefore
opens the leaf, and by~(i) it is its
opening; its Asset Base is the derived one, which differs from
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ except with negligible probability
(Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation}), and its own Issuance Action
includes the leaf.
\end{proof}

ZIP 226 states the conclusion in rationale: ``This ensures that the value
base points of all output notes of a transfer are actual outputs of a
GroupHash, as they originate in the Issuance protocol which is publicly
verified'' (``Rationale for Split Notes''), and the output notes ``receive
an Asset Base that exists on the global state'' (``Rationale for Asset
Identifiers''). In Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}, existence on the global state
is realised by membership in the tree; the proof never consults
$\mathsf{issued\_assets}$ (ZIP 227, ``Addition to the Note Commitment
Tree''). Figure~\ref{fig:provenance} draws the induction.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[
  font=\footnotesize,
  lf/.style={draw=#1, fill=#1, fill opacity=0.14, text opacity=1,
    line width=0.6pt, rounded corners=2pt, minimum width=14mm,
    minimum height=8mm, inner sep=1pt, align=center},
  old/.style={draw=darkgray, fill=black!14, line width=0.6pt,
    rounded corners=2pt, minimum width=14mm, minimum height=8mm,
    inner sep=1pt, align=center},
  emp/.style={draw=darkgray, dashed, line width=0.6pt, rounded corners=2pt,
    minimum width=14mm, minimum height=8mm, align=center},
  ed/.style={line width=0.6pt, draw=#1, -{Stealth[length=4pt]}},
  nt/.style={align=center, font=\scriptsize},
]

\node[old] (e2) at (1.7,0) {$B$};
\node at (3.05,0) {$\cdots$};
\node[lf=tierin] (a1) at (4.4,0) {$B$};
\node[lf=tierfull] (a2) at (6.1,0) {$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$};
\node[lf=tierrecv] (i1) at (8.2,0) {$B'$\\[-1pt] {\tiny reference}};
\node[lf=tierrecv] (i2) at (9.9,0) {$B'$};
\node[emp] (z) at (11.7,0) {$2$};
\draw[ed=tierin] (a1.north) to[out=115, in=65]
  node[nt, above, pos=0.5] {A1$'$, A2$'$: one witnessed base;\\
  A3$'$: the consumed note opens this leaf} (e2.north);
\node[nt, draw=tierrecv, rounded corners=2pt, fill=white, inner sep=3pt]
  (der) at (9.05,2.0)

   $(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash})$: (I4);\\
   commitment computed by (I6)};

\draw[ed=tierrecv] (der.south) -- (i2.north);

\node[nt, below=3pt of a2] {C1, A3$'$: exempt only\\ for ZEC of value $0$};
\node[nt, below=3pt of z] {no opening\\ (uncommitted)};
\foreach \a/\b/\t in {-0.7/3.35/earlier leaves:\\ induction hypothesis,
    3.7/6.8/first:\\ Action outputs of $T$,
    7.5/10.6/then:\\ Issue Notes of $T$}
  {\draw[line width=0.5pt] (\a,-1.3) -- (\a,-1.45) -- (\b,-1.45)

\caption{Provenance of Asset Bases in the tree, the induction of
Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}. One transaction $T$ appends its leaves in the
order of Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}: the outputs of its Actions, then
its Issue Notes. Earlier leaves (grey) satisfy the induction hypothesis; a
leaf appended by an Orchard-protocol Action opens only with
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. An Action output of a Custom Asset $B$ (blue)
inherits $B$ from an earlier leaf through A1$'$, A2$'$ and A3$'$; C1 confines
the exemption from membership to ZEC (amber). The Asset Base $B'$ of an Issue
Note (green) is derived by the validator from $\mathsf{issuer}$ and
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$ by (I4), and its commitment computed by (I6). The
empty leaf $2$ opens to no note.}
\label{fig:provenance}
