\begin{corollary}[Asset Bases of accepted Actions]\label{cor:action-bases}
Under the assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}, in every valid chain,
except with negligible probability, every Asset Base witnessed by an
accepted OrchardZSA Action is $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ or
$\mathsf{ZSAValueBase}(\mathsf{AssetDigest})$ of the Asset Identifier of an
Issuance Action of an accepted Issuance Bundle of an earlier transaction.
No transactor chooses an Asset Base: every Custom Asset Base in an accepted
Action is one that a validator derived. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks} and, through Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}, on a
fixed derivation of $\rcm$ for Issue Notes.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For a witness with $\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ there is
nothing to show. The exemption of A3$'$ applies only to such consumed notes,
since C1 makes $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset} = 1$ exactly when
$\mathsf{AssetBase} = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$. Otherwise, as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}, A3$'$ and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Proposition ``Membership soundness'' make the consumed note an opening of a
leaf of the treestate at the anchor, appended by an earlier transaction;
A1$'$ fixes its Asset Base as the witnessed one; and
Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(ii) applies to that leaf. The only place where an
Asset Base not derived by a validator could enter an Action is a consumed
note without a membership proof, which C1 and A3$'$ confine to
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$; Proposition~\ref{prop:dummy-attack} shows that the
confinement is necessary (ZIP 226, ``Circuit Statement'', ``Asset Base
Equality'' and the modified Merkle path condition).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[No counterfeiting across Assets]\label{thm:no-counterfeit}
Assume Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr}, and
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Knowledge soundness of the Action
proof'', ``GroupHash as a random oracle'', ``Discrete logarithms on
Pallas'' and ``The RedPallas hash as a random oracle''; the collision
resistance of the Sinsemilla instances used by that volume's Proposition
``Membership soundness'' follows from ``GroupHash as a random oracle'' and
``Discrete logarithms on Pallas''. In every valid
chain, except with negligible probability, every accepted OrchardZSA bundle,
with Actions $i$, burn set $\mathsf{assetBurn}$ and balancing value
$v^{\mathsf{balance}}$, satisfies the following, as equalities of integers,
the values being read from the witnesses:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] for every point $B \neq V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$,
  \[
    \sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = B} (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) = v_B,
  \]
  where $v_B$ is the amount of the pair $(B, v_B) \in \mathsf{assetBurn}$,
  or $0$ if there is none;
\item[(ii)] $\sum_{i :\, \mathsf{AssetBase}_i = V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}}
  (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) = v^{\mathsf{balance}}$;
\item[(iii)] distinct Assets have distinct Asset Bases, and no Custom Asset
  has the Asset Base $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, so that (i) and (ii) hold per
  Asset.
\end{enumerate}
Hence no accepted bundle creates value of one Asset from value of another
Asset or from nothing (requirements Z1 and Z2). The statement concerns the
accepted bundles of a valid chain, not single bundles: the Asset Bases of
burn pairs are constrained only by rule (T4) of
Construction~\ref{def:state-transition} against the issuance state. Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here for the specified
constructions; conditional on Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which concerns
the designed but unspecified circuit, and on a fixed derivation of $\rcm$
for Issue Notes, and not on Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}. ZIP 226
argues the property in rationale only.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Witnessed Asset Bases.} By Corollary~\ref{cor:action-bases}, each is
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ or
\[
  \GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, D)
\]
for the Asset Digest $D$ of an accepted Issuance Action.

\emph{Asset Bases of burn pairs.} By rule (T4) every burn pair of an
accepted transaction has an Asset Base in the domain of its input state,
and by Lemma~\ref{lem:state-keys} every such Asset Base is
$\GroupHash(\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, D)$ for the Asset Digest $D$ of an
accepted Issuance Action. Rule (B1) alone does not give this.

\emph{Premise and balance.} Every input $(\texttt{z.cash:OrchardZSA}, D)$
differs from the inputs of $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and
$R^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:grouphash-distinct}). Each such
input is computable from the chain, by searching its Issuance Actions for
one whose derived Asset Base is the point. So the algorithm that runs the
adversary with the extractors, and outputs the accepted bundles together
with these inputs whenever it finds them, outputs bundles whose set
$\mathfrak{B}$ meets the premise on the bases of \S\ref{sec:burn-balance};
by the two preceding steps it fails to find an input only with negligible
probability. Parts~(a) and~(b) of Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance} then
give~(i) and~(ii), the congruences modulo $p_{\Vesta}$ being lifted to
integers by Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-integer}, whose hypotheses hold for every
bundle of a valid block. A Split Action contributes $v' = 0$ by A4$'$, so
the value of a copied note never enters these sums. No other term of
$\mathsf{bvk}$ carries a Custom Asset (Remark~\ref{rem:no-transparent-asset}).

\emph{Part~(iii).} Claims~2 and~3 of
Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation}: distinct Asset Identifiers
have distinct Asset Bases, and no Asset
Base of a Custom Asset equals $V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, except with negligible
probability, under Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr} and the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumption ``GroupHash as a random oracle''.
\end{proof}

Each premise of the proof is necessary. For witnessed Asset Bases,
Proposition~\ref{prop:dummy-attack} creates ZEC from a witnessed base
$[-1]\,V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ when the exemption of A3$'$ is not confined to
ZEC. For burn pairs, the example after Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-balance}
creates $2v$ units of an issued Asset $B$ with the pair $([-2]\,B, v)$,
which rules (B1) to (B3) admit and rule (T4) rejects. ZIP 226, ``Rationale
for Split Notes'', ``Value Balance Verification'' and ``Additional
Consensus Rules for the assetBurn set'', gives the argument informally; the
proof here does not change the class of that rationale, and ZIP 227,
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'', supplies rule (T4).

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Circulating supply}\label{sec:security-supply}

\begin{lemma}[One consumption per OrchardZSA note]\label{lem:one-consumption}
Under Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-ro}, and
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumptions ``Knowledge soundness of the Action
proof'', ``GroupHash as a random oracle'' and ``Discrete logarithms on
Pallas'', in every valid chain, except with negligible probability:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] the openings of the leaves of the tree have pairwise distinct
  elements $\rho$, and are therefore pairwise distinct notes;
\item[(b)] every committed note is consumed by at most one accepted real
  spend;
\item[(c)] a Split Action contributes $v' = 0$ and publishes a nullifier
  that is not the nullifier of its copied note.
\end{enumerate}
Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which concerns the designed but unspecified
circuit, and, for the leaves of Issue Notes, on a fixed derivation of
$\rcm$, any deterministic one.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(i), the opening of a leaf appended by an
Action is the note that the Action's witness creates, and the opening of a
leaf appended by issuance is the Issue Note.

(a)~Three cases cover every pair of leaves.

\emph{Two Action outputs.} The note created by an accepted Action, of
either protocol, has $\rho$ equal to that Action's published nullifier
(A2$'$ with $\rho^{\mathsf{new}} := \nfsym^{\mathsf{old}}$, or A2), and the
published nullifiers, real and split, are pairwise distinct by the
nullifier-set rule, check~(5) of Construction~\ref{def:zsa-verify}.

\emph{Two Issue Notes.} Their elements $\rho$ are pairwise distinct by
Proposition~\ref{prop:issued-rho-unique}, under
Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-ro}.

\emph{An Action output and an Issue Note.} It suffices that no accepted
Action publishes a nullifier equal to the $\rho$ of an Issue Note of the
chain, in whichever order the two occur;
Proposition~\ref{prop:issued-rho-unique} covers only the Action outputs
fixed before the issued value. Let an efficient algorithm $\Adv'$, running
the adversary with the extractors, find such a pair with probability
$\epsilon$. The published nullifier is $\Extract_{\PP}(Q)$, where by A5$'$
(or A5)
\[
  Q = [s]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}
  \quad\text{or}\quad
  Q = [s]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}
    + L^{\mathsf{Orchard}},
\]
the second for a split nullifier, with $s$ computed from the witness. By
the unweakened A1$'$ (or A1), $\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}} \neq \bot$ is, by the
unfolding of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Construction ``Sinsemilla hash on
Pallas'' used in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(i), an
integer combination of values of $\GroupHash$ with coefficients computable
from the witness; so is $Q$. A reduction to the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Assumption ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas'' receives a challenge $Y$ to a
base $G$, programs every value of $\GroupHash$ as $[a]\,G$ for a fresh
uniform scalar $a$, as in the proof of that volume's Lemma ``Relations among
fixed generators'', chooses uniformly one of the at most $q$ queries to the
oracle of personalisation \texttt{Zcash\_ExpandSeed}, and answers it with a
uniform $64$-byte string $u$ conditioned on
$\mathrm{ToBase}(u) = \Extract_{\PP}(Y + [b]\,G)$, for a uniform scalar
$b$. If $\Adv'$ succeeds at that query, then $\Extract_{\PP}(Q) =
\Extract_{\PP}(Y + [b]\,G)$, so $Q = \pm(Y + [b]\,G)$ by the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Lemma ``Coordinate extraction identifies opposite points''; with
$Q = [c]\,G$ for the computed $c$, the reduction outputs $\pm c - b$, the
discrete logarithm of $Y$. The programmed answer differs from a uniform one
in that its reduction is the $x$-coordinate of a point, up to the
statistical distance of $\mathrm{ToBase}$ from uniform, below $2^{-257}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}); success requires the issued $\rho$ to be
such a coordinate in any case, and, given that it is one, the two
distributions of $\rho$ are within negligible statistical distance. The
reduction therefore succeeds with probability at least $\epsilon / q$ less a
negligible term, and $\epsilon$ is negligible.

Notes with distinct $\rho$ are distinct.

(b)~The steps of the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``One
nullifier per note'' apply, with A1$'$ in place of A1 and A5$'$ with
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 0$, which sets $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}} =
\psi^{\mathsf{old}}$ and publishes
$\mathsf{DeriveNullifier}_{\nksym}(\rho^{\mathsf{old}},
\psi^{\mathsf{old}}, \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}})$, in place of A5; A7 is
unchanged. Two real spends, of either protocol, whose consumed notes have
the same extracted commitment consume the same note, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(b), with the same $\nksym$, by the
\emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Binding of $\ivk$ to $(\aksym,
\nksym)$'', and publish the same nullifier. The nullifier-set rule rejects
a repeated nullifier within a transaction and across the chain.

(c)~The value $v' = 0$ is the notation of A4$'$ for
$\mathsf{split\_flag} = 1$. The nullifier is the split nullifier of A5$'$,
and by Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(i), which holds for every
$\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$, including one that the adversary chooses, it is not
the nullifier of the copied note, except with negligible probability. A
split nullifier that equals the nullifier of some other note only prevents
the later spend of that note; it creates no value, and the note is not
thereby consumed in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-accepted}(c).
(ZIP 226, ``Split Notes''; ZIP 227, ``Computation of $\rho$''; protocol
specification, \S``Nullifier Sets''.)
\end{proof}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:state-map] MAX_ISSUE = 2^64 - 1 = 18446744073709551615
%   = 2^l_value - 1, the largest value of one Issue Note, so one note can
%   issue a whole supply
% Cited: MAX_ISSUE := 2^64 - 1: ZIP 227 'Global Issuance State'
\begin{theorem}[Supply integrity]\label{thm:supply}
Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}, for every valid chain, every point $B \neq
V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ and every state of the chain after a transaction,
except with negligible probability,
\[
  \sum_{n} v(n) = \mathsf{issued\_assets}(B).\mathsf{balance},
\]
the sum being over the committed notes $n$ with Asset Base $B$ that no
accepted real spend has consumed, and $v(n)$ being the value of $n$. By
Proposition~\ref{prop:recorded-balance}, the right-hand side is the amount
of $B$ issued less the amount burnt. Consequently the amount of $B$ in
circulation is never negative and is at most $\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE} = 2^{64} -
1$, and it is constant or falling once $B$ is finalised
(Proposition~\ref{prop:final-supply}). The invariant holds per branch,
since the issuance state is a function of the branch
(Remark~\ref{rem:reorg-state}). Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}):
proved here for the specified constructions; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-ks}, which concerns the designed but unspecified
circuit, and on a fixed derivation of $\rcm$ for Issue Notes, any
deterministic one. ZIP 227 argues the property in rationale only.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Induction over the transactions of the chain. Write $U_B$ for the left-hand
side.

\emph{Base.} Before the first transaction of the OrchardZSA activation
block the issuance state is the empty map
(Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}), whose balances read $0$, and every
leaf was appended by an Orchard-protocol Action, so by
Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(iii) no committed note has a Custom Asset Base:
$U_B = 0$.

\emph{Step.} Let $T$ be a transaction of the chain, $B \neq
V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$, and let the claim hold before $T$. The leaves that
$T$ appends open uniquely (Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(i)) to notes distinct
from the openings of all other leaves (Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}(a)),
and each is unconsumed when appended.

\emph{Orchard-protocol Actions of $T$.} They create native notes only
(Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(iii)). A note that such an Action consumes is a
native note; by Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}(i) it is not the opening of a
leaf of Asset Base $B$. They leave $U_B$ unchanged.

\emph{The Action Group of $T$ and its burn set.} Let $R_B$ be its real
spends witnessing $B$, and $A_B$ all its Actions witnessing $B$. A note of
Asset Base $B$ consumed by an Action of $T$ is consumed by an Action
witnessing $B$, by A1$'$. The consumed notes of $R_B$ are committed, by
A3$'$ with C1 and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Membership
soundness'', as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance}; and they were
unconsumed before $T$ and are pairwise distinct, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}(b). Split Actions are not real spends and
remove no note from $U_B$; each contributes $v' = 0$
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-accepted}(c), Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}(c)).
Every Action of $A_B$ creates one note
of Asset Base $B$ whose leaf $T$ appends (Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}).
Hence $U_B$ changes by
\[
  \sum_{i \in A_B} v^{\mathsf{new}}_i - \sum_{i \in R_B} v^{\mathsf{old}}_i
  = -\sum_{i \in A_B} (v'_i - v^{\mathsf{new}}_i) = -v_B,
\]
the first equality because $v'_i = v^{\mathsf{old}}_i$ on $R_B$ and $v'_i =
0$ on $A_B \setminus R_B$, and the second by
Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}(i), whose premise on burn-pair bases rests
on (T4) and Lemma~\ref{lem:state-keys}. Rule (T4) lowers the balance of $B$
by the same $v_B$. No transparent term carries $B$
(Remark~\ref{rem:no-transparent-asset}).

\emph{The Issuance Bundle of $T$.} Each Issue Note of Asset Base $B$, that
is of an Issuance Action whose derived base is $B$ by (I4), enters the tree
after the Action outputs (Definition~\ref{def:tree-order}) with its public
value and unconsumed, and no Action of $T$ consumes it: a consumed note of
$T$ opens a leaf of an earlier block, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}(a) that opening is not the Issue Note. Rule
(I6) raises the balance of $B$ by exactly that value; a reference note adds
$0$ to both sides. Issuance Actions of other Asset Bases write other entries.

Both sides therefore change equally, and the claim holds after $T$. The
bounds follow from $U_B \ge 0$ and
Proposition~\ref{prop:recorded-balance}, and the behaviour after
finalisation from Proposition~\ref{prop:final-supply}.
\end{proof}

The theorem, not the issuance state, excludes counterfeit value. ZIP 227,
``Rationale for Global Issuance State'', keeps the state along the lines of
ZIP 209 (``Specification''), and ZIP 227, ``Global Issuance State'' and
``Management of the Global Issuance State'', maintain it. The state detects
counterfeit value directly only when a burn exceeds the recorded balance,
by rule (T4). A bundle that created unbacked value of $B$ and did not burn
it would pass every check on the map; Theorem~\ref{thm:no-counterfeit}
excludes such a bundle, and Theorem~\ref{thm:supply} inherits the exclusion.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Authority over issuance}\label{sec:security-authority}

\begin{theorem}[Issuance authority]\label{thm:issue-authority}
Let an honest issuer generate $\mathsf{isk}$ as in
Definition~\ref{def:issuance-keys} and Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf}
and publish $\mathsf{issuer} = \mathsf{ik\_encoding}$
(Definition~\ref{def:issuer}), and let the adversary of
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-accepted} obtain the transactions that the issuer
signs. An \emph{Asset of the honest issuer} is an Asset whose Asset
Identifier has that $\mathsf{issuer}$. Under
Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf}, Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover},
Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr} and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Assumptions ``Collision resistance of BLAKE2b'' and ``GroupHash as a random
oracle'', in every valid chain that the adversary outputs, except with
negligible probability:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] every accepted Issuance Action whose Asset Base is that of an
  Asset of the honest issuer, and which raises its balance by rule (I6),
  sets its finalisation bit by rule (I7) or creates its entry with the
  reference note by rule (I5), belongs to a transaction whose Issuance
  Bundle has the effecting data, and whose transaction has the burn set and
  the nullifier $\nfsym_{0,0}$, of a transaction that the honest issuer
  signed;
\item[(ii)] the Issuance Bundle of each signed transaction is accepted at
  most once.
\end{enumerate}
Hence only the holder of $\mathsf{isk}$ issues or finalises the issuer's
Assets (requirement Z4). Status (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}):
proved here for the specified rules; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}, which concerns the designed but
unspecified signed message.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Reduction. Let an accepted Issuance Action change the entry at an Asset
Base $B$ of an Asset of the honest issuer, with Asset Identifier
$(\mathsf{issuer}_h, h)$. Rule (I4) derives $B$ from the bundle's
$\mathsf{issuer}$ and the action's $\mathsf{assetDescHash}$. If
$(\mathsf{issuer}, \mathsf{assetDescHash}) \neq (\mathsf{issuer}_h, h)$,
two distinct Asset Identifiers have the Asset Base $B$, which claim~2 of
Proposition~\ref{prop:assetbase-separation} excludes, under
Assumption~\ref{ass:blake2b512-cr} and the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s
Assumption ``GroupHash as a random oracle'', except with negligible
probability. So the bundle's $\mathsf{issuer}$ is the honest
$\mathsf{ik\_encoding}$. Rules (I2) and (I3) then require a signature valid
under the honest $\mathsf{ik}$ on the transaction's $\mathsf{SigHash}$.

If the honest issuer made no signature on that digest, the reduction, which
answers the honest issuer's signing through the signing oracle of the
EUF-CMA experiment, outputs the digest and the signature of the chain, a
forgery against Assumption~\ref{ass:issueauthsig-euf}. If it made one, on a
transaction $T_h$, then the two transactions have equal $\mathsf{SigHash}$,
and Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}, which for the withdrawn digest rests
on the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Assumption ``Collision resistance of
BLAKE2b'' (Remark~\ref{rem:digest-commitments}), makes their Issuance
Bundles' effecting data equal ($\mathsf{issuer}$ and, per Issuance Action,
$\mathsf{assetDescHash}$, $\mathsf{finalize}$ and every field of every
Issue Note), and their burn sets and nullifiers $\nfsym_{0,0}$ equal. Every
change of the entry, the finalisation bit included, is therefore one that
the issuer authorised; this is~(i).

Part~(ii) is Proposition~\ref{prop:issue-replay}, by rule (I1) and the
nullifier set on $\nfsym_{0,0}$. Burns of the issuer's Assets by note
holders are outside the claim: rule (T4) only lowers the balance (ZIP 227,
``Specification: Consensus Rule Changes'' and ``Issuance Authorization
Signature Scheme'').
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Issuance key compromise]\label{rem:key-compromise}
No rotation of issuance keys exists, and $\mathsf{issuer}$ is the encoding
$\mathsf{ik\_encoding}$ of the key; ZIP 227 notes that this equality
``might not hold in future when key rotation is specified for issuance key
pairs'' (``Issuer Identifier''). The Asset Identifier of an Asset, and hence
its Asset Base, stays bound to the key under which it was issued. On
compromise of $\mathsf{isk}$, ZIP 227 recommends, without a requirement
keyword, setting $\mathsf{finalize} = 1$ for every Asset issued with that
key, changing to a new issuance authorising key, hence a new
$\mathsf{issuer}$, and issuing new Assets, each with a new Asset
Identifier (``Issuance Key Compromise''). By rule (I4), finalisation bounds
only the future issuance of the finalised Asset. It does not undo issuance
by the attacker before the finalising transaction is accepted. An attacker
that holds $\mathsf{isk}$ can issue up to $\mathsf{MAX\_ISSUE}$ less the
recorded balance of each unfinalised Asset, can finalise first, and can
issue Assets under new descriptions, which no finalisation prevents; by
consensus these are indistinguishable from the issuer's own, and ZIP 227
treats their presentation only as a matter of display (``Security and
Privacy Considerations'', ``Displaying Asset Identifier information to
users''). Theorem~\ref{thm:issue-authority} assumes the secrecy of
$\mathsf{isk}$ and says nothing after its compromise. Key rotation has no
design in any text. The specified protocol needs it for no result of this
volume, so its absence is a stated limitation, not an open problem in the
sense of Definition~\ref{def:classes} (ZIP 227, ``Issuance Keys'').
\end{remark}

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Hiding of the Asset type}\label{sec:security-privacy}

The \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Theorem ``Privacy of an Action'' is confined to
Ironwood-pool bundles by precondition (P2) of its Definition ``Public
leakage and privacy preconditions''. It is not cited as proved here; the
definition below has the form of that definition.

\begin{definition}[Public leakage of an OrchardZSA bundle]
\label{def:zsa-leakage}
The \emph{public leakage} of an OrchardZSA bundle is its number of Actions,
its anchor, its flags $\mathsf{enableSpends}$, $\mathsf{enableOutputs}$
and $\mathsf{enableZSA}$, its balancing value, and its burn set, which
publishes each burnt Asset Base and amount. The rest of the transaction is
public as well, including any Issuance Bundle with every field of its Issue
Notes (Remark~\ref{rem:public-issuance}) and the carrier that holds the
bundle. The \emph{public fields} are, per Action, $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}$,
$\nfsym$, $\rk$, $\cmx$, $\mathsf{epk}$, $C_{\mathsf{enc}}$,
$C_{\mathsf{out}}$ and the spend-authorisation signature, and, per bundle,
the proof and the binding signature. The \emph{private inputs} are, per
Action, its witness (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}, with
$\mathsf{AssetBase}$, $\mathsf{is\_native\_asset}$, $\mathsf{split\_flag}$
and $\psi^{\mathsf{nf}}$), the plaintext of its created note
(Definition~\ref{def:zsa-plaintext}), the outgoing viewing key under which
its $C_{\mathsf{out}}$ is formed, or $\bot$, and whether its consumed side is
a real note, a Split Input or a dummy. The \emph{privacy preconditions}
are:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(Q1)] every key involved is generated as in~(P1) of the cited
  definition, except the all-zero spending key of a reference note copied
  as a Split Input, which is public (Definition~\ref{def:reference-note});
\item[(Q2)] honest construction: each created note has a fresh uniform
  $\mathsf{rseed}$, and its $\rcm$ is derived from $\mathsf{rseed}$ by the
  designed $\mathsf{Derive\_rcm}$ under an assigned lead byte
  (Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}); $\rcv$ is uniform; $\alpha$ is drawn by
  $\mathsf{GenRandom}$; each Split Input has a fresh uniform
  $\mathsf{rseed\_nf}$ (Construction~\ref{def:split-nf}); dummy consumed
  notes occur only for ZEC and are formed as in the protocol
  specification, \S``Dummy Notes (Orchard)''; each Split Input copies a
  leaf with the Action's Asset Base whose recipient key is a key of~(Q1)
  held by the sender, or the reference note; consumed notes are padded per
  Asset by the padding rule after Definition~\ref{def:split-input}; each
  real consumed note is a note of a key of~(Q1) that the sender accepted by
  the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s \S``Trial decryption and note acceptance'';
  and the plaintext encoding has a length independent of the Asset;
\item[(Q3)] authentication paths are computed from public data (the
  \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Lemma ``Authentication paths from public data'');
\item[(Q4)] the adversary holds no viewing key of a key of~(Q1).
\end{enumerate}
Within these preconditions the adversary may choose the consumed notes, the
recipients, the Assets and the values, and may know the contents of the
consumed notes (ZIP 226, ``Burn Mechanism'', ``Split Notes'' and
``Backward Compatibility''; protocol specification, \S``Dummy Notes
(Orchard)'').
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Privacy of the Asset type]\label{thm:asset-privacy}
Assume (Q1) to (Q4) of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-leakage}, and let two
sequences of private inputs of one OrchardZSA bundle have equal public
leakage, both satisfy Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action} with the common anchor
and flags, and each balance per Asset against the common burn set and
balancing value. Assume Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-zk} and the \emph{Ironwood
Guide}'s Assumptions ``Pseudorandomness of PRF expansion'', ``Joint hiding
of the viewing keys'', ``Poseidon is a PRF'', ``DDH on Pallas'', ``GroupHash
as a random oracle'', ``The note-encryption hashes as random oracles'',
``Confidentiality and integrity of ChaCha20-Poly1305'', ``The RedPallas hash
as a random oracle'' and ``Discrete logarithms on Pallas''. Then the public
fields of the two bundles are computationally indistinguishable.

Precisely, every efficient adversary has negligible advantage in the
experiment of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Theorem ``Privacy of an Action'',
with the following changes: the leaves that the adversary outputs are those
of the tree of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-accepted}(a); it outputs in addition
the common burn set and the flag $\mathsf{enableZSA}$; and a specification
gives, per Action, its Asset Base, a consumed side that is a dummy (for
$V^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ only), a note of a key of~(Q1) whose extracted
commitment is a leaf, or a Split Input as in~(Q2), and a created side as in
that experiment. Each specification balances per Asset, the consumed notes
of one key have pairwise distinct $\rho$, and the construction of~(Q2)
yields witnesses of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action}.

In particular a bundle reveals, beyond its leakage, neither which Assets its
Actions move, nor how many distinct Assets, nor their values, nor which
consumed sides are real, split or dummies; Remark~\ref{rem:privacy-limits}
states what the leakage itself reveals (requirement Z5). Status
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}): proved here; conditional on
Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-zk}, which concerns the designed but unspecified
circuit, on the unassigned lead byte for the uniformity of $\rcm$, and on
the open plaintext encoding. ZIP 226 argues the property in rationale only
(``Overview'', ``Privacy Implications'').
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For each of the two specifications, the hybrids $H_0$ to $H_{10}$ below
change the probability that the adversary outputs $1$ by a negligible
amount (\emph{Crypto Guide}, \S``The hybrid argument'', Lemma ``Hybrid
lemma''; each hybrid is reached in polynomially many steps, one key or one
Action at a time), and the two hybrids $H_{10}$ are identically distributed.
Every reduction runs the experiment with the adversary and samples every
value that its hop does not concern. The keys involved are those of~(Q1),
the keys of the dummy consumed notes and the keys of dummy created notes.

Hybrid $H_0$ is the real bundle.

Hybrid $H_1$ (\emph{proof}). The aggregate proof is the output of the
simulator of Assumption~\ref{ass:zsa-zk} on the primary inputs, which are
public, $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ included; the witnesses satisfy
Definition~\ref{def:zsa-action} by hypothesis. The cost is the statistical
distance of that assumption. From $H_1$ on, a witness enters the game only
through the public fields computed from it.

Hybrid $H_2$ (\emph{keys}). Each key of~(Q1) and each dummy key is replaced
as in game $G_2$ of the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Theorem
``Privacy of an Action'' (its Lemmas ``Rejection in key generation'' and
``Independence of domain-separated expansions'', and Assumption ``Joint
hiding of the viewing keys''), so that $\ask$, $\nksym$, $\ivk$, $\dk$
and $\ovk$ of each key are independent and $\dk$ and $\ovk$ uniform. The
public all-zero key is left unchanged.

Hybrid $H_3$ (\emph{randomisers}). Each randomiser $\alpha_i$ is replaced by a
uniform scalar, as in game $G_4$ of that proof (\emph{Ironwood Guide},
Lemma ``Distance of GenRandom from uniform'', and Assumption ``The RedPallas
hash as a random oracle''). Then $\rsk_i = \ask + \alpha_i$ is uniform and
independent of $\ask$, the public $\ask$ of the all-zero key included, and
each $\rk_i$ is a uniform point independent of $\aksym$.

Hybrid $H_4$ (\emph{signatures}). Every spend-authorisation signature and the
binding signature are replaced by simulated signatures under $\rk_i$ and
$\mathsf{bvk}$, programming the RedPallas hash, by the \emph{Crypto Guide}'s
Lemma ``Signature simulation'' (\S``Security in the random oracle model and
the forking lemma''). Its simulator applies to RedPallas, a Schnorr
signature whose challenge is the RedPallas hash of the nonce commitment,
the validating key and the message (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Construction
``RedPallas''); the costs are the abort probability of the lemma and the
statistical distance of the reduced hash output from a uniform scalar. No
signing key enters the game from $H_4$ on: neither $\rsk_i$ nor
$\mathsf{bsk}$.

Hybrid $H_5$ (\emph{value commitments}). After $H_4$, each trapdoor $\rcv_i$
enters the game only through $\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$. Each
$\cvsym^{\mathsf{net}}_i$ is replaced by an independent uniform point, by
perfect hiding for every Asset Base (Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-cv}(i)); the key
$\mathsf{bvk}$ is computed from these points, the public burn set and the
balancing value by Definition~\ref{def:zsa-bvk}. The hop is perfect.

Hybrid $H_6$ (\emph{split nullifiers}). Each split nullifier is replaced by the
$x$-coordinate of a uniform point, by Proposition~\ref{prop:split-nf}(ii),
at cost $\epsilon_{\mathsf{expand}} + \epsilon_{\mathsf{ToBase}} + \delta$
each. That proposition holds against an adversary that knows the copied
note and $\nksym$, so the reduction samples every key itself; it holds in
particular for the public key of a reference note, and for a copied note
that the same bundle spends.

Hybrid $H_7$ (\emph{nullifiers of consumed notes}). For each key that consumes a
real or dummy note, $\nksym$ enters the game after $H_6$ only through the
nullifiers of those notes,
$\Extract_{\PP}([\mathsf{PRF}^{\mathsf{nfOrchard}}_{\nksym}(\rho) +
\psi]\,K^{\mathsf{Orchard}} + \cmsym^{\mathsf{old}})$, where
$\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}$ depends on the Asset Base. As in hybrids $H_4$ and
$H_5$ of the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Nullifier
unlinkability'' and in game $G_5$, the PRF is replaced by a random function
(Assumption ``Poseidon is a PRF'') and each multiplier of
$K^{\mathsf{Orchard}}$ by a uniform scalar, so each nullifier is the
$x$-coordinate of a uniform point whatever $\cmsym^{\mathsf{old}}$ is. The
real consumed notes of one key have pairwise distinct $\rho$ by the
definition of the experiment, which in a valid chain holds by
Lemma~\ref{lem:one-consumption}(a), and a dummy consumed note has a key of
its own.

Hybrid $H_8$ (\emph{outgoing ciphertexts}). Each $C_{\mathsf{out}}$ is
replaced by the encryption of a fixed string under an independent uniform
key, as in game $G_3$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Proposition
``Confidentiality of the outgoing ciphertext'').

Hybrid $H_9$ (\emph{ephemeral keys and note ciphertexts}). For each created
note, $\mathsf{epk}$ is replaced by a uniform non-identity point and
$C_{\mathsf{enc}}$ by the encryption of a fixed plaintext of the common
length, keeping every note commitment, by steps~(1) to~(4) of the proof of
the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Confidentiality and key privacy of
note encryption'', as in game $G_6$. The common length is the hypothesis
of~(Q2) on the plaintext encoding.

After $H_9$, the seed $\mathsf{rseed}$ of each created note enters the game
only through $\psi$ and $\rcm$, that is through $\cmsym$. With $\rcm$ a
PRF-expansion output of $\mathsf{rseed}$ under an assigned lead byte, as
for either derivation of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-seed}, the argument of
the proof of the \emph{Ironwood Guide}'s Proposition ``Hiding under the
derived trapdoor'' places $\rcm$ within negligible distance of a uniform
scalar independent of the rest of the game.

Hybrid $H_{10}$ (\emph{note commitments}). Each $\cmx$ is replaced by the
$x$-coordinate of a uniform point, by hiding with one output distribution
for both branches (Proposition~\ref{prop:zsa-cm}(a)), as in game $G_7$.

In $H_{10}$ every public field is a uniform value independent of the
specification, a simulated proof or signature on public values, or a
function of the leakage of Definition~\ref{def:zsa-leakage}: the value
commitments, nullifiers and extracted commitments are uniform, each
$\mathsf{epk}$ is a uniform non-identity point, the ciphertexts encrypt
fixed strings under fresh keys, each $\rk_i$ is uniform, and $\mathsf{bvk}$
is a function of these and of the common burn set and balancing value. The
two hybrids $H_{10}$ are therefore identically distributed, and the
advantage is at most the sum of the costs for both specifications, which is
negligible.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Limits of Asset-type privacy]\label{rem:privacy-limits}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] \emph{Leakage.} Each burn publishes its Asset Base and amount,
  ``the amount and identifier of the Asset being burnt'' (ZIP 226,
  ``Rationale for Value Balance Verification''). The flag $\mathsf{enableZSA} =
  0$ shows that a bundle moves ZEC only (C2). The balancing value is an amount
  of ZEC. Issuance is public (Remark~\ref{rem:public-issuance}), although a
  later spend of an Issue Note is unlinkable to it
  (Proposition~\ref{prop:issue-unlink}), except for a reference note.
\item[(b)] \emph{Action count.} Every Action has one Asset Base (A1$'$,
  A2$'$), consumes at most one real note and creates one note. A bundle
  whose Actions move $k$ Assets, Asset $j$ with $i_j$ real consumed notes
  and $o_j$ created notes, therefore has at least
  $\sum_{j=1}^{k} \max(i_j, o_j) \ge k$ Actions, and the public number of
  Actions bounds $k$. Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy} hides the Assets only
  among bundles with equal Action counts.
\item[(c)] \emph{Order.} ZIP 226 fixes no Action order.
  Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy} holds for every order against an
  adversary bound by~(Q4); it claims nothing against a recipient, which
  by~(e) is outside its scope.
\item[(d)] \emph{Anonymity set.} ZIP 226, ``Privacy Implications'',
  separates Orchard and OrchardZSA note commitments by transaction version,
  version~$5$ for Orchard and version~$6$ for OrchardZSA
  (Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-cm-context}); no current format assigns these
  versions so (Remark~\ref{rem:carrier-status}). The anonymity set of an
  Action is the set of OrchardZSA Actions of whatever carrier is specified,
  and an Orchard-protocol bundle reveals that its notes are ZEC. The pool
  that carries
  OrchardZSA notes is the open problem of Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}.
\item[(e)] \emph{Non-claims.} The theorem claims nothing against a holder
  of a viewing key of a key involved; nothing for the copied note of a
  Split Input beyond the unlinkability of its split nullifier; and nothing
  about timing, network metadata or information outside the transaction.
\end{enumerate}
(ZIP 226, ``Privacy Implications'', ``Rationale for Value Balance
Verification'' and ``Asset Base Equality''.)
\end{remark}

\section{Classification of the components}\label{sec:status}

This section collects the classes that the preceding sections assign. It
tabulates the class of every construction of the volume under
Definition~\ref{def:classes} and the unspecified objects on which every
result is conditional under Definition~\ref{def:result-status}
(\S\ref{sec:status-classes}), and it enumerates the open problems, each
with the texts that leave it open and the obligation that closes it
(\S\ref{sec:status-open}). No class is derived here; each is the one stated
where the construction or result is made.

\subsection{Classes of the constructions and results}
\label{sec:status-classes}

\begin{remark}[Reading of the table]
Each row of the first part of Table~\ref{tab:classification} is a
construction, or a named part of one, with the subsection that states it;
the rows are grouped by their class under Definition~\ref{def:classes}.
``Specified'' means specified by Draft text: no ZIP of OrchardZSA is Final
and no network upgrade includes one (item~(1) of ``Open problems'',
\S\ref{sec:status-open}). The second part lists every theorem, proposition,
lemma and corollary of the volume. A result carries no class
(Definition~\ref{def:result-status}); its row states only the unspecified
objects that its hypotheses concern, and the rows are grouped by those
objects. The standing assumptions of ``Accepted transactions and
assumptions'' (\S\ref{sec:security-model}) on specified primitives, namely
the group hash, discrete logarithms on Pallas, BLAKE2b, RedPallas,
$\mathsf{IssueAuthSig}$, note encryption, Poseidon as a PRF, PRF expansion
and the joint hiding of the viewing keys, are not repeated per row.
\end{remark}

The reading of condition A8 has a group of its own. ZIP 226 leaves
conditions A8 and A9 unchanged by its delta, so A8 reads $v^{\mathsf{old}}$
and A9 reads $v^{\mathsf{new}}$, and a Split Action that copies a note of
non-zero value needs $\mathsf{enableSpends} = 1$; this is specified by the
delta. The confirmation of that reading in the circuit document outside the ZIPs
is designed but unspecified (Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-action-status}).

The groups of the second part read as follows. A fixed derivation of $\rcm$
for Issue Notes is needed only so that the validator's commitment is
computable; any deterministic derivation from the published fields
suffices (Definition~\ref{def:result-status}), and the corollary and the
theorem that use Lemma~\ref{lem:provenance} carry that qualification only
through it. Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy} needs the lead byte for $\rcm$
to be uniform as a pseudorandom output, and the plaintext encoding to have
a length independent of the Asset; both are open, items~(2) and~(8). No
result is conditional on the carrier otherwise than through
Assumption~\ref{ass:sighash-cover}.

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Open problems}\label{sec:status-open}

Each item below is an open problem in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:classes}: no text supplies a design, every existing
design conflicts with live text, or live texts contradict one another. Each
item names the texts that leave it open and the obligation that closes it.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Network upgrade.} ZIP 226, ``Deployment'', names NU7; ZIP 227,
  ``Deployment'', reads ``TBD''. ZIP 259 (Draft), which specifies NU7,
  lists its consensus changes without either ZIP (``NU7 consensus
  changes'') and introduces no transaction format (``Rationale for no new
  transaction format''); ZIP 258, which specifies NU6.3, mentions neither.
  Consequently the first block of OrchardZSA, whose input issuance state is
  the empty map (Definition~\ref{def:issued-assets}), is not assigned.
  Closed by a deployment ZIP that includes ZIPs 226 and 227.
\item \emph{Value of the lead byte of OrchardZSA notes and Issue Notes.}
  ZIP 226, ``Note Structure and Commitment'', fixes only the placeholder
  $\{\{\mathsf{ZSALEADBYTE}\}\}$; ZIP 2005, ``Usage with other proposals
  requiring note plaintext format changes'', requires a new lead byte for
  plaintext changes; and the removed design's $\mathtt{0x03}$ is the
  Ironwood lead byte in live text (protocol specification, \S``Note
  Plaintexts and Memo Fields''; Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}).
  Consequently the commitment of an Issue Note is not computable from live
  text (Remark~\ref{rem:issue-cm-open}). Closed by an assignment of the
  value and an extension of $\mathsf{Derive\_rcm}$ to it.
\item \emph{Live carrier.} ZIP 226, ``OrchardZSA Transaction Structure'',
  and ZIP 227 defer the encodings to ZIP 230, and ZIP 226, ``Sighash
  modifications relative to ZIP 244'', and ZIP 227, ``Modifications
  relative to ZIP 244'', defer the signature message to ZIP 246; both are
  Withdrawn. Version~$6$ is the format of ZIP 229, which carries no ZSA
  bundle, and ZIP 248 lists OrchardZSA and ZSA Issuance only as potential
  bundle types without an allocated type (Remarks~\ref{rem:carrier-status}
  and~\ref{rem:sighash-status}). The flag $\mathsf{enableZSA}$ has no bit
  and no instance position in any current format: the withdrawn design
  places it at bit~$2$ of $\mathsf{flagsOrchard}$, which the consensus
  rules of ZIP 229 reserve and require to be $0$, the format table of that
  ZIP naming the bit $\mathsf{enableCrossAddress}$. Closed by a live format
  that encodes the OrchardZSA bundle, the Issuance Bundle and
  $\mathsf{enableZSA}$, with a signature digest that meets
  Remark~\ref{rem:digest-commitments}.
\item \emph{Carrying pool.} ZIP 226, ``Backward Compatibility'', places
  OrchardZSA notes in the Orchard pool, which ZIP 258, ``Consensus rules
  from NU6.3 activation'', closes to new value and to cross-address
  transfers from NU6.3 (Remark~\ref{rem:pool-premise}). The rules of that
  pool then forbid transfers of Custom Assets between distinct addresses,
  leave issuance into it unaddressed, and leave undetermined the tree that
  Definition~\ref{def:tree-order} extends. Closed by a reconciliation of
  ZIP 226 with ZIP 258 that names the pool and its rules.
\item \emph{Composition of the delta with condition A10.} ZIP 226,
  ``Circuit Statement'', states its delta against A1 to A9; the current
  statement adds condition A10 and the primary input
  $\mathsf{disableCrossAddress}$ (\emph{Ironwood Guide}, Definition
  ``Orchard Action statement''; Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-action-status}).
  Closed by a statement text that includes both, which depends on the
  resolution of item~(4).
\item \emph{Empty Issuance Action for a new Asset.} For an Issuance Action
  without Issue Notes on an Asset Base without a reference note, rule (I5)
  refers to a first Issue Note that does not exist, so its validity,
  finalising or not, is undetermined (ZIP 227, ``Specification: Consensus
  Rule Changes''; Remark~\ref{rem:empty-action}).
  Lemma~\ref{lem:first-issuance} is conditional on this case. Closed by a
  rule that rejects such an action or fixes its effect on the issuance
  state.
\item \emph{Issue Note whose commitment is $\bot$.} The protocol
  specification, \S``Sending Notes (Orchard)'', makes a sent note whose
  commitment is $\bot$ invalid and resamples $\mathsf{rseed}$, which
  covers sent notes only; ZIP 227, ``Issue Note'', states no rule (Remark
  ``Commitment $\bot$ for an Issue Note'' in \S\ref{sec:issue-note}). The
  event has negligible probability. Closed by a rule: resampling by the
  issuer or rejection by validators.
\item \emph{Encoding of the OrchardZSA note plaintext.} The field set is
  specified (Definition~\ref{def:zsa-plaintext}); ZIP 226, ``Note Structure
  and Commitment'', delegates the encoding to ZIP 230, which is Withdrawn.
  Theorem~\ref{thm:asset-privacy} is conditional on it. Closed by a live
  encoding whose length does not depend on the Asset.
\end{enumerate}

% compute/OUTPUT.md, bounds.py:
% [sec:burn-balance] Actions per transaction <= 2000000 / 32 = 62500
%   < 2^16 = 65536 (each Action carries its 32-byte nullifier)
% Cited: block size <= 2000000 bytes: spec 'Block Header Encoding and
%   Consensus'
\begin{remark}[Items not open]
Four items are not open problems. The bound on the number of Actions that
Lemma~\ref{lem:zsa-integer} needs, at most $62\,500 < 2^{16}$ Actions per
transaction, follows from the block-size rule (protocol specification,
\S``Block Header Encoding and Consensus'') whatever the carrier. The ZSA
fee terms (Remark~\ref{rem:fees-status}), the derivation of $\rcm$
(Remark~\ref{rem:leadbyte-open}) and the OrchardZSA proof length
(Remark~\ref{rem:zsa-proof-status}) are designed but unspecified, as the
first part of Table~\ref{tab:classification} records: each has a design
that no live text contradicts. ZIP 227 and ZIP 317 disagree about the fee
terms, which the protocol does not need (Remark~\ref{rem:fees-status}).
\end{remark}
